# §15 Phase 15: One boot flow — aarch64 to the first machine's level

Part of the split `docs/notes/` evidence library — routing for every `§N.M` pointer is in
[index.md](index.md). Section numbers are unchanged from the original `NOTES.md`.

## Phase 15: One boot flow — aarch64 to the first machine's level

**Status: 15.1–15.6 landed, all four of 15.6's pins green. 15.1, 15.3, 15.4 and 15.5 and the init
seam unified - all four check families run on both machines, the reporting/exit-hook/net/cmdline/log-sink
copies are one each, and 15.2's six device classes have all landed (the input registry §13.24, the
host-driven console §13.25, the display adapter + framebuffer console §13.26, the sound card §13.27,
the vsock device §13.28, and the host share §13.29). 15.6, the initramfs flow, is landed:
`boot-aarch64` full chain + `INIT_EXITED`, `test --arch aarch64` `pass=27 fail=0`,
`cargo xtask test` `pass=158 fail=0 (158 built)` exit 33, `alpine-aarch64` reaching
`mpos login:` in 16 s, and `depgraph` within the ratchet. `### 15.6` below records what it cost.
The last named difference this machine carried from the first - `init_net` +
`virtio::spawn_service` behind `mpos.selftest` - is also closed, measured the same day.
Measured 2026-10-07.** *(This header is that day's snapshot; the phase has since landed through
§15.36. Two of its words are retired facts, not current ones: `boot-aarch64`/`alpine-aarch64`
are commands §15.7 deleted — the same work is `run`/`rootfs`/`test --arch aarch64` — and the
`mpos.selftest` boot word is gone with §15.19's test-build change.)*

Phase 13 closed the *ABI* gap (13.3–13.16) and §13.19/§13.20 closed two of its consequences. What
is left is the *boot*: the second machine reaches the same userspace surface as the first, but it
gets there along its own path, and the parts of that path which are not about a bus, a UART or a
page-table format are written twice - once in `src/board/x86_64.rs`, once in `src/board/aarch64.rs`.
The goal of this phase is that the machine-independent half of the boot has **one** copy, so that
"which machine is this?" is answered by the machine layer and by nothing else.

### What is measured today

| What | x86_64 | aarch64 | Where it is answered |
|---|---|---|---|
| `check_mm` (allocator, frames, buddy merge, slab classes, heap/frame cross-check) | `board/checks/mm.rs` | **same** | machine-independent: `mm` is one module |
| `check_sched` + its five scenarios and helpers | `board/checks/sched.rs` | **same** | machine-independent: `sched` is one module |
| `check_vfs` (dispatch, ramfs, a mounted tree crossing, two tasks on one fd) | `board/checks/vfs.rs` | **same** | machine-independent |
| `check_ipc` (pipe, tty line discipline, signals) | `board/checks/ipc.rs` | **same** | machine-independent |
| `banner`, `park`, `check_boot_stack`, `report_stack_use`, `panic_context` | `board/report.rs` | **same** | machine-independent: `BootInfo`/`hal`/`sched`, with the stack pointer read through `hal::Cpu::stack_pointer` and the text range from `arch::kernel_sections` (§13.23, 15.4) |
| the `init` callback's signature | `fn(&BootInfo) -> !` | `fn(*const u8, usize) -> !` (the archive is stashed *before* the address-space switch) | a fork in `mpos_arch::arch::boot`'s own seam |
| the root switch | **userland**: the initramfs's `/init` mounts `root=` and `switch_root`s | **kernel**: `check_disk_root` mounts the disk at `/mnt`, `switch_root` pivots, `/init` runs from the disk | the largest duplication: the first machine's flow is a script the second machine has no copy of |
| `M0`–`M8` milestone checks | the boot's verdict | a different set, and they run on **every** boot (in front of a distribution's `init`) | §13.14's rule applies: a check that changes shared state damages the machine it checks |
| capabilities (`.cargo/config.toml`) | `mpos_msi`, `mpos_serial_ring`, `mpos_fpu_trap`, `mpos_signal_frame`, `mpos_framebuffer_console`, `mpos_net`, `mpos_qemu_debug_exit`, `mpos_pci_config`, `mpos_input_registry` | `mpos_console_input`, `mpos_serial_ring`, `mpos_net`, `mpos_signal_frame`, `mpos_input_registry`, `mpos_framebuffer_console` | the seam works; four of the first machine's nine are missing here (`mpos_msi`, `mpos_fpu_trap`, `mpos_qemu_debug_exit`, `mpos_pci_config`), and `mpos_input_registry` + `mpos_framebuffer_console` moved across in §13.24/§13.26 |
| device discovery | PCI enumeration | device tree + virtio-MMIO windows | genuinely the machine's; both answer `hal::map_mmio_window` |

Of the four missing capabilities, three are hardware absence on this port and one is port work:
`mpos_msi` and `mpos_fpu_trap` have no counterpart here (a `virt` machine with a GIC and no `#NM` -
both honest "absent", not stubs), and `mpos_qemu_debug_exit` is an emulator exit port AAVMF does not
answer. `mpos_pci_config` is *not*: the bus is there, but it is not the bus this port drives its
devices over - the device tree is. The three that *have* crossed over are the ones whose device was
already reachable over that tree: `mpos_input_registry` (§13.24), the console (§13.25) and
`mpos_framebuffer_console` (§13.26, on the `virtio-gpu` card the same MMIO transports carry) - each
by handing the same device to the shared driver instead of opening it, reading its identity and
dropping it.

### The target shape

1. **One home for the neutral checks.** `src/board/checks/{mm,sched,vfs,ipc,report}.rs`, under the
   wiring crate for the reasons `board/steps.rs` already records (the depgraph lets the wiring name
   the machine and both libraries; `boot` would close a mutual pair with `vfs`). A body keeps a
   private `devices()` and a private `arch_checks()`; everything else is called from one place.
2. **One `init` seam.** `mpos_arch::arch::boot`'s `init` callback becomes `fn(&BootInfo) -> !` for
   both machines. This is what makes "the same sequence, both machines" expressible at all: the
   aarch64 fork exists only because it stashes the archive before the switch, and *that* is a
   consequence of (3).
3. **One root-switch flow: the second machine gets an initramfs `/init`, like the first.** The
   kernel unpacks the archive and hands the machine to `/init` on both machines; the *script* mounts
   `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, `root=` and pivots. That is Linux's own model, it is what
   `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §4 and `AGENTS.md` §3's kernel-space discipline require ("a kernel that
   assembles them first
   leaves pieces the owner of the system cannot unmount"), and it deletes `check_disk_root`'s mount
   half and `switch_root` outright. The alternative - moving the pivot into the kernel for *both*
   machines - is the same line count with more risk: it re-implements the script's `root=`/
   `rootfstype=`/`rootflags=` loop in Rust, breaks `cargo xtask run`'s "no `root=` → drop to a shell
   in the initramfs" behaviour, and contradicts §4. Cost of the chosen direction: one
   `initramfs-aarch64/` tree plus its toml, a busybox in it (`mount`, `pivot_root`), and `M7_ROOT_OK`
   moving from a kernel marker to a script-printed one - which moves `xtask/src/aarch64.rs`'s
   `TERMINATORS` in the same commit (AGENTS §5's trap: a wrong terminator kills the boot seconds in,
   indistinguishable from a hang).
4. **Nothing machine-independent stays in a body.** After this, `board/<arch>.rs` holds: the entry's
   three callbacks, `devices()`, the machine's own checks (the paging/IDT proof, the UART's TX/RX
   proof, the MMIO transport probes), and the ordering its own milestones need - and every one of
   those is machine work by definition.

### Stages

Each is one commit and green alone (AGENTS §3 rule 7). Only stage 0 is measurement.

| Stage | Contents | Pin | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| 15.0 | the inventory above becomes a §13.16-adjacent measurement, with the numbers `grep`-able | — | none |
| 15.1 | extract `check_mm`, `check_sched` (+ helpers), `check_vfs`, `check_ipc` into `board/checks/**`, x86_64 calling them at the same points | `cargo xtask test` prints the same `pass=157 fail=0 (157 built)` + exit 33; `depgraph` unchanged (the wiring is exempt); **both** bare-metal targets build (the modules are cfg-free, so aarch64 compiles them while not yet calling them) | **landed (§13.21)** — identical x86_64 numbers before and after the move |
| 15.2 | aarch64 device parity, one class per commit: the input registry (`mpos_input_registry` + `input::close_at_exit`), virtio-console, virtio-gpu + framebuffer console (`mpos_framebuffer_console`), virtio-sound, vsock, 9p | per class: `boot-aarch64` with the new `M*_OK` **and** its failure marker added to `TERMINATORS`; `test --arch aarch64` stays green; `machine.toml` gains the `-device` | **all six classes landed: the input-registry (§13.24), console (§13.25), virtio-gpu + framebuffer-console (§13.26), virtio-sound (§13.27), vsock (§13.28) and 9p (§13.29)** (§13.4's rule: measure the flip before designing the seam). The input registry's flip was re-measured for this cut and costs **1 error** - `E0432: unresolved import super::hal::transport_all` at `drivers/virtio/input.rs:40`, where the item is gated in `drivers/virtio/hal.rs` - against the **4** recorded here before, because three of those were in `src/vfs/sysfs.rs`'s `build_input` and §13.23's capability flip made them resolve. The design cost the number did not show was real and is now paid: `EvdevNode` was monomorphic over `VirtIOInput<HalImpl, PciTransport>` through `read_caps`, `service`'s harvest and `init`'s enumeration, so the class became a transport-neutral contract (`drivers::virtio::InputDevice`, with the crate-backed `VirtioInputDev<T>` as the one implementation of its six methods), the MMIO side opens its own window and hands the live device to `input::register`, and the board carries `check_input` + `check_input_registry`. The marker moved with the pin: `M8_INPUT_OK` is printed after `check_dev`, where the registry can be read back through a mounted `/dev` - `input::nodes()` non-empty *and* `/dev/input/event0` resolving at evdev's `13:64`, because event flow needs a keypress and the harness is headless (the `/sys/class/input` half is the same capability's, and appears when sysfs is mounted). The console class (§13.25) then cost the same **1** error (`E0432: unresolved import super::hal::transport`) for the same reason and paid the same kind of design debt: a `ConsolePort` contract, one `VirtioHvc<T>` over the crate's driver, `mmio::console_device` opening the window, the devfs `#[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]` on `/dev/hvc0` deleted, and the marker resolved through the mounted `/dev`. The virtio-gpu class (§13.26) was measured the same way and cost the same **3** errors - but they hid a *type-erasure* refactor rather than a one-helper seam, which is the one place this phase's "measure the flip first" rule under-reported: the crate's `Transport` is not object-safe, so `Gpu` became `Gpu<T: Transport>` and the render task had to hold a `Box<dyn GpuDevice>` (a 16-method trait) to keep one copy of it. The rest of the stack needed nothing (one `pci::` reference in 7.6 kLoC). The sound class (§13.27) then cost the same **1** error and was the console class's shape exactly - a three-method `SoundCard` contract, one `VirtioSnd<T>` over the crate's driver, `mmio::sound_device` opening the window, the devfs gate deleted, and a marker that resolves the node and moves a silent burst through the data path (the OSS *parameter* face is `abitest`'s `t-dsp`, which this machine's sweep does not carry - a named gap). The vsock class (§13.28) was the console class's shape again - the same **1** error (`E0432: unresolved import super::hal::transport`) and the same one-contract/one-generic-driver design (`VirtioVsock<T: Transport>`, `mmio::vsock_device`, the module's `#[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]` deleted, `spawn_service`'s vsock half moved to the contract) - but it is the one class whose device this host cannot always provide: QEMU does not emulate vsock in userspace, so `vhost-vsock-device` needs `/dev/vhost-vsock`, and the aarch64 boot attaches it only when the host has one, requiring `M8_VSOCK_OK` only then and printing the skip otherwise (never silently, and with a one-line summary that then claims seven devices rather than eight). The 9p class (§13.29) cost the same **1** error and turned up two defects the machine had carried all along: the PCI probe (`fs9p::init_global`) had **no caller at all**, so `devices()` was always empty and `/mnt/share` was never mounted on *either* machine, and the client spoke plain `9P2000`, which QEMU 11 answers `"unknown"` to (measured: `.u` and `.L` are what it serves). The client now speaks `9P2000.u`, `mount_share` moved out of the first machine's body into the shared steps, and the class is pinned twice - the aarch64 boot's `M8_P9_OK` (the mounted share serves a 39-byte file back) and `abitest`'s `t-9p` on the first machine (the boot's mount, a program's own `mount -t 9p` over the dispatch, an unexported tag's `ENOENT`) - with `parse_stat` newly driven by a `fuzz/` target. A **third** defect on the same path was found later, by a measurement that happened to *write* through the share: `Tcreate`'s body stopped at `mode` and omitted the dialect's trailing `extension[s]`, which the *server* unmarshals - so a guest creating a file through the share wedged with `Failed to decode VirtFS request type 114` and no answer, while every read worked and `t-9p`, whose subject was the read half, stayed green. Fixed (the field is sent, and the encoded bytes are pinned by a host test, shown red against the old body); with it the host's complaint moves to **126**, so `Twstat` carries a second undecodable body and is the next item - the guest test that found both is *not* landed with the fix, because what it pins is a hang |
| 15.3 | aarch64 adopts the shared checks, in an order its own markers permit | `boot-aarch64` full chain + `INIT_EXITED`; `test --arch aarch64` green; `alpine-aarch64` still reaches `localhost login:` | **landed** (§13.21 + §13.22): all four families run on both machines, and adopting them found three defects the machine had carried since its scheduler landed - the clock never preempted, the EL0 interrupt stub never saved the interrupted program's resume state, and the EL1 one never saved the kernel's (the abort that held `checks::vfs`/`checks::ipc` out until it was fixed). They run **under `mpos.selftest` on both machines** (§13.14's rule: the Alpine boot must not pay for or be damaged by them) |
| 15.4 | shared reporting: `banner` on both, `check_boot_stack` on both (measure the loader's canary first), `park` instead of `halt`, a real `panic_context` | `boot-aarch64`, both sweeps, host tests | **landed** (§13.23): `src/board/report.rs`, one copy for both bodies, with `hal::Cpu::stack_pointer` as the one machine question and the boot-stack canary moved into `crates/bootinfo` so the loader and both kernels read one definition |
| 15.5 | one exit-hook registry instead of two bodies (`register_exit_hooks`: five hooks + two cloexec on x86_64, one on aarch64) | `t-tcp`/`t-tcp-listen` (they measured the socket hook's absence as `EADDRINUSE`), both sweeps | **landed** (§13.23): `steps::register_exit_hooks`, one list in the one order, the two input hooks under `mpos_input_registry` - which also became the gate for the input modules and their `/dev` block, replacing four `target_arch` gates |
| 15.6 | the initramfs flow: `initramfs-aarch64/` + toml, `alpine-aarch64` stages it, the script pivots, `switch_root`/`check_disk_root`'s mount half deleted, the `init` seam unified to `fn(&BootInfo) -> !` | `alpine-aarch64` → `localhost login:`; `boot-aarch64`; both sweeps; `depgraph` | **landed, all four pins green.** `boot-aarch64` full chain + `INIT_EXITED`; `test --arch aarch64` `pass=27 fail=0`; `cargo xtask test` `pass=158 fail=0 (158 built)` exit 33; `alpine-aarch64` reaches `mpos login:` in 16 s; `depgraph` within the ratchet. Cost seven defects, all recorded below - and the risk was real: §13.20's bug lived on this exact path, and six more had hidden behind it |
| 15.7 | **the other half of "one flow": the archive *build* is one pipeline too.** `xtask/src/aarch64_initramfs.rs` (249) was the second machine's copy of `xtask/src/initramfs.rs` (156) - same staging tree, same apk, same farm, same ustar, same staleness question, written twice. It is one module now, parameterised by the machine: the toml, the overlay, the staging/archive names, the package source, the applet farm, and whether a stamp records the tree's mtime. The `aarch64-initramfs` subcommand is retired for `initramfs --arch aarch64` - which needed `main.rs`'s `--arch aarch64` refusal to stop covering `Initramfs`, because it never was the x86_64 disk's: it is a directory of files, and both machines have one. `--arch` then decides the *root requirement* too: the first machine's farm is `chroot … busybox --install -s` (root), the second's is a hand-laid symlink list under `apk --usermode` (not root), which is why the retired command needed no root either. **What is deliberately *not* unified** is the two `/init` scripts and the two tomls: the script differences (the `R:` selftest read, `MPOS_SWITCH_ROOT_OK`, and the `exec` target - the second machine's disk carries its own pid-1 chain) are the *disk root's* difference rather than duplication, and the toml pair is the same convention `rootfs.toml`/`rootfs-aarch64.toml` already follow | `cargo xtask initramfs --arch aarch64`'s archive is member-for-member identical to the retired command's (138 members, `/init` md5 `66e653b6…`, all eight farm symlinks and modes equal; the one mode delta, `var/empty` 555→755, is `--usermode`, i.e. *who ran the build*, not the pipeline - the pre-change archive was laid as root); `cargo xtask boot-aarch64` `EXIT=0` with the whole chain through `MPOS_INITTAB_OK bytes=1957` and `INIT_EXITED`; `cargo xtask test` `pass=159 fail=0 (159 built)`; `cargo test -p mpos-xtask` 69 passed; `depgraph` within the ratchet; `cargo fmt --check` clean | **low** - the pin is byte-identity, and it was measured before and after |
| 15.8 | **the sweep's machine-independent half is one copy.** `xtask/src/sweep.rs` holds what `selftest::run_tests` and `aarch64::run_sweep` had written twice: the `tests.toml` load and the undefined-group refusal, the staged/skipped selection, the `"user tests: {} staged, {} left out by tests.toml"` line and the per-group list under it, and the `=== RUN/PASS/FAIL/SUMMARY/END` parser. Each arm keeps what only it can answer - which target to build, how the programs travel (initramfs+FAT vs ESP+ext4), which firmware, when the run is over - **and its own verdict wording**, because the claims differ: the first quotes the debug exit (`status == Some(33)`), the second has no exit to read and asserts an exact count instead. The parser takes the stricter of the two rules each arm carried (the *last* `=== SUMMARY`, and a failed program's name as the first word after `=== FAIL `, since the runner appends `exit=N`). **One rule that looked shared is not**, and it was measured rather than argued: "every name in `tests.toml` must be answerable by a program this arm built" has two scopes, because the arms do not build the same file - x86_64 builds both suites (169 programs) and can require it of the whole file, while aarch64 builds only the std suite (30) so `t-sh`, `t-dns`, `t-rawicmp`, `t-tcp-close`, `t-fill-ramfs`, `t-forkcost`, `t-netlat` and `t-pipecost` legitimately have no program there. With the check shared, the aarch64 sweep refused on exactly those eight names; each arm keeps its own question and its own wording now | both sweeps' reportable lines are **byte-identical** to the pre-refactor runs (`user tests: 169 programs built` / `159 staged, 10 left out` with the three group lines, `pass=159 fail=0`, `self-test: all 159 …` on x86_64; `30 aarch64 programs built` / `28 staged, 2 left out`, `pass=28 fail=0` on the second machine), `EXIT=0` on both; `cargo test -p mpos-xtask` 75 passed (5 of them this module's); `cargo xtask test --arch aarch64 --groups slow` still refused by name with the same message; `depgraph` within the ratchet; `fmt --check` clean | **medium** - the only hard evidence is that the output did not move, so it was captured before and diffed after |
| 15.9 | **the two machines' QEMU wiring is in one file and reads one `machine.toml`.** `aarch64_qemu` (190 hardcoded lines, `-m 256`, `vsock_available()`, `-display none`) and `find_ovmf`/`firmware` were the last of the second machine's boot written twice. Now: `machine.toml` carries the second machine's own values under an `aarch64_` prefix (`xtask/src/config.rs::take_for` - the plain key is the first machine's, the prefixed one wins for the second, and a key with no override stays shared), `xtask/src/qemu.rs` holds both machines' command lines (`machine_args`, `aarch64_machine_args`) with one firmware lookup (`find_firmware`), and one capture loop (`capture`) replaces the two polls. **What stayed per-machine is the device list**: every aarch64 device is a `virtio-*-device` MMIO form because that is how that kernel finds anything at all, so it is a machine fact rather than a `machine.toml` key, and `aarch64_extra_args = []` is written explicitly because the key falls back to the shared `["-vga","none"]`, an x86-only option a `-machine virt` boot refuses. **And the loop's marker poll had never worked**: it read the log with `read_to_string`, the second machine's log carries openrc's escapes and one byte sequence that is not valid UTF-8, so the read failed and every `boot-aarch64` waited out its whole 60 s deadline before reading a marker that had been in the file for ten seconds. `log_contains` searches bytes; the same boot is now 7 s | `boot-aarch64` `EXIT=0` in **7 s** (was ~60 s), the whole chain through `INIT_EXITED`, no deadline line; `cargo xtask test` `pass=159 fail=0 (159 built)` exit 33; `alpine-aarch64` `EXIT=0` in 14 s, `mpos login:` with no `[ !! ]`; `cargo test -p mpos-xtask` 81 passed (10 qemu, incl. the second machine's line, its MMIO device set, the host-conditional vsock, the byte-wise marker search and the three capture stop reasons); `depgraph` within the ratchet; `fmt --check` clean | **medium** - the value is one wiring surface, and the measurement is the marker poll finally firing |
| 15.10 | **S4's premise was measured before a seam was designed, and the measurement says the seam is already there.** Ungating the four x86-only virtio modules (`blk`, `net`, `rng`, `msix`) for aarch64 costs **9 errors**, and every one of them is the *PCI helpers* (`crate::drivers::pci`, `hal::pci_root`, `hal::transport`, `hal::init_message_signalled`) - not a hidden second bring-up. The bring-up itself is already one copy per class, because Phase 15 landed it that way: each class's *install* is shared (`console::install`, `sound::install_probed`, `input::register`, `net::vsock::init`, `fs9p::install`, `VirtioGpuDev::install`, `steps::bring_up_net`) and only the *transport open* is the machine's - x86_64 enumerates PCI, aarch64 walks its device tree - which is what §15's target shape says it should be. So there is no second bring-up to unify, and forcing one would mean a `dyn` over `virtio_drivers::Transport`, which is not object-safe. **What the comparison did find is a defect**, and it is exactly this class: the disk's registration was written twice, and the second copy drifted - `board/aarch64.rs` registered the boot disk as `add_disk("vda", 254, "/dev/vda", dev)` while the first machine wrote `/devices/virtio/block`, so the second machine filed its disk at a tree position no device has and every event about it announced `DEVPATH=/dev/vda/vda`. The fix is one call (`drivers::virtio::register_block_disk`) with the facts in one place (`uevent::VIRTIO_BLK_DEVPATH` beside `VIRTIO_BLK_MAJOR`), so the second copy cannot come back | `cargo check` clean on both bare-metal targets; `boot-aarch64` `EXIT=0` with the whole chain through `INIT_EXITED`; `test --arch aarch64` `pass=28 fail=0`; `cargo xtask test` `pass=159 fail=0 (159 built)`; host tests **514** (one new: the virtio disk is numbered and filed where its driver says); `depgraph` within the ratchet - the edge `drivers->blk`/`drivers->uevent` already existed per-module, so no new edge and no ARCHITECTURE.md change | **low** - and the stage's own answer is that the rest of it is not work |
| 15.11 | **the ABI suite starts moving to the std side, so the second machine can run it.** The gap TEST-AND-BOOT-REFACTOR.md §1 measured is "139 programs only one architecture can run": `user/abitest` is `no_std` raw-syscall, built for `x86_64-unknown-none` with the kernel's own `nr.rs`, while `user/tests` is `std` + `rustix` and builds for both machines. The migration is mechanical per program (`#![no_std]`+`lin::raw_sys` -> `fn main()` + `rustix::`, `lin::check_true`/`done` -> `Report::check`/`finish`) and it moves a `[[bin]]` block from `user/abitest/Cargo.toml` to the std crate's flat `src/bin`. **Every batch so far, every program self-contained** - batch 1 `t-getuid`, `t-getpid`, `t-getrandom` (no filesystem, device or net at all); batch 2 `t-mkdirat` (its scratch directory is under `/tmp`, which is what the second machine's payload carries) and `t-fionbio` (its fixture is a `pipe`, and `FIONBIO` is served unconditionally on both machines - checked before the move rather than after); batch 3 `t-read` and `t-write`, whose fixture **had** to move: the no-std versions read `/etc/motd`, and the second machine's sweep disk has no `/etc/motd` at all (measured with `debugfs`: `/etc` carries `inittab`, `doas.conf`, `resolv.conf`, `local.d`, `network`) - so `t-read` writes its own file under `/tmp`, which changes the *fixture* and not the claim. Their `PROT_NONE` page is mapped at an address the kernel chooses rather than the no-std suite's fixed `0x5000_0000`: the claim is that the page cannot be read, not where the machine's address space happens to have room. `t-write`'s `EBADF` probe uses descriptor 999 rather than `-1`, because `rustix`'s `BorrowedFd::borrow_raw` refuses a negative raw fd by contract; the kernel's answer is the same branch. **Batch 4 (`t-dup`, `t-dup2`) is where the migration's rule had to change, and the measurement is the reason**: `rustix::io::dup2` is not a binding of `dup2` - its own source substitutes `dup3(fd, new, DupFlags::empty())` under `cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")`, because its `new` is `&mut OwnedFd` and `oldfd == newfd` is therefore unreachable through it. That case is the middle of `t-dup2`, so the wrapper would pin a different call on one machine than the other. `user/tests/Cargo.toml`'s rule now names this shape (a wrapper that is not the syscall the ABI promises, or that discards its result - `rustix::io::close` returns nothing, so "close answers 0" needs `libc::close`) beside the original one (a call rustix does not bind at all). And `t-dup2` pins a *different level* on each machine, which is honest rather than a defect: arm64 has no `dup2` syscall (`nr_aarch64.rs`: `DUP2 = 64006; // no arm64 number; unreachable on aarch64`), so on the second machine musl implements it over `dup3` and the program pins the answer a caller gets, while on the first it reaches the syscall itself. What had to exist before either: `user/tests/src/lib.rs`'s `Report`, the crate's own verdict helper (every one of the thirty wrote its own `struct Report`, which is also how `t-process-vm-libc` came to end on "all passed" where its neighbours end on `PASS`). **Batch 5 (`t-access`, `t-openat`, `t-dup3`) measured the fixture again and found the rule's third shape.** The first two had to move their "existing file" off `/etc/motd` for the reason batch 3 found, and `t-openat`'s one path it does *not* create itself - `/bin/sh` - was measured on the second machine's disk before the move (a 479176-byte static ELF, ELF magic), because "the image starts with ELF magic" is a claim about a real program rather than a fixture the test just made. `t-access` writes its own file after removing any leftover, so the mode its X_OK claim rests on is `0666 & !umask` rather than whatever the image happened to ship. `t-dup3` is where `user/tests/Cargo.toml`'s `libc` rule gained its third clause, and again the measurement is the reason: `rustix::io::dup3` *binds* the syscall but its shape cannot express the claims - it returns `()` rather than the new number and takes its target as a non-aliasing `&mut OwnedFd` (so `oldfd == newfd`, the one case dup3 differs from dup2 on, is unreachable through it) - so `libc::dup3` carries those two, and the third claim (a flag word with bits beyond `O_CLOEXEC`) rides along. The flags are *not* a reason, and finding that out took a measurement: `DupFlags` is a bitflags type carrying bitflags' unnamed flag (`const _ = !0`), so `from_bits_retain(!0)` holds an invalid word and rustix *can* express that claim. Unlike `t-dup2`, it pins the *same* syscall on both machines: `DUP3` has a real number on each (`24` / `292`) and both reach `handle_dup3`. **Batch 6 (`t-getcwd`, `t-chdir`) is the third clause a second time, and the fixture moved once more.** `rustix::process::getcwd` binds the call but not the contract: it takes a `Vec<u8>` and returns a `CString`, growing the vector on ERANGE until the path fits, so the *size the caller chose* (and its EINVAL for a buffer under two bytes and its ERANGE answer) and the *address the syscall returns* are both outside it - `libc::getcwd` has the shape the ABI promises, and both machines reach the same `handle_getcwd`. `chdir`/`fchdir` do come from `rustix`. `t-chdir`'s fixture had to move for batch 3's reason: the no-std version chdir'd to `/etc` and opened `motd` relatively, so it now owns `/tmp/t-chdir_file` and chdirs to `/tmp`, whose `..` is `/`, before the relative step into `/bin` - a directory both payloads carry. **Batch 7 (`t-faccessat`, `t-faccessat2`, `t-openat2`) measures the rule a second and a third time, and the numbers a raw call needs turn out not to be in `libc` at all.** `t-faccessat` is the three-way agreement `apk`'s bug was about, and the third spelling has to be the third syscall: `rustix::fs::accessat` *is* `faccessat` (its linux_raw backend issues `__NR_faccessat` when the flag word is empty) and reaches `faccessat2` only when a caller passes a flag, so the program calls `libc::syscall(libc::SYS_faccessat2)` for the no-flag form. Its privilege drop is `rustix::thread::set_thread_uid` rather than a `libc` call - the wrapper's own documentation says it is the kernel's per-thread `setuid` and not POSIX's, which is precisely the call a single-threaded program wants and precisely `__NR_setuid`. `t-faccessat2` makes the flag word its whole subject, so all twelve of its claims are the raw call; and `AT_EACCESS` turned out to have *no* `libc` binding on this target (`libc` 0.2.189 defines it for fuchsia, the BSDs, Hurd and AIX and in no `linux_like` file - measured), so its flag numbers come from `rustix::fs::AtFlags` while the call stays raw. `t-openat2` is the rule's third clause a third time: `rustix::fs::openat2` writes `size_of::<open_how>()` itself, so the size word - 23 `EINVAL`, 25 `E2BIG`, 24 opens, which *is* the program's subject - is unreachable through it, so the whole program is `libc::syscall(libc::SYS_openat2)` with a local `#[repr(C)]` `OpenHow` while every flag and mode word still comes from `rustix`'s tables. **The resolve bit is not a second reason, and that correction is the one batch 8 turns on**: `ResolveFlags` carries bitflags' unnamed flag (`const _ = !0`) exactly as `DupFlags` does, so an unnamed bit *is* expressible through `rustix`'s `from_bits_retain`. A rustix *plain enum* (`SeekFrom`, `Advice`) has no such escape, so an out-of-vocabulary *word* genuinely has nowhere to go - and that, rather than a bit, is what forces `libc`. Its readable file moved off `/etc/motd` for batch 3's reason. `chmod` has no arm64 number either (`nr_aarch64.rs`: `CHMOD = 64020; // no arm64 number`), and `rustix::fs::chmod` is written as `fchmodat(AT_FDCWD, ...)` on every machine, so batch 7's `0000` step is one call on both. **Batch 8 (`t-lseek`, `t-fadvise`, `t-fallocate`) is the first batch written under the corrected rule, and the correction pays off in one of the three.** `t-fallocate` is *pure `rustix`*: its unnamed mode bit travels as `FallocateFlags::from_bits_retain(0x8000)`, bitflags' own escape for "a word from a kernel newer than the flag list", where the pre-correction rule would have sent the whole program to `libc` for a reason that was not true. `t-lseek` and `t-fadvise` do need `libc`, and for exactly the shape the correction tells apart from a bit: `SeekFrom` and `Advice` are plain *enums*, so an out-of-vocabulary word - whence 5, advice 6, advice 99 - has nowhere to go in them at all. Both reach for the named wrapper rather than a raw number, `libc::lseek` and `libc::posix_fadvise`, and the second has a shape worth naming: POSIX's wrapper *returns the error number*, so those two claims compare against `libc::EINVAL` directly while the `rustix` ones compare against `Err(Errno::INVAL)`. `t-lseek`'s fixture moved off `/etc/motd` for batch 3's reason, and the size its claims are stated against is the one it drains out of the file rather than a constant it also wrote down. `t-fadvise` needs no fixture at all: its regular file is a `memfd` and its pipe comes from `pipe_with`. `t-fallocate` also had to drop the no-std `lin::Stat`, which is the x86_64 `struct stat`, so the size is read through `rustix::fs::fstat`, whose `Stat` is the machine's own. All three calls carry real numbers on both machines (`LSEEK` 62/8, `FADVISE64` 223/221, `FALLOCATE` 47/285) and both machines reach the same handlers. **Batch 9 (`t-link`, `t-readlink`, `t-unlinkat`) is a pure-`rustix` batch, and one claim turned out not to need the fixture everyone assumed it would.** All three calls are served on both machines (`LINKAT` 37/265, `READLINKAT` 78/267, `UNLINKAT` 35/263) - and two *spellings* are not: `READLINK` and `SYMLINK` have no arm64 number (`READLINK = 64015; // no arm64 number`, `SYMLINK = 64016`), and `rustix::fs::readlink`/`symlink` are written as `readlinkat`/`symlinkat(AT_FDCWD, ...)` on *every* machine, so the migrated program pins one handler on both where the no-std version reached `readlink`/`symlink` themselves on the first. `t-readlink` is the batch's instructive one: `rustix::fs::readlink` is clause (c) - a `Vec` in and a `CString` out, growing until the whole target fits, which hides exactly the truncation and zero-size EINVAL the program is about - but beside it `readlinkat_raw` takes *the caller's* buffer and returns how many bytes were initialised, so the rule is satisfied with no `libc` at all. And its `/proc/self/exe` claims, which looked like they would need a procfs the second machine's payload does not mount, need no mount: the handler matches that literal path string and answers with the path the task was execve'd from, which both machines confirm by printing the same three `ok:` lines. `t-link` drops the no-std `lin::Stat` for `rustix::fs::statat` and reads S_IFLNK through `FileType::from_raw_mode`; `t-unlinkat`'s unknown-flag claim goes through `AtFlags::from_bits_retain(0x1000)` - the bit-versus-enum distinction the rule correction bought, costing no `libc` where the old wording would have called for one. **Batch 10 (`t-umask`, `t-priority`, `t-prlimit64`) is the batch that found the two kernel bugs, and neither was a missing call.** `t-umask` is pure `rustix`, and it is the third time the bit-versus-enum distinction pays: `rustix::process::umask` takes and returns a `Mode`, and `Mode::from` masks with the low twelve bits on *this* side - so the claim that the *kernel* keeps only twelve needs the word to arrive whole, which is `Mode::from_bits_retain(0o17777)` (a bitflags type carries bitflags' unnamed flag; an enum would have nowhere to put it). `t-priority` needed `libc::syscall` for two separate reasons: the `20-nice` word is clause (c) because `rustix::process::getpriority_process` has already converted it (`Ok(20 - ret_c_int(..))` in its own backend, and the named `libc::getpriority` converts the same way), and `sched_setattr`/`sched_getattr` are clause (a) - `rustix` binds neither - so the 48-byte `struct sched_attr` goes through the raw call too, with the numbers from a table that has `GETPRIORITY`/`SETPRIORITY` *swapped* between the machines (140/141 and 141/140), which is what `libc::SYS_*` is for. `t-prlimit64` is clause (b) and (c) at once: `rustix::process::getrlimit` returns an `Rlimit` with **no `Result`**, so the success four of the seven claims read is discarded - and for exactly the reason the bug survived, since a closed `Resource` enum cannot be `99`. **What the two bugs were**: the resource table put `RLIMIT_NOFILE` at row 5, and `setpriority` answered `EINVAL` for a nice outside `-20..=19` where Linux clamps. Both are §15.13 and §15.14; both were invisible because the one program touching each had the wrong number written into it (`lin.rs`'s `RLIMIT_NOFILE = 5`, and `EINVAL` asserted as the contract), and the whole point of the migration is that a claim's number and its claim now have different sources. The clamp also had a second pin: `t-posix` (`user/abitest/`) asserted the same `EINVAL`, so it moved in this commit too. **Batch 11 (`t-fcntl`, `t-fd-list`, `t-filelock`) is the descriptor trio, and all three of the rule's clauses show up in one batch.** `t-fcntl` is clause (c) twice, and *not* for the word a reader would reach for first: `rustix` makes `fcntl` type-safe by splitting it into one function per *command*, so the command number `999` the kernel has no meaning for has no spelling in it, and the descriptor `-1` is the same clause one step down (its `AsFd` cannot name one) - while the word with bits beyond `FD_CLOEXEC` needs no `libc` at all, because `FdFlags` is a bitflags type and `from_bits_retain` hands the kernel the whole word. `t-fd-list` is the batch's *binding* choice: `rustix::fs::RawDir` - `getdents64` with **the caller's** buffer, the call the no-std version made by hand - rather than its high-level sibling `Dir::read_from`, which `fcntl(F_GETFL)`s the descriptor and then `openat(fd, ".")`s one of its own, two calls this program's subject never makes. `t-filelock` is all three clauses in one program: `flock` and `F_SETLK` stay on `rustix` (`flock`, `fcntl_lock`; `FlockOperation` is a plain enum, but every operation this program asks for is one of its named variants, so the missing escape hatch for an out-of-vocabulary word costs nothing here), while `F_DUPFD` is clause (a) for a measured reason (its only plain `fcntl_dupfd` is `#[cfg(target_os = "espidf")]`; the Linux spelling is `fcntl_dupfd_cloexec`), `F_GETLK` is clause (a) again (`fcntl_lock` is `F_SETLK`/`F_SETLKW` only - there is no query), and `close` is clause (b) a second time after `t-dup2`. Its `struct flock` is `libc::flock` rather than the no-std version's hand-rolled 32 bytes, and all twelve of its claims were run against real Linux *in the shape the program makes them*, because a pin's assertions are the first thing that can be wrong: `0, EWOULDBLOCK, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, F_UNLCK, 0`. The batch also **measured the harness and had to correct itself**: `t-fd-list` mounts a procfs of its own when the payload has none, and *neither* sweep takes that branch, because the aarch64 payload does have `/proc` - its disk chain (`rootfs-aarch64/init`) mounts one before the runner takes over, which is why `aarch64_init.rs`'s "this payload reaches neither /proc, /sys nor the network" is corrected in this commit. And it is the second batch in a row to find a kernel bug by reading a claim's answer against Linux: §15.15. Two further divergences its pins surfaced are named, not fixed, in §15.16. **Batch 12 (`t-fstat`, `t-newfstatat`) is the pair the board had flagged as still open, and it measures the harness from both ends.** Both carried the x86_64 `struct stat` in `lin::Stat`, so both claims became the *answers* through `rustix`'s machine-owned `Stat` - `fstat`/`statat`, `st_size`/`st_ino`/`st_nlink`, `FileType::from_raw_mode` - which is the pattern batches 8-10 used. The *length* stays `t-stat-buffer`'s subject (§13.10) and the comments say where it went, because a wrapper that owns its buffer cannot express "and it wrote exactly this far". Both fixtures moved off `/etc/motd` for batch 3's reason (`/tmp/t-fstat_file`, `/tmp/t-newfstatat_file`), while `/bin` stays: measured, the second machine's staging tree has a flat `/bin` of six regular files and no subdirectory, which is what its `nlink` 2 rests on, and `/bin/sh` there is a 479176-byte static ELF. `t-newfstatat` is pure `rustix`, `AT_EMPTY_PATH` and all - its claim is that two spellings of one description agree - and `t-fstat` needs the raw call for exactly one claim, which is a *new* measurement of what the raw door is for: the null-buffer `EFAULT` claim is outside `rustix` (it owns the buffer) **and** outside musl's `fstat`, which faults in userspace where the raw call answers `EFAULT` (NULL and `0x1` alike, SIGSEGV, measured in child processes so one crash could not hide the next). Run against the host first, as always: `t-newfstatat` 14/14, and `t-fstat` 12/13 - the difference being this host's `/bin`, which is `/usr/bin` behind a symlink, so its `nlink` is larger; that claim is about the payloads' flat `/bin` and not about any Linux. One of the batch's findings is a divergence of this kernel's, §15.17 **Batch 13 (`t-set-robust-list`, `t-set-tid-address`, `t-writev`, `t-kill`, `t-sched-getaffinity`, `t-uname`, `t-rseq`, `t-enosys`, `t-brk`, `t-nanosleep`, `t-clock-gettime`, `t-rt-sigprocmask`, `t-sigaltstack`) is the self-contained thirteen, and it measures the raw door's *answer shape* for the first time**: musl's `syscall` door returns `-1` and leaves the refusal in `errno`, so every raw claim compares through `std::io::Error::last_os_error()` (batch 12's `t-fstat` already did this) rather than against the negated constant the no-std harness could. Three programs turned out to need a *new* kind of honesty, named per machine: on real Linux `rseq(32)` answers EINVAL where mpos answers ENOSYS (measured with both a C probe and this crate's own binary), `sigaltstack` treats a null base as SS_DISABLE and answers 0 where mpos refuses ENOMEM, and `clock_nanosleep(TIMER_ABSTIME)` sleeps to the deadline where mpos refuses EINVAL - so those three claims accept their host's answer under `MPOS_ON_HOST` and pin mpos's refusal on the payload sweep, each with a printed note; `t-enosys`'s seven probes are served calls on a current host and take the same branch. Two more measurements came out of the fixture rule biting the runtime: `t-brk` can no longer grow *the program break* under musl - that is Rust's heap - so it parks its own span above the heap with a private map and states every claim against the break the kernel actually gave; `t-sigaltstack` starts inside a process whose sigaltstack state std's guard-stack setup may have touched, so it pins the lifecycle from a known point (disable, then install, read back, disable) and hands the original state back. `t-rt-sigprocmask`'s unreadable-set probe moved off the fixed `0x5000_0000` because under a dynamic musl that address names nobody's mapping, and is now a page mapped PROT_NONE and unmapped after. The libc/libc-table absences this batch measured: `libc::set_robust_list` does not exist on linux-musl (only glibc/uclibc declare it); `RSEQ_SIG` has no libc binding on this target (musl's own header spells `(0xAB << 24) | 0`); rustix binds neither `sched_getaffinity` nor `clock_nanosleep`'s four-word shape, and its `Signal`/`ClockId` enums cannot name 0/65/999 or clock 10/999 - clause (c) again. **Batch 14 (`t-mmap`, `t-munmap`, `t-mprotect`, `t-madvise`, `t-mremap`, `t-mmap-demand`, `t-mmap-reserve-commit`, `t-segv`, `t-arch-prctl`) is the mmap family, and it moved the fixed-address rule from a comment into a fixture**: the no-std versions mapped at `0x5000_*` because their static image could not be elsewhere; under a dynamic musl those addresses may belong to any mapping, so each program first reserves its own PROT_NONE arena wherever the kernel puts it and runs every MAP_FIXED / hint / NOREPLACE / mremap claim inside that owned span. Five claims turned out to differ between Linux and mpos *by design* and are named per machine rather than hidden (each accepts the host answer under `MPOS_ON_HOST` with its reason in the code): an unaligned `mprotect` inside nobody's region is silently skipped by Linux where mpos answers EINVAL; a grow-in-place `mremap` without MAYMOVE is ENOMEM on Linux (it only grows over already-mapped pages) where mpos keeps the mapping; `MAP_FIXED|MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE` is EPERM-conflict/EEXIST-ordered on Linux before mpos's pair-refusal EINVAL; advice 5 is still Linux's MADV_REMOVE slot where mpos retired it; and Linux's mincore reads a faulted page's neighbourhood resident (readahead) where mpos faults exactly one. Two more measurements of the raw door: musl's `syscall` wrapper reads the stack canary through `fs:[0x28]` on entry, so t-arch-prctl runs every SET/read-back pair in a forked child (the parent's own TLS cannot survive a foreign FS base), and the same call has no arm64 number at all - the program builds on both machines, says "skipped" on the second, and joins tests.toml's `aarch64` group, which gains its first name. `t-segv` keeps libc::fork/wait4 (std names neither the death-by-signal nor the status word). The family's bindings: rustix binds neither mincore nor madvise nor mremap (clause (a)), and `GetRandomFlags`/`ProtFlags` spellings ride rustix's own enums where they carry the claim. **Batch 15 (the tmpfile/pipe thirteen: `t-copy-file-range`, `t-pipe2`, `t-readv`, `t-renameat2`, `t-eventfd`, `t-splice`, `t-preadv2`, `t-timerfd`, `t-xattr`, `t-fchmodat2`, `t-truncate`, `t-sync`, `t-inotify`, plus the device-shaped `t-dsp`) is where the migration met Linux's *ordering*, not just its answers.** The raw door's split-offset ABI came first: `preadv2`/`pwritev2` take their position as two signed halves (glibc's own convention, measured here), and the test's single-`i64` spelling read back EBADF on a live fd until the helper split it like the ABI does. Six claims turned out to differ between Linux and mpos by design and are named per machine rather than hidden: splice's unknown-flag-vs-zero-length ordering (Linux returns early at len 0; mpos validates flags first), splice's pipe-offset refusal arriving as EBADF/ESPIPE through Linux's fd check where mpos names EINVAL, `fchmodat2` on a symlink (EPERM on Linux, mpos's documented EOPNOTSUPP), ftruncate on a pipe (EINVAL there, EOPNOTSUPP here - the divergence t-truncate has always named, **closed 2026-10-16**: both of a pipe's ends answer Linux's own words now, `docs/uapi/` §5.1 item 22, and `t-truncate` has no per-machine branch left), xattr on a tmpfs that *does* store attributes (every EOPNOTSUPP claim takes the host branch), and inotify's whole attribution model: Linux reports CLOSE_WRITE/DELETE_SELF/IGNORED only on the file's own watch, queues the unlink's DELETE bare on the parent, retires silently, interleaves OPEN/MODIFY/CLOSE_NOWAIT across watches, and delivers its queue a buffer at a time - so the life-sequence claims accept Linux's five-step subsequence under the host branch while the payload sweep pins mpos's merged six-record 123456. Two fixture rules from earlier batches paid off again: every `/etc/motd` reader now owns its `/tmp` file (batch 3), and `t-xattr`'s unreadable-value probe maps its own PROT_NONE page instead of naming the no-std suite's never-mapped `GUARD_PAGE` (batch 14's arena rule). Numbers measured against libc's tables where they exist (`SYS_pipe2` 293/59, `SYS_renameat2` 316/276, `SYS_copy_file_range` 326/287, `SYS_splice` 275/76) - and twice they did not: linux-musl aarch64 declares no `SYS_fchmodat2` at all, so that program carries the number from this kernel's uapi table (452 on both machines, which is Linux's), and rustix binds none of inotify/syncfs/timerfd-create's refused words, so those probes ride `libc::syscall`. `t-dsp` keeps the OSS `_IOC` words as soundcard.h computes them (neither library names them) and forgives a host with no card as the honest absence it is. |`t-dsp` keeps the OSS `_IOC` words as soundcard.h computes them (neither library names them) and forgives a host with no card as the honest absence it is. **Batch 16 (`t-stat-consistency`, `t-statfs`, `t-statx`, `t-mount`, `t-chroot`, `t-ns-uts`, `t-9p`) is the stat/mount/namespace seven, and its raw door is about *buffer ownership* three times over.** `rustix::fs::{statfs, statx}` own their buffers (`MaybeUninit`, never seeded), so the two claims that read the kernel's *write coverage* - statfs's poisoned-0xff struct coming back whole, statx's pattern-seeded 256-byte struct - have no spelling through any wrapper and ride `libc::syscall(SYS_statfs/SYS_statx)` into a caller-owned struct; the same door carries statx's out-of-vocabulary flag word (0x10000 EINVAL), because rustix's backend *masks the mask off* before sending it ("users passing flags unknown to rustix could end up writing outside the buffer", says its source) - a wrapper that edits the argument cannot pin what the kernel does with it. One measurement corrected this batch's own first draft: real Linux leaves `stx_mnt_id` at 144 nonzero under a BASIC_STATS request (host, kernel 7.2.9: the only seed survivor past 144 is exactly that word), where mpos zeroes the whole 256 - so the tail claim is named per machine rather than relaxed. The statx struct's *length* is pinned against the ABI's 256 (`STATX_SIZE`), not `size_of::<libc::statx>()` (320 on this glibc host): the declared type's tail is nobody's promise. Three programs met the host's *privilege* rather than its semantics: mount(2)'s bind answers EPERM for an unprivileged user (measured, errno 1), chroot(2) the same, and an unprivileged user namespace refuses unshare by policy - so those sections print a skip verdict under `MPOS_ON_HOST` instead of reporting the host's capabilities as this kernel's answer; the payload always runs as root. t-ns-uts keeps the no-std program's whole observation shape - forked child writes its three doors down a pipe, parent compares bytes - and learned twice the hard way that a forked std child must neither flush nor keep: musl's `exit_group` would run the inherited atexit list (the child leaves through raw `SYS_exit`), and a *leaked-but-open* pipe fd is as wrong as a dropped one (the parent's drain waits on every writer; measured EOF-with-nothing-on-it). Its capability gate still lowers only the effective id, which needs raw `SYS_setresuid` because libc's typed `setresuid` takes `uid_t` and cannot name `-1`. t-stat-consistency drops `lin::Stat` for rustix's machine-native pair (`stat`/`fstat`) and keeps both lock paths including niri's `/run/user/0` (its mkdir chain is best-effort, the open is the check). t-9p's subject exists only inside the sweep boot - no host has a virtio-9p export tagged `hostshare` - so it skips on the host with its reason printed and pins all four claims on the payload. x86_64 `user tests: 169 programs built` / `159 staged, 10 left out` / `pass=159 fail=0`; aarch64 `61 aarch64 programs built` (was 59) / `59 staged, 2 left out` / `pass=59 fail=0`, with `=== RUN t-getpid` / `t-mkdirat` / `t-fionbio` / `t-access` / `t-openat` / `t-dup3` / `t-getcwd` / `t-chdir` / `t-faccessat` / `t-faccessat2` / `t-openat2` / `t-lseek` / `t-fadvise` / `t-fallocate` / `t-link` / `t-readlink` / `t-unlinkat` / `t-umask` / `t-priority` / `t-prlimit64` / `t-fcntl` / `t-fd-list` / `t-filelock` / `t-fstat` / `t-newfstatat` and their `=== PASS` lines in its log; **every migrated program's per-claim `ok:` lines are byte-identical on both machines** - `t-getuid` 4 lines, `t-getpid` 5, `t-getrandom` 7, `t-mkdirat` 5, `t-fionbio` 9, `t-read` 6, `t-write` 5, `t-dup` 10, `t-dup2` 8, `t-access` 6, `t-openat` 16, `t-dup3` 9, `t-getcwd` 11, `t-chdir` 17, `t-faccessat` 15, `t-faccessat2` 12, `t-openat2` 16, `t-lseek` 12, `t-fadvise` 9, `t-fallocate` 20, `t-link` 25, `t-readlink` 6, `t-unlinkat` 14, `t-umask` 7, `t-priority` 19, `t-prlimit64` 7, `t-fcntl` 10, `t-fd-list` 7, `t-filelock` 14, `t-fstat` 13, `t-newfstatat` 14, **Batch 17 (`t-epoll`, `t-poll`, `t-unix`) is the multiplexer trio, and it is the first batch whose raw door was chosen by *disassembling the maintained table* rather than by reading its API.** `epoll_create` is the measurement that forced it: the crate's own static `libc.a`, disassembled on both machines, spells it `size <= 0 ? -EINVAL : epoll_create1(0)` - musl's own check, then a tail call into `epoll_create1` - so the wrapper never reaches `__NR_epoll_create` at all. On x86_64 the claim therefore rides the raw `SYS_epoll_create` (213), which is the kernel's positive-size rule the no-std version pinned; on arm64 that call has no number (`EPOLL_CREATE = 64028; // no arm64 number; unreachable on aarch64`), so musl's own check is the only door a caller has - and both answer EINVAL, which is why the claim and its `ok:` line are the same on both machines. The wait and the poll are one step down the same shape: `EPOLL_WAIT`/`POLL` have no arm64 number (`64029`/`64003`) where `EPOLL_PWAIT = 22` and `PPOLL = 73` do, so `t-epoll`'s wait is `SYS_epoll_wait` here and `SYS_epoll_pwait` with a null mask there, and `t-poll`'s millisecond word is `SYS_poll` here and `SYS_ppoll` (a timespec, `-1` spelled as a null pointer) there - each number checked against the kernel's own `nr_aarch64.rs` and libc's table before it was written. The rest of the trio's raw door is clause (c) and the *caller's* buffer five times: `PollFd::new` zero-fills the slot (the sentinel seed that catches a kernel scribbling on `revents` has no spelling through `rustix`), `BorrowedFd::borrow_raw` refuses `-1` (so musl's resolver-shaped array - placeholders parked at `-1` beside the live socket - cannot exist inside a rustix array), `rustix::event::epoll` splits `epoll_ctl` into `add`/`modify`/`delete` (op `7` and a null event pointer have nowhere to go), the wait derives `maxevents` from the buffer's length ("maxevents 0 while handing over eight records" is inexpressible), and `getsockname`'s echoed `addrlen` is hidden behind `SocketAddrAny`'s parsed variants, where the number *is* the claim. `t-unix` keeps `rustix` for the other calls (`socket`, `bind`, `connect`, `listen`, `accept_with`, `shutdown`, `SocketAddrUnix::new_abstract_name` - so the abstract-namespace and empty-path probes reach the kernel *as* addresses), takes the raw door for three (a foreign family word under a unix body, the addrlen echo, and the empty path, which `SocketAddrUnix::new("")` builds and then refuses to send), and makes the listener nonblocking through `rustix`'s own `fcntl_getfl`/`fcntl_setfl` pair (the shape `t-pipe2` and `t-tcp-listen` use) beside the connector's `SOCK_NONBLOCK` type word - a first draft reached for `libc::fcntl` on a claim about `rustix` that reading its source refuted. One fixture changed for a measured reason: the no-std version re-bound a socket that had *already* been bound, and Linux refuses exactly that (EINVAL whether or not the name is free, measured on the host), so the re-bind is made from a socket that has never been bound - the claim unix(7) states rather than the one the old test happened to make. Three per-machine answers the no-std version had already named are re-measured and kept, each with its host branch beside its payload answer: an unregistered target answers ENOENT here and EBADF on a host (errno 9 - Linux refuses an unbound number at the door before the interest list is consulted), `getpeername` on a connected unix socket answers the anonymous triple here and the listener's bound path on a host (measured: `/tmp/t-unix.sock`, length 19) because this kernel keeps no peer name on a connect, and a connect to a bound-but-unlistened name is `ECONNREFUSED` here where Linux rendezvouses with the backlog-0 queue (the divergence the notes' socket section already carries). x86_64 `user tests: 169 programs built` / `159 staged, 10 left out` / `pass=159 fail=0` (exit 0); aarch64 `107 aarch64 programs built` (was 104) / `104 staged, 3 left out` / `pass=104 fail=0` (exit 0), with `=== RUN t-epoll` / `t-poll` / `t-unix` and their `=== PASS` lines in its log; the three programs' per-claim `ok:` lines are **byte-identical on both machines** - `t-epoll` 37 lines, `t-poll` 20, `t-unix` 58, **Batch 18 (`t-fork`, `t-exit-group`, `t-wait4`, `t-waitid`) is the process/reap four, and it moved the layout question from this repository into the machine.** `fork` is unbound in `rustix` on both machines, so creation is `libc::fork` (arm64 reaches `clone`; `nr_aarch64.rs`'s `FORK = 64007; // no arm64 number`), and every child leaves through the raw `SYS_exit_group` - the subject of `t-exit-group`, and the one door that cannot flush a forked std child's inherited copy of the parent's buffered stdout. That musl's `_exit` *is* that call was measured from the crate's own static `libc.a` (`__syscall(SYS_exit_group, status)`, then a `SYS_exit` loop for the return that cannot happen), which makes the raw spelling a choice rather than a shortcut. Reaping is `rustix::process::waitpid` - `wait4` with a null `rusage`, the call the no-std versions made by hand - and its `WaitStatus` is where `WIFEXITED`/`WEXITSTATUS` went (`exited()`, `exit_status()`, `as_raw()`); `ECHILD` becomes `Err(Errno::CHILD)` and `WNOHANG`'s "0, not an error" becomes `Ok(None)`, a *different variant*, which is the same claim at full strength. `t-fork`'s `static mut SHARED` became an `AtomicI32` (the 2024 edition refuses to reference a `static mut`) and states copy-on-write just as plainly. **`t-waitid` is where the raw door was kept and the *offsets* handed over.** The record the kernel writes *is* this program's claim and `rustix::process::waitid` owns its buffer, so the call is `libc::syscall(SYS_waitid, …)` (247 here, 95 on the second machine) into the caller's own record; the no-std version read `si_pid` at byte 16 and `si_status` at 24 through an offset written here, and the batch measured what those bytes are before replacing them - dumping a real record out of this host's kernel (musl, `waitid(P_PID, child, WEXITED)`) shows `si_signo` 17 at byte 0, `si_code` 1 at byte 8, the child's pid at 16, its uid at 20, the code at 24: asm-generic's `{signo, errno, code, union}` with the union pushed to 16 by its own 8-byte alignment, which is the shape of *both* machines here (x86_64 includes asm-generic's header, arm64 is asm-generic). The offsets were therefore right on both and are now unnecessary on both: the fields are read through the machine's own `libc::siginfo_t` (`si_signo`/`si_code` as fields, `si_pid()`/`si_status()` as libc's accessors) with the record's length pinned against the ABI's `SI_MAX_SIZE` (128) rather than a number written here. **Two fixtures moved and the claims did not**: `t-wait4`'s "still running" child was `/bin/sleep 2`, and the second machine's `/bin` has no `sleep` at all (measured - its farm is `initramfs.rs`'s fixed `AARCH64_APPLETS`, which names `true`/`false` for `t-stdspawn`'s sake and nothing else this test needs), so the sleeper became the test's own forked child, asleep 500 ms and leaving through `SYS_exit_group`; and `t-wait4`'s `execve` is `libc`'s, because the crate's one `execve` sits in `rustix::runtime`, a doc-hidden experimental module outside the enabled feature set - clause (a), recorded in the file. **Three divergences `t-waitid`'s pins surfaced here were named rather than fixed, and all three were closed on 2026-10-16** (docs/uapi/ §5.2 carried them until then; each was measured on the host before its claim was written): the `id` argument is ignored, so `P_PID` hands back whichever child finishes first where `wait4` *does* honour its pid; `P_PGID` (2) and `P_PIDFD` (3) are answered `EINVAL` where Linux *serves* `P_PGID` (`idtype = 2`, `id = 0` is accepted and a childless caller gets `ECHILD`, errno 10); and a call asking for no event at all is answered `0` with the record cleared, where Linux clears the record and then refuses with `EINVAL` (errno 22) - the mild-direction §3.2 shape, a success where Linux refuses. None was fixed then: the first two needed a *feature* (waiting on a process group, and using the id at all), the third one condition in `handle_waitid`, and that handler's file was held by another line of work in the tree. **Closed 2026-10-16, and the fix is Linux's answer in each half.** The door resolves `P_ALL`/`P_PID`/`P_PGID` into the same `WaitFor` selector `wait4`'s named child has always used - a group through its leader's number, resolved the way `setpgid`/`getpgid` resolve one, with `0` the caller's own group (measured: a negative id is `EINVAL`, a group with no child of ours is `ECHILD`) - and `P_PIDFD` stays `EINVAL` because there is no pidfd family here for a descriptor to name a task through (`.workbuddy/probe_waitid.c` measured the host answering `EBADF` for a descriptor that is not a pidfd; `docs/uapi/` §5.3 names the prerequisite). The options word is Linux's whole: the three event classes are *served* rather than faked - `WSTOPPED` and `WCONTINUED` used to be a zero-filled 0, the one answer a job-control parent cannot tell from "nothing yet" - `WNOWAIT` reports without collecting, and a call that names no class, or carries a bit Linux's own mask does not define, is `EINVAL` **with the record cleared first**, which is Linux's *order* as measured rather than read (all three refusals leave a zeroed record; the first draft of the fix cleared after the refusal and the probe said otherwise). The record's `CLD_*` vocabulary came with it, each code measured on the host before it was written: `CLD_STOPPED` for a group stop, `CLD_TRAPPED` for the same stop under a tracer (which is why `WaitEvent::Stopped` carries `traced` - the event word cannot tell the two apart), `CLD_CONTINUED` with `SIGCONT` for a resume. Two things the fix names instead of guessing: `__WCLONE` *narrows* to children cloned with an exit signal other than `SIGCHLD`, and this kernel's tasks carry no exit signal at all (`wait4` has ignored the bit since it landed); and `si_uid` in the record is left zero, because the record outlives the task's slot and does not carry the uid - both now in `docs/uapi/` §5.2 beside the measurement, and the second **closed 2026-10-16**: `Retired` carries the uid now and the live path asks the child's identity, so `t-waitid` reads the child's real uid off an exit, a stop and a resume (`docs/uapi/` §5.1 item 23), leaving `__WCLONE` as this row's one named remainder. That closing run's gates: x86_64 `176 staged, 11 left out` / `pass=176 fail=0` + `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, aarch64 `175 staged, 12 left out` / `pass=175 fail=0` + the same 32 cases, host unit tests 545, xtask 121, `fmt --check` clean, clippy 0 error on the four targets, `depgraph` within the ratchet - and `t-waitid` is 56 claims in a sweep (49 in a host run, where the privilege-dependent section is named rather than made) with its per-claim `ok:` lines byte-identical on the two machines. `t-waitid`'s claims moved with it and are 46 `ok:` lines, every one of them green **on this host first** - which is what caught the draft's own two fixtures silently consuming the records they meant to leave in place. x86_64 `user tests: 169 programs built` / `159 staged, 10 left out` / `pass=159 fail=0` (exit 0); aarch64 `111 aarch64 programs built` (was 107) / `108 staged, 3 left out` / `pass=108 fail=0` (exit 0), with `=== RUN t-fork` / `t-exit-group` / `t-wait4` / `t-waitid` and their `=== PASS` lines in its log; the four programs' per-claim `ok:` lines are **byte-identical on both machines** - `t-fork` 5 lines, `t-exit-group` 4, `t-wait4` 12, `t-waitid` 20, **Batch 19 (`t-execve`, `t-mmap-shared-fork`) is the pair beside batch 18's family - replacing a process's image, and the memory a `fork` keeps shared - and it is the batch where the family's door split in two.** `t-execve` rides `libc` end to end: `libc::execve`, because the crate's one `execve` lives in `rustix::runtime`, a doc-hidden experimental module outside the enabled feature set (clause (a), the reason `t-wait4` gives for the same call), `libc::fork`, the raw `SYS_exit_group` for the code the child leaves with, and `rustix::process::waitpid` with `WaitStatus` for the reaping. Its one fixture moved: the no-std version's real-and-non-executable file was `/etc/motd`, which the second machine's payload does not carry at all (batch 3's measurement), so the program makes its own under `/tmp` at `0644 & !umask` - the mode the EACCES claim rests on is the test's own now, which is the shape batch 5's `t-access` gives the same kind of probe. `t-mmap-shared-fork` is the batch's other half and the first program in the suite that is **pure `rustix` for a reason**: `mmap_anonymous`, `munmap` and `madvise(.., Advice::DontNeed)` carry every call, and that is exactly the distinction the memory family's neighbours could not make - `t-mmap` and `t-madvise` ride `libc` because their claims *are* the six-word call and the advice vocabulary, while this program's claims are about what the pages are (whether two processes alias them), and `rustix`'s `Advice` carries Linux's own `MADV_*` numbers rather than a POSIX translation. The one wrapper edit is named in the file rather than hidden: `mmap_anonymous` ORs `MAP_ANONYMOUS` into the caller's flags and `MapFlags` has no name for that bit - harmless here precisely because the flag word is not this program's subject, and the same kind of edit is why `t-mmap` goes through the raw door. x86_64 `user tests: 169 programs built` / `159 staged, 10 left out` / `pass=159 fail=0` (exit 0); aarch64 `113 aarch64 programs built` (was 111) / `110 staged, 3 left out` / `pass=110 fail=0` (exit 0), with `=== RUN t-execve` / `t-mmap-shared-fork` and their `=== PASS` lines in its log; the two programs' per-claim `ok:` lines are **byte-identical on both machines** - `t-execve` 6 lines, `t-mmap-shared-fork` 15, diffed clean - the pin the whole exercise exists for; **Batch 20 (`t-rt-sigaction`, `t-rt-signal-queue`, `t-signal-delivery`) is the signal family's first three, and it is the batch whose raw door had to be *declared* - a record the ABI owns, which no header either machine publishes to Rust declares.** The record `rt_sigaction` copies in and out is `{handler, flags, restorer, mask}`: 32 bytes, one 8-byte mask word, `sigsetsize` the ABI's 8. It was measured on *both* machines before a line of it was written - this host's kernel fills a query's record in exactly those four slots (`rt_sigaction(SIGSEGV, NULL, &rec, 8)` leaves handler 0, flags 0, restorer 0, mask 0), and arm64's own musl builds the same 32 bytes for its `__libc_sigaction`, which the crate's `libc.a` shows storing the handler at 0 and the flags at 8, putting the restorer at 16, `memcpy`ing 8 bytes of mask to 24, ORing `0x04000000` into the flags word and calling `SYS_rt_sigaction` (134) with `sigsetsize` 8. So the no-std `lin::SigAction` was right on both machines; what moves is where the numbers come from (`libc::SYS_rt_sigaction`, 13 and 134, and libc's signal names) and the one number no table names - `SA_RESTORER`, which this libc defines for redox alone, so it is written down here with the musl measurement beside it. `libc::sigaction` could not have carried the program: it is the machine's *user-space* struct - a 128-byte `sigset_t` in the middle of it, a different field order - which its wrapper converts, and that wrapper validates the signal number itself (0 and 65 in userspace, and on musl its own three real-time numbers 32, 33 and 34 before the syscall), so the refusal set, the seeded record and the `EFAULT` probe would all have measured the libc rather than the kernel: clause (c) on the record's shape beside clause (b) on the wrapper's answers, the pair batches 12 and 17 measured. **Two things moved under the test.** The *runtime*: std arms SIGSEGV and SIGBUS on its guard stack before `main`, so "the fresh query reports SIG_DFL" is no longer what a caller finds - the value claim moved to a number nothing has touched (`SIGUSR1`), the SIGSEGV query keeps the half that *is* about the kernel (it must overwrite every seeded word of the record), and the two guard dispositions are saved and handed back on the way out, which is the shape batch 13 gave `t-sigaltstack`. The *fixture*: the unreadable-record probe's fixed `0x5000_0000` - safe only in a static-PIE image - is now a page the process maps `PROT_NONE` and unmaps, batch 13's `t-rt-sigprocmask` shape. **One divergence this pin surfaced is closed, and the fix is Linux's own split** (docs/uapi/ §5.2 carried it; §5.1 item 16 is the landing): Linux refuses SIGKILL and SIGSTOP only when a disposition is being *set* (`act && sig_kernel_only(sig)` in `do_sigaction`), so a bare `rt_sigaction(SIGKILL, NULL, &old, 8)` answers 0 and fills the record with SIG_DFL - measured on this host - where this kernel's `catchable` check ran before it looked at `act` and answered EINVAL. Batch 20 named it rather than fixed it because no claim of the day failed on it; **fixed 2026-10-16** by asking the two questions in Linux's order - `sched::signal::is_signal` is `valid_signal` (0 and `_NSIG`+1 name nothing, and are refused whether or not a disposition is handed over), `catchable` is that answer minus the kernel's own two and is asked only when `act != 0` - so the payload's claims are Linux's on **both** machines now: a bare query of either is served with SIG_DFL, clear flags and an empty mask, an install of either is EINVAL, and its 24 lines are `ok:`-identical between them. The counterfactual is the new claim itself - under the old order `a bare query of SIGKILL is served` reads EINVAL - which is what makes it a pin rather than a description. **The other two programs are named doors, because their subject is what the queue and the frame do rather than the shape of the calls that reach them.** `t-rt-signal-queue` rides `libc::sigqueue` (musl's build of it is the 128-byte `SI_QUEUE` record `rt_sigqueueinfo` takes - `si_signo` at 0, `si_code` -1 at 8, the sender at 16 and 20, the value at 24, number 138 - from the same archive), `libc::sigtimedwait` (the same build passes `sigsetsize` 8 and retries `EINTR`), `libc::sigprocmask`, `libc::sigaction` and `libc::kill`, and reads each copy's payload through the machine's own `libc::siginfo_t::si_value()` - the union `t-waitid` pinned on both machines. The one thing a libc *would* have hidden is the signal number, since musl's `sigaction` refuses its own three real-time numbers before the syscall: that is why the three copies under test live at 35, 36 and 37, and `t-rt-sigaction` is where the raw boundary itself is pinned. `t-signal-delivery` keeps exactly one raw door - `SYS_rt_sigpending` with the caller's own 8-byte word - because the claim *is* the wire's `sigset_t` and the `sigsetsize` the caller names (`pending & (1 << (SIGTERM - 1))`, the spelling `t-rt-sigprocmask` pins), while `libc::sigpending` fills the machine's own 128-byte set and blocks-and-restores around the call, so both the size the kernel is handed and the word it writes are outside it (clause (c), the same reason `t-rt-sigprocmask` is raw throughout). Its signal is `SIGTERM`, a fixture choice for the reason the SIGSEGV claim moved: the runtime has opinions about SIGPIPE, SIGSEGV and SIGBUS and none about SIGTERM - what `t-signal-frame` chose `SIGUSR1` for. x86_64 `user tests: 169 programs built` / `159 staged, 10 left out` / `pass=159 fail=0` (exit 0); aarch64 `116 aarch64 programs built` (was 113) / `113 staged, 3 left out` / `pass=113 fail=0` (exit 0), with `=== RUN t-rt-sigaction` / `t-rt-signal-queue` / `t-signal-delivery` and their `=== PASS` lines in its log; the three programs' per-claim `ok:` lines are **byte-identical on both machines** and equal to their `MPOS_ON_HOST` runs - `t-rt-sigaction` 16 lines, `t-rt-signal-queue` 12, `t-signal-delivery` 16, diffed clean; the host run of `t-rt-sigaction` prints one extra `note:` line for each claim whose host answer differs, which is not an `ok:` line - and `user/abitest` is down to 53 programs (22 syscall plus 31 integration); **Batch 21 (`t-sa-restart`, `t-signalfd`) is the signal family's other two halves - the flag that rewinds an interrupted call, and the descriptor that hands signals over as data - and it is the batch that first moved a fixture because the *claim* was wrong about Linux, then found the kernel defect standing behind it.** `t-sa-restart` is `t-tty-eintr`'s counterfactual made observable: with `SA_RESTART` set, the parked terminal read is rewound and answers the line typed 400 ms after the signal, and with the flag clear the same read answers `EINTR` at the signal and the retry takes the byte - beside the never-restarted pair, `poll` and the sleep, which answer `EINTR` *with* the flag set, and the sleep's remainder pointer, the one thing that makes a retry cost the rest of the sleep rather than the whole of it. Its doors are the named ones: `libc::sigaction` (whose musl build fills the restorer), `rustix::io::read` (a plain `read`, with no `EINTR` retry in its backend, which is what makes the counterfactual a claim), `rustix::event::poll`, and `libc::nanosleep` *for its second pointer* - `rustix::thread::nanosleep` takes only the request, so the remainder has nowhere to go through it. Two wrapper differences are named rather than hidden: rustix's `poll` is `ppoll` on **both** machines (the call `t-poll` pins, `poll` itself, has no arm64 number), and musl's `nanosleep` is not a binding of `nanosleep` - the crate's own `libc.a` shows it calling `__clock_nanosleep(CLOCK_REALTIME, 0, req, rem)`, once, with no `EINTR` loop - so the wait this program pins on both machines is `clock_nanosleep`, which is the one call mpos serves both spellings from. Two numbers no maintained table carries: `ITIMER_REAL` (this libc defines it for fuchsia, hurd, aix and nto and in no `linux_like` file - measured) and `TIOCGPTN`/`TIOCSPTLCK`, which no libc names at all and which are computed the way `asm-generic/ioctls.h` computes them and checked against a compiled probe. The fixture is a pty pair per check, minted by opening `/dev/ptmx` and asking `TIOCGPTN` for its slave, both ends `O_NOCTTY` so a test cannot acquire a controlling terminal by accident - and it is the first pty this suite opens at all. **`t-signalfd` is where the batch's two findings came from, and the first is that the no-std claim was wrong about Linux.** It never blocked `SIGCHLD`, and on this kernel it did not have to; Linux discards a generated signal that is unblocked and whose disposition is the ignoring default, and `SIGCHLD`'s default action is exactly that - measured on this host with the pair one child's exit makes: unblocked, `-1`/`EAGAIN`; blocked, the 128-byte record. So the block became part of the fixture, which is also what the file's own contract for the *other* half already said. With it in place a second divergence showed, and it is a defect rather than a difference of style: this kernel keeps a child's exit in one table and *also* queues a generated `SIGCHLD` when the caller has it blocked, so a descriptor whose mask names `SIGCHLD` hands over **two** records for one death - the queued copy first, `ssi_code` `SI_USER`, then the exit record itself, `CLD_EXITED` with the child's pid (the payload log, verbatim: `note: 2 records for one death - the first carries ssi_code 0`) - where Linux's pending queue is the one table and one death is one record. The program therefore drains the descriptor and claims the *count* (1 under `MPOS_ON_HOST`, 2 on the payload, with the note that names both halves), while the claims about the *exit* record - `CLD_EXITED`, the child's pid, `wait(4)` still collecting - hold on both machines. Both are named in docs/uapi/ §5.2 and not fixed here: the owner is `src/sched/runtime.rs`'s `queue_signal` call, and that file is held by another line of work in this tree. The record itself is the machine's own `libc::signalfd_siginfo` now, its length pinned against the ABI's 128 as a claim of its own (the `t-waitid` move a second time), and the door is `libc::signalfd` - one spelling rather than two, because this libc binds no `signalfd4` at all (measured) and its `signalfd` *is* `SYS_signalfd4` on both machines (crate `libc.a`: 74 here, 289 on x86_64, with the size word 8), where the no-std version's "plain spelling" reached the legacy `signalfd`, which has no arm64 number. x86_64 `user tests: 169 programs built` / `159 staged, 10 left out` / `pass=159 fail=0` (exit 0); aarch64 `118 aarch64 programs built` (was 116) / `115 staged, 3 left out` / `pass=115 fail=0` (exit 0), with `=== RUN t-sa-restart` / `t-signalfd` and their `=== PASS` lines in its log; the two programs' per-claim `ok:` lines are **byte-identical on both machines** and equal to their `MPOS_ON_HOST` runs - `t-sa-restart` 26 lines, `t-signalfd` 26, diffed clean, with the payload's signalfd run adding the one `note:` line above, which is not an `ok:` line - and `user/abitest` is down to 51 programs (20 syscall plus 31 integration) after batch 21; host tests 516; xtask tests 89; `depgraph` within the ratchet. **The threading pair** (`t-clone`, `t-futex`) is the first batch to find a *kernel* bug rather than a test-side one, and it is an aarch64-only ABI break: Linux's raw `clone` is the one syscall whose last two arguments are not in the same order on every machine - the kernel calls the difference `CLONE_BACKWARDS`, x86-64 taking `(flags, stack, parent_tid, child_tid, tls)` and arm64 `(flags, stack, parent_tid, tls, child_tid)`, which glibc's arm64 `__clone` states in its own comment ("X0:flags, x1:newsp, x2:parenttidptr, x3:newtls, x4:childtid") - and this kernel's handler read the x86-64 order on both. Measured, in this order: `t-clone`'s `CLONE_CHILD_SETTID` claim was refused `EINVAL` on the second machine, because the tid pointer sat in the slot the handler does not look at; the musl thread the program then made faulted at `pc 0x3` on its way out, because `CLONE_SETTLS` had handed it the *tid word's* address as its thread pointer and its exit path walked a TLS block that was never one; and the sweep never reached its end, because the word `pthread_join` waits on was never woken. The fix is `syscall::linux::mod`'s `clone_arguments`, one `swap(3, 4)` under `cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")`, and it is the first thing on this machine that puts a musl thread's tid and thread pointer where the kernel looks for them. Both programs are `std`-side now: `rustix::thread::futex` carries four of the five futex calls (`wait`, `wake`, `wait_bitset`, `wake_bitset`, whose `NonZeroU32` *is* the man page's "an empty bitset is not a wake anyone could answer"), the fifth - the empty bitset's `EINVAL` - rides `libc::syscall(SYS_futex, ..)` because no value of that type is zero, `clone` rides `libc::syscall(SYS_clone, ..)` with the two argument orders reconciled in one helper, and the real thread is `std::thread::spawn` where the no-std version mapped its own stack and entered through a `global_asm` stub - so its "a stack was mapped for the thread" and "the tid was parked where the caller named" checks retired with the scaffold (what they were really reaching for, `CLONE_CHILD_SETTID` parking a tid, is `t-set-tid-address`'s subject).
