Two claims are named divergences, both measured on this host rather than assumed: an unknown flag bit is `EINVAL` here and reads as `CLONE_NEWNET` there (a new namespace to a privileged caller, `EPERM` to an unprivileged one), and a null tid pointer is refused here where Linux starts the child and skips the write. A third is *avoided* rather than gated, because a request that means different things on the two machines cannot be one claim: the no-std version cloned with a literal zero flag word, and an empty word means "no signal announces the child", which Linux answers by auto-reaping it (`wait4` is `ECHILD`; measured) - so every process-shaped clone here names `SIGCHLD`, the word `fork` is actually built from, and the reaping and exit-code claims are about the same request on both machines. The spawn shape (`CLONE_VM|CLONE_VFORK`) retires to `t-stdspawn`, whose header already names that flag word: under `CLONE_VM` a child shares the caller's address space and therefore the caller's stack, so a raw-syscall child has no frame of its own to return on - that entry-point shuffle is what a libc's `clone` wrapper exists to do, and rebuilding it here would be the scaffolding this migration removes. x86_64 `170 programs built` / `160 staged, 10 left out` / `pass=155 fail=5` (the five are other work in flight - `t-drm-sync` and the tty family), exit 33; aarch64 `123 staged` / `pass=119 fail=4` (the same tty four), reaching `=== END`, with `=== PASS t-clone` and `=== PASS t-futex` in both logs and their per-claim `ok:` lines **byte-identical on the two machines**; **Batch 23 (`t-unix-dgram`) is the AF_UNIX pair's first half - the names that receive whole messages instead of byte streams - and it is the first batch whose claims had to be *measured against Linux* before they could be pinned, because the no-std original had only ever run here (`unix(7)` was its source, not a measurement).** The sockets, names and byte doors are `rustix`'s; the two claims about `recvfrom`'s `addrlen` ride `libc::syscall(SYS_recvfrom, ..)` with a `libc::sockaddr_un`, because the crate parses the address into a `SocketAddrUnix` and does not hand the count back. Running it against a host Linux - the raw-syscall probe is `.workbuddy/probe_unix_dgram.c` - split the claims in three. One got *truer*: the `SEQPACKET` listen claim has always said "*named*", and Linux refuses `listen` on an unbound `SEQPACKET` with `EINVAL`, so the socket is bound first now and the claim holds on both machines where the no-std version only held here. Four answers are this kernel's own, each named in its claim and accepted under `MPOS_ON_HOST=1` with the host's own answer printed: a second `connect` is `EISCONN` here where Linux accepts a repeat connect to the same name; an addressed `sendto` on a connected datagram is `EISCONN` here where Linux takes the address; an unbound sender's name is three bytes here where Linux reports `addrlen` 0; and a datagram aimed at a stream listener's name answers `ECONNREFUSED` here where Linux answers `EPROTOTYPE` - these want docs/uapi/ §5.2 rows, and read like kernel divergences rather than test-side ones. The queue's *depth* (64) is this kernel's number too: Linux bounds the queue by `SO_RCVBUF`, so its count is a byte budget and not a message count, and what holds on both is the shape - a full queue refuses the sender with `EAGAIN` instead of dropping what was sent, and a drained read makes room again. The four tty programs the previous batch left red reached `=== PASS` on both machines in this batch's gate, and the *kernel* reason is worth recording here beside it: `rustix::termios::tcgetattr` asks `TCGETS2` first, `ENOTTY` sent it to the `TCGETS` fallback, and `TCGETS`'s 60-byte structure overran the 44-byte `termios2` the caller had handed it - the last eight bytes written are the two 38400 speeds, so the caller's saved return address became `0x960000009600` on aarch64 (an instruction abort at its own garbage PC, `ESR 0x82000004`) and a quiet stack clobber on x86_64. `src/vfs/termios.rs` gained the `termios2` layout (`store_to2`/`from_ptr2`, with a host unit test pinning the offsets) and `src/syscall/linux/fs/ioctl.rs` the `TCGETS2`/`TCSETS2` families, leaving `TCGETS`'s existing 60-byte contract untouched. x86_64 `170 programs built` / `160 staged, 10 left out` / `pass=159 fail=1` (the one was `t-drm-sync`, other work in flight), exit 33; aarch64 `124 staged` / `pass=124 fail=0`, reaching `=== END`, with `=== PASS t-unix-dgram` in both logs and its per-claim `ok:` lines identical on the two machines; host tests 517; `user/abitest` was down to **43** programs (12 syscall plus 31 integration). **Batch 24 (`t-unix-scm`) is the AF_UNIX pair's other half - the descriptors and the identities a unix socket carries - and it is the batch that found a second `kernel` bug, this time in a flag's *value*, by spelling it from `linux/socket.h` instead of from this kernel's own table.** Every door that is not a control message is `rustix`'s (`socketpair`, `pipe`, `sockopt::{socket_peercred, socket_passcred, set_socket_passcred, socket_type}`, `fcntl_getfd`/`dup`, `event::epoll`, `io::{read, write}`); the control chain stays on the raw door, because half the claims *are* numbers on the wire - `msg_controllen == CMSG_SPACE(4)`, `cmsg_len == CMSG_LEN(12)`, `msg_controllen == 0`, `MSG_CTRUNC` at `sizeof(cmsghdr)` of room - and `rustix`'s `SendAncillaryBuffer`/`RecvAncillaryBuffer` compute those numbers internally and hand back only the parsed messages, so a claim about the number cannot be made through them. The chain's header is also read field by field rather than through `libc::cmsghdr`, because musl's `msghdr` carries a `socklen_t` control length where glibc's carries a `size_t` while the kernel writes one layout either way. (One helper turns libc's `-1`/`errno` back into the negative errno the claims are written against, which is the shape a bare syscall hands back.) **The bug:** this kernel's `MSG_CMSG_CLOEXEC` was `0x10000` - Linux's `MSG_WAITFORONE`, the bit `recvmmsg` reads for something else entirely, and the bit `src/net/socket.rs`'s own `recvmmsg` already honours. Linux's is `0x40000000`. The no-std suite's `lin::MSG_CMSG_CLOEXEC` carried the same wrong number, so a test that read the bit out of *either* table agreed with the other and the flag was never exercised from `linux/socket.h`: a caller asking Linux's way got no close-on-exec on the received descriptor, and a caller asking `0x10000` got it while also asking `recvmmsg` for `MSG_WAITFORONE`. One constant in `src/net/socket.rs` (and the two comments that had the neighbouring bits wrong) plus `lin.rs`'s copy, and the migrated claim - written from `libc` - is what stands behind it. One *divergence* joins batch 23's four: a data-less `SCM_RIGHTS` on a stream socketpair is accepted and queues nothing on Linux, so a blocking `recvmsg` **parks** there (measured twice - `.workbuddy/probe_unix_scm.c` and, before that probe existed, a host run that had to be killed), where this kernel delivers the record with a zero-byte return; that section is accepted under `MPOS_ON_HOST=1`, where it makes the non-blocking read and prints the host's answer. x86_64 `170 programs built` / `160 staged, 10 left out` / `pass=159 fail=1` (the one is `t-drm-sync`, other work in flight), exit 33; aarch64 `125 staged` / `pass=125 fail=0`, reaching `=== END`, with `=== PASS t-unix-scm` (77 claims) and `=== PASS t-unix-dgram` in both logs; host tests 517; **Batch 25 (`t-prctl`, `t-seccomp`)** is the process-control pair, and it found two kernel bugs. `t-prctl` (32 claims) keeps the option table honest - `rustix` for the name pair, the keepcaps pair and `secure_computing_mode`, the raw door for the dumpable and bounding-set options and for the option *numbers*, which are themselves half of every claim. The bug is a **wrong constant**: this kernel wrote `PR_GET_KEEPCAPS` as **9** - the setter's 8 plus one, the shape a reader expects - where `linux/prctl.h` says **7** (9 is `PR_GET_FPEMU`), and `lin.rs` carried the same 9, so the no-std suite and the kernel agreed on a number no Linux program sends and a caller using the real `PR_GET_KEEPCAPS` was refused `EINVAL`. `rustix::thread::get_keep_capabilities` sends 7, which is what failed against the unfixed kernel; one constant in `syscall::linux::abi` is the fix, and the claim that 9 is *not* the getter is pinned beside it (measured: 9 is `EINVAL` on Linux too). `t-seccomp` (37 claims) is the filter half - `rustix` binds none of `seccomp(2)`, so the BPF program, the eight actions and `struct sock_fprog` come from `libc`'s uapi mirrors - and it found a **stale architecture gate**: the enforcement block in `syscall::linux::dispatch` was `#[cfg(not(target_arch = "aarch64"))]`, with a comment saying no filter could be *installed* there until `seccomp(2)` was ported. That stopped being true when `posix` grew the handler for both machines, and the leftovers were exactly the shape that hides: installing answered 0 and `PR_GET_SECCOMP` answered 2 on aarch64, so both halves looked right while **nothing ever judged a call**. Removing the gate is the fix, and the second machine now enforces filters. Two more things the port had to get right, both measured rather than assumed: the *judged number* comes from `libc::SYS_getpid`/`SYS_getppid` instead of the literal 39/110 the no-std file wrote down (aarch64's are 172/173, and this crate *is* staged on the second machine, so a literal would have installed a filter judging a call nothing makes); and the judged *call* is the raw `syscall`, because a libc wrapper is a second thing that can answer - under a filter denying `getpid`, `syscall(SYS_getpid)` answers `-1`/`EPERM` while `getpid()` answers `-1` with `errno` left at **0** (measured on a host Linux, `.workbuddy/probe_seccomp_self.c`), and the first draft's wrapper call reported five false failures about filters that were working. One divergence joins the list: `SECCOMP_GET_ACTION_AVAIL` for `USER_NOTIF`, which this kernel refuses (no supervisor to summon) and a host Linux serves, accepted under `MPOS_ON_HOST=1` with the host's answer printed. x86_64 `160 staged` / `pass=159 fail=1` (the one is `t-drm-sync`, other work in flight), exit 33; aarch64 `127 staged` / `pass=127 fail=0`, reaching `=== END`; `=== PASS t-prctl` and `=== PASS t-seccomp` in both logs with identical per-claim counts; host tests 517; **Batch 26 (`t-times`, `t-timer-create`)** is the time pair - CPU accounting and POSIX interval timers - and it found **two more argument-shape bugs**. `t-times` (20 claims) pins that `times()`/`getrusage()` measure real CPU: a user spin raises `tms_utime`/`ru_utime` and leaves `stime` behind (the ring split is the point), the elapsed clock advances strictly across a 50 ms sleep and only monotonically across a spin, `tv_usec` is microseconds, an unknown `getrusage` who is `EINVAL`, and a reaped child's CPU folds into `RUSAGE_CHILDREN` and `times()`'s `tms_cutime`. `rustix` binds neither call, so both go through the raw door - and the migration found that **`libc`'s `rusage` is not the uapi's record**: measured, it is **272** bytes on this target, a generic-unix superset of thirty `long`s where `linux/resource.h` has fourteen, so the 144-byte record is spelled in the test the way `t-unix-scm` spells `cmsghdr`, with a compile-time size assert (a size claim about `libc::rusage` would be false rather than tight). One no-std claim was also **vacuous** and is now real: `[0u8; 144]` was declared zero and checked all-zero after the call, which a kernel that wrote nothing would also pass; the migrated claim seeds the record with `0xAA` first, so what it pins is the kernel's write. `t-timer-create` (45 claims) is the family itself, in the shape musl builds for `SIGEV_THREAD` (a thread blocked in `rt_sigtimedwait` plus a `SIGEV_THREAD_ID` timer carrying `SIGRTMIN`); `libc` binds no `sigevent`, `itimerspec`, `timer_t`, `SIGEV_*` or `SI_TIMER` for linux at all (measured: those exist in the crate for fuchsia, hurd, aix and vxworks only), so the three wire records are spelled at the uapi's offsets with compile-time size asserts and every call is raw. **Bug one: `SIGEV_SIGNAL` is 0 and `SIGEV_NONE` is 1** in `linux/signal.h`; the kernel had them the other way round, and `lin.rs` carried the same swap, so the suite and the kernel agreed with each other and with no libc and the suite passed. What that cost a real caller: the true `SIGEV_NONE` (1) was read as `SIGEV_SIGNAL`, and since a silent timer leaves `sigev_signo` at 0 it was refused **`EINVAL`** - a `timer_create` that could never succeed - while the true `SIGEV_SIGNAL` (0) got `SIGEV_NONE`, a timer that silently never fired. The two claims that name the numbers are what found it. **Bug two: `getrusage` compared the whole 64-bit `who`.** `RUSAGE_*` are `int`s in the uapi and Linux's syscall wrapper reads one as the register's low 32 bits, sign-extended - so `RUSAGE_CHILDREN` arrives as `0x0000_0000_ffff_ffff` from every compiled call and every `syscall(2)` vararg, and as `0xffff_ffff_ffff_ffff` only from a caller that sign-extended it itself. The handler compared the full word, so a libc's ordinary `getrusage(RUSAGE_CHILDREN, ..)` was refused `EINVAL` on **both** machines; the no-std suite spelled the sign-extended form and never saw it. The fix truncates to the `int` the ABI names, and it surfaced in the first guest run of the migrated test. Three divergences join the list, all measured: `CLOCK_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID` answers `EOPNOTSUPP` here where Linux serves it; `TIMER_ABSTIME` is refused (one clock, no absolute domain) where Linux arms it; and a **deleted timer's already-queued record** stays takeable here, where a host Linux frees the `sigqueue` - `sigpending` still shows the bit while the take answers `EAGAIN` (`.workbuddy/probe_timer_delete.c`). The first draft of that take used a *null* timeout and hung on the host for exactly that reason: the polling form (a zero timeout) is what the no-std file used and what both machines answer, and a claim that can hang is worse than one that fails. x86_64 `160 staged` / `pass=159 fail=1` (the one is `t-drm-sync`, other work in flight; `t-clock-gettime` flaked red once - a monotonic-monotonicity claim - and passed on the immediate re-run); aarch64 `129 staged` / `pass=129 fail=0`, reaching `=== END`; `=== PASS t-times` and `=== PASS t-timer-create` in both logs with identical per-claim counts; host tests 517. **Batch 27 (`t-wait-eintr`)** migrated the last of the blocking-wait family: the ten nodes a catchable signal must end - a pipe's read *and* its write, an eventfd, a signalfd, an inotify watch, a unix datagram queue, a unix accept queue, a pty slave's write into a full master, and `wait4`/`waitid` - each pinned twice (the `EINTR` with the handler run, then the positive control that the retried call answers when the thing it waited for arrives), with the two writes on the partial-count rule rather than `EINTR`. The nodes are `rustix`'s (`pipe`, `eventfd`, `inotify`, `socketpair`, `socket`/`bind`/`listen`/`accept`) and the reads/writes are `rustix::io`, which has no `EINTR` retry in its backend; `wait4` is `rustix::process::waitpid`, `waitid` the raw door `t-waitid` opened, and the one node no library binds here is `signalfd` (this libc binds neither spelling), so its descriptor is the raw `SYS_signalfd4` and its delivery the raw `SYS_tgkill`. The pty fixture is `t-pty`'s `mint` (returning `Option`, not a `panic!`): a host with no `devpts` cannot build it, so there the four pty-write claims are named and not made (guarded by `host_run`), while on the kernel a missing pair is a failed check. x86_64 `160 staged` / `pass=159 fail=1` (the one is `t-drm-sync`, other work in flight; `t-clock-gettime` flaked red once and passed on the immediate re-run, the same monotonic flake as batch 26); aarch64 `130 staged` / `pass=130 fail=0` reaching `=== END`; `=== PASS t-wait-eintr` in both logs with the **same 55 claims** on each (the pty-write four among them on both, so the guest minted a pair); host tests 517; `user/abitest` is down to **37** programs (6 syscall plus 31 integration). **Batch 28 (`t-getdents64`)** moved the raw directory stream: the syscall is the raw door `libc::syscall(SYS_getdents64, ..)` because the record's *shape* is what half the claims are - `d_reclen` strides the buffer and `d_type` labels each name, which a wrapper handing back parsed entries would hide - and `SYS_getdents64` is `libc`'s own per-machine number (217 here, 61 on the second machine), the last no-std literal this program carried. The wire layout is no longer hand-written: the field offsets come from the machine's own `libc::dirent64` (`offset_of!`), pinned against the uapi's numbers 0/8/16/18/19 as compile-time claims, so a drifting `dirent64` is a build failure rather than a silently wrong stride. **One claim moved rather than migrated**: the no-std version wrote the busybox layout down ("sh" and "cat" answer `DT_LNK`, root `/bin` answers `DT_DIR`) - a fact about the root the program happens to run in, false on a merged-`/usr` host where `/bin` is a symlink to `usr/bin` - so what is pinned now is the property a client's `ls -F` actually compares: the kernel's `d_type` **is the type `lstat` reports** (`symlink_metadata`, which answers for the link and not its target), and both names are *listed* at all. The rest is the named calls: the opens and the rewind are `rustix::fs::{open, seek}`, `EINVAL` for a buffer too small for one record, and the lowest free descriptor discovered with `rustix::io::dup` rather than assumed to be 3. x86_64 `160 staged` / `pass=160 fail=0` (the count does not move - the program was already in the sweep from the abitest side; this batch only changes which crate it rides in); aarch64 `131 staged` / `pass=131 fail=0` reaching `=== END` - **the first run of this program on the second machine**, which the aarch64 sweep's abitest-only staging had never carried; host tests 517; `user/abitest` is down to **36** programs (5 syscall plus 31 integration). **Batch 29 (`t-syslog`)** moved the kernel log's own door, and the host measured the one thing this migration could not measure anywhere else: **a gate this kernel does not have**. The door is the raw door `libc::syscall(SYS_syslog, ..)` (`libc`'s own per-machine number, 103 here and 116 on the second machine) because every answer is the number the door itself gives; the sixteen claims are the no-std file's, unchanged - the counters (`SIZE_UNREAD` a count, `SIZE_BUFFER` the 16 KiB this kernel designs in), the non-destructive read-all asked twice, the null-buffer `EINVAL`s, the console-level validation 1..=8, the five bookkeeping types, and a type outside the eleven. The probe (`.workbuddy/probe_syslog.c`) cannot get past real Linux's privilege check: this sandbox's host answers `EPERM` for *every* type - the counters included - because `dmesg_restrict` is on and a container's root holds no `CAP_SYSLOG` in the initial user namespace, and the gate comes *first*: even an unknown type (99), which this kernel answers `EINVAL`, gets `EPERM` from the host. So the host run (`MPOS_ON_HOST=1`, the named divergence: Linux validates permission before it validates anything, and this kernel has no privilege check at all) accepts the `EPERM` the host actually gave on every check; the gate keeps pinning this kernel. x86_64 `160 staged` / `pass=160 fail=0` (again the count does not move - the program was already in the sweep from the abitest side); aarch64 `132 staged` / `pass=132 fail=0` reaching `=== END`, `=== PASS t-syslog` in both logs with the **same 16 claims**, the `SIZE_BUFFER` 16 KiB pin among them on both - so the second machine's kernel keeps the same ring capacity by the same design; host tests 517; `user/abitest` is down to **35** programs (4 syscall plus 31 integration). **Batch 30 (`t-maps`, plus the `t-process-vm` removal)** is two moves in one: the `t-process-vm` no-std original was lifted out outright - its std-side successor `t-process-vm-libc` predates this batch and covers every claim the original made (the handshake, the guard-page and no-page `EFAULT`s, the `ESRCH` pids, the `EINVAL` flags word) plus several the original never had (the cursor walk, the 1024 iovec cap, the null-array `EFAULT`) - and **`t-maps`**, the `/proc/self/maps` reader, moved properly. The format is the whole contract, so the no-std parser moved with it on purpose: six whitespace fields, lowercase hex addresses, the offset zero-padded to eight digits, and the name starting in column 73 - re-measured on this sandbox's host (`.workbuddy/probe_maps.c`), where every named line starts its name there and a nameless line stops after the inode, exactly as this kernel prints them. The probe measured **two divergences**. The first is the shared anonymous object's *name*: this kernel spells it the tmpfs way, `/memfd: (deleted)`, where the host's Linux names the very same mapping `/dev/zero (deleted)` - so the pin keeps the property (a shared anonymous mapping is *named*, with a nonzero inode) and a host run accepts the host's spelling beside this kernel's. The constants moved from literals to `libc` - `SYS_ptrace` is the per-machine number the no-std file had pinned as x86_64's 101 (117 on the second machine), and the requests and signals are `libc::PTRACE_*`/`SIG*` - the exact migration the aarch64 sweep exists to catch; the sleeps are `rustix::thread::clock_nanosleep_relative`, and the bounded `WNOHANG` polls that replaced the wait that once hung the machine for ten minutes are kept. The probe (`.workbuddy/probe_ptrace.c`) measured **a door that does not open at all**: this sandbox's container seccomp profile answers `EPERM` for *every* `ptrace` request before the pid is resolved - attaching to the caller's own child, which yama's scope 1 would allow, fails the same way - so a host run measures nothing about the door, and the ptrace claims accept that `EPERM` under `MPOS_ON_HOST`, named as such. What the probe *did* measure is everything around the door, and it agrees on all of it: the user's `SIGSTOP` is reported through `WUNTRACED` (`WIFSTOPPED`), a plain `detach(0)` leaves that stop alone, and the user's own `SIGCONT` is what ends it - the split a debugger relies on, pinned on both machines by the non-ptrace half. x86_64 `159 staged` / `pass=159 fail=0` (the count does not move - the program was already in the sweep from the abitest side); aarch64 `134 staged` / `pass=134 fail=0` reaching `=== END`, `=== PASS t-ptrace-attach` in both logs with the **same 15 claims** - the non-descendant attach among them, which the aarch64 sweep had never carried; host tests 517; `user/abitest` is down to **32** programs (1 syscall plus 31 integration). **Batch 32 (`t-posix`)** moved the largest and the last of the syscall family, and the probes (`.workbuddy/probe_posix.c`, `probe_seccomp_mode.c`, `probe_diag.c`) earned their keep: **musl's `libc` wrapper protocol differs from glibc's** (a failure is a plain `-1` with the errno beside it, not a negative errno - every wrapped call whose errno a claim pins now travels through one `rc()` that restores the `-errno` spelling the no-std file's stubs answered in), and **this target's `libc::sched_getscheduler` wrapper answers `ENOSYS` where the raw door succeeds** (measured: the raw `sched_getscheduler(0)` answers 0 in the same process) - so all four scheduler doors are raw. The probe measured **four host divergences, each a gate or a service this kernel does not have**: the host's `setresuid(-1, 1, -1)` answers `EINVAL` (raw and glibc alike) where this kernel lowers the effective id, so the whole sudo dance and the claims that depend on it are named and not made on a host run (`t-pty`'s mint rule) while the gate keeps pinning all of them; `personality(0x1000)` the host stores where this kernel validates the table; `vhangup` the host answers `EPERM` for (`CAP_SYS_TTY_CONFIG` first, the klogctl-shaped gate) where this kernel pins the no-terminal `ENOTTY`; and `seccomp(STRICT)` the container refuses to install outright (`EFAULT`, flags or no flags) so the strict child's death shape is unmeasurable there - the child's early exit is accepted, named. `unshare(CLONE_NEWNS|CLONE_NEWTIME)` the host *serves* to root where this kernel keeps its deliberate `EOPNOTSUPP` (superseded 2026-10-16: TIME is the ladder's fifth rung and both sides grant it, §6.3); `getcpu` the host answers with the core it really ran on. Everything else the probe measured agrees: the fresh-process identity words, the capability words (`ffffffff`/`0x1ff` from `CAP_LAST_CAP`, nothing inheritable, the `capset` round-trip), the raw `getpriority` encoding 20..1 and its clamp, `GET_ACTION_AVAIL`'s `EOPNOTSUPP` with the caller's word untouched, the namespace refusals and their ordering, `setns`, `tgkill`'s probes, and every answer of the memory advice family including the freshly-mapped-not-resident pin. `libc::CAP_LAST_CAP` and `libc::tgkill` do not exist on this target - the constant is the uapi's value (40) and the call the raw door. x86_64 `159 staged` / `pass=159 fail=0` (the count does not move - the program was already in the sweep from the abitest side); aarch64 `135 staged` / `pass=135 fail=0` reaching `=== END`, `=== PASS t-posix` in both logs with the **same 99 claims** - the whole family, first time on the second machine; host tests 517; `user/abitest` is down to **24** programs (**0 syscall** plus 24 integration; batches 34-36 moved t-epoll-socket, t-memfd-shm, t-mountinfo, t-aslr and t-uevent) - **the syscall family is fully migrated**; the integration half is under way | **low per batch, large in total** - 29 programs remain, and each batch must first classify whether the second machine *serves* the call (a genuine gap is named in `tests.toml`'s `aarch64` group, never silently dropped). *Batch 33 (`t-sock-flags`, `t-fpu-across-switch`)*: the first integration batch, and both of its programs ran on the second machine for the first time - `t-sock-flags` pinned `recvfrom`'s number there (`207`, against `45` on the first) and got the same `EOPNOTSUPP`, which is the whole reason the sweep stages only `user/tests` on that arm. Two things a migrated integration program has to decide that a syscall one does not: **which register the claim is about** - `t-fpu-across-switch` pins `xmm7` on x86_64 but `v15.d[0]` on aarch64, because this kernel's switch saves `q8..q15` and not `q0..q7` (AAPCS64 leaves the low eight caller-saved and the kernel is entitled to use them as scratch), so pinning `v0` there would fail for a reason that is not the bug - and **whether a claim can park**: Linux implements `MSG_PEEK` and waits on an empty socket, this kernel refuses it, so a host run sets `O_NONBLOCK` before the first claim and accepts `EAGAIN` beside the refusal rather than hanging. Both programs: 4 claims, identical on both machines. *Batch 34 (`t-epoll-socket`, `t-memfd-shm`)*: the first integration batch that had to ask what a *door* is worth on each machine. `t-epoll-socket` (9 claims) needs a `SOCK_RAW` socket, and the host has no `CAP_NET_RAW` (measured `EPERM`, `probe_rawicmp_epoll.c`) - so on a host run there is nothing to watch and the program says so and finishes with **no claims** rather than inventing one. On the guest it is 9/9 on both machines, which is the first time an *IP* wakeup of a parked `epoll_wait` has been pinned on the second machine at all. `t-memfd-shm` (18 claims) has exactly one divergence - `MFD_HUGETLB`: this kernel refuses it `EOPNOTSUPP` rather than answering with an ordinary memfd the caller believes is huge-backed, current Linux creates one (`probe_memfd.c`), and the claim names that instead of pinning Linux's. Everything else - the closed flag vocabulary (`0x1000` is `EINVAL` on both), both mappings of one fd aliasing, `write(2)` reaching the mapping, the seal round-trip, and the `SCM_RIGHTS` handover whose child reads the parent's marker - is the same answer on both, which is what makes that one line the whole divergence. One bug the migration caught in itself: a `check` written *between* `fork` and `if pid == 0` is printed by both processes, so the child's copy of the report is not the one judged - the claim about the fork now follows the child's branch. *Batch 35 (`t-mountinfo`, `t-aslr`)*: **one new gap named, in a place nobody had looked.** `t-mountinfo` (19 claims) is the same answer on both machines except for the two files whose honest content is "this machine has none": `/proc/modules` is empty here and 11 KiB on a host with modules, `/sys/bus/usb/devices` is empty here and lists 31 there - both named, since the claim is that the file *exists* and reads as nothing, which is what `lsmod`/`lsusb` need, not that the machine has no modules. Everything else about the file holds identically on a current host: 119 lines, every one of them ten fields, the `-` at field seven, and field three a `maj:min` equal to the mountpoint's `st_dev`. `t-aslr` (5 claims) turned up the gap: the code address is fixed on both (this image is linked for the addresses it names, measured the same on a host), and the stack and the anonymous mapping are drawn on both - but **the heap anchor is not drawn here**: `brk(0)` comes back just above the image every run, where Linux randomises it (measured at a spread of megabytes, `0x1f99e` against `0x265ff` in one pair of runs). No program's correctness rests on it - a heap that moves is hardening, not a contract - so the claim names which answer a machine gets and leaves the gate pinning mpos's; closing it is a change to what this kernel randomises (`docs/notes/` §12.2's three landmarks) rather than a fix to a wrong number, and it is written down here as the thing to decide rather than as a divergence to accept forever. *Batch 36 (`t-uevent`)*: 23 claims, identical on both machines, and the second machine has never run this program before. Two things it cost to get there. **A precondition became a claim.** The blocking `recvmsg` this test exists for is only answerable if there is a device whose sysfs `uevent` file can be written to announce it - `udevadm trigger`'s door, so no device has to appear - and a machine without one does not answer `EAGAIN`, it answers *nothing*: the C probe written to measure the host hung there until it was killed, and the same call in a sweep would have taken the whole run with it. So the file's presence is now checked and claimed before the wait is attempted, and that is also the host's divergence - a host has neither that path nor the right to unicast between two monitors (`EPERM`, measured), so a host run says so and claims nothing. **The musl error protocol bit a third program.** Five of the twenty-three claims - the `EAGAIN` on an empty queue, `ECONNREFUSED`, both `EINVAL`s, and the sender's own broadcast - compare against a negated errno, and `libc::sendmsg`/`recvmsg` answer a plain `-1` with the errno beside it on this target; they all read `-1` and failed on both machines identically, which is the signature of a protocol mistake rather than a kernel one. One `fix()` at the door, the same one `t-posix` and `t-epoll` carry | **Between batch 36 and batch 37, the second machine's own gaps were closed instead of migrated past** (the user asked for the aarch64 hole to be filled before more migration). An audit - an Explore agent over the whole tree, every finding re-verified by hand - turned up four real ones, of which three were closed: **the evdev/uinput ioctl gates** (the 'E' and 'U' families plus three gates in `file.rs` were `#[cfg(not(target_arch = "aarch64"))]` while the machine itself has carried an input registry, `/dev/input/event0` and `/dev/uinput` all along - stale gates of exactly the shape the batch-25 seccomp one was), **the fbdev ioctl gates** (five commands behind the same shape of gate; the machine publishes `/dev/fb0` unconditionally), and **`/dev/dri` itself** (`card0`/`renderD128` were published only under `cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")` and the 'd' family was gated too, though the aarch64 render task has been publishing its card since it was ported). The boot check now resolves `/dev/dri/card0` through the mounted `/dev` on the second machine (`M8_GPU_OK` names it), and the same fixing pass found a real bug beside the gates: aarch64's `check_input` returned after the first MMIO window, so only one of the machine's two virtio-input devices ever registered - and it would have been `event0` twice, because `input::register` numbers nodes by the length of the list a single call passes; it now collects every window and registers them in one call, and the boot log shows `event0` (tablet) and `event1` (keyboard) with `M8_INPUT_OK` counting 2. The fourth gap stays open rather than faked: aarch64's debug-exit - QEMU 11.1.2's `virt` machine has no debug-exit device to attach and the second machine's harness reads the `=== END` marker rather than an exit code, so `mpos_qemu_debug_exit` still falls back to `halt()` there, recorded as blocked rather than worked around. Gates: aarch64 `143 staged, pass=143 fail=0`; x86_64 `160 staged, pass=160 fail=0`; no dead-code warnings on the newly opened paths. **Batch 37 (`t-tcp-close`, `t-fill-ramfs`, `t-dns`) is the batch where the gate's *default* sweep stopped being the whole answer.** All three land in `tests.toml` groups the default run excludes - `t-dns` and `t-tcp-close` in `external` (they need the slirp network) and `t-fill-ramfs` in `slow` - so the x86_64 gate reported them as NOT RUN, which is not a failure but is also not evidence; the aarch64 arm takes no group filter at all, which is why they ran there and not on the first machine. They were then run deliberately with `--groups external,slow`: `169 staged, pass=169 fail=0`, per-claim verdicts identical to the second machine's. The rule for later batches is that a program whose group is filtered out must be run in its group before the batch counts. **The musl error protocol bit a fourth program**: `t-tcp-close` sets and reads back `SO_LINGER` through the raw `libc::setsockopt`/`getsockopt` door (there is no typed rustix option for it, so `struct linger` is spelled locally from the uapi layout) and both answers needed the same `rc()` at the door that `t-posix`, `t-epoll` and `t-uevent` carry. Closing had to go through `libc::close(IntoRawFd::into_raw_fd(sock))` rather than `rustix::io::close`, which takes an `i32` and returns `()` - the claim is about the door's answer, so the wrapper that cannot report one is not the one to ask. **Two of the three are named-not-made on a host, and for two different reasons.** `t-tcp-close` connects to `10.0.2.2`, which is slirp's address *for its guest*: a real host has no peer there to refuse it, so the host run prints a note and claims nothing rather than reporting the host's own `ENETUNREACH` as if it were the kernel's answer. `t-fill-ramfs` is not run on a host at all - its whole action is to consume a mount until it refuses, and the sandbox's `/tmp` is not a budget this run may drain. That one also carries a distinction the no-std original blurred: `rustix::io::write` returns `Errno` rather than `io::Error`, so a *partial* accept is reported by a separate `short_write` counter instead of being folded into the `NOSPC` claim - the assertion is that the write is refused outright, and a short write would have been a different, quieter answer. `t-dns` sends twelve independent queries with twelve ids to slirp's resolver on twelve sockets, each waited on with a bounded `poll` whose timeout is the resolver's own patience, and counts opened/connected/sent/polled/answered/short/id_ok separately so a run that got fewer answers than queries is described rather than averaged away; a host has no route to `10.0.2.3`, so it is not made there either. **Batch 38 (`t-drm-args`, `t-prime`, `t-dhcp`) is the first batch the aarch64 gap-closing pass *unblocked* rather than merely allowed**: all three need `/dev/dri/card0` or the link layer, and the card was published on the second machine - and the `'d'` family opened there - by the pass before this one, so the DRM family moved at all. Two of the three are machine-internal and ran in both machines' default sweeps; `t-dhcp` went into `external` *before* the first run rather than after it, which is batch 37's lesson applied up front: the door it pins - the `AF_PACKET` socket, its `sockaddr_ll` name, its bind - is the machine's, but the BOOTP answer it waits for is the host's slirp's, so a red one would be a statement about the host. **Two magic numbers left with the move, and both were numbers the no-std version could only have read off the first machine**: `crtc_id = 34` is now asked of `GETRESOURCES` (it happens to be the kernel's fixed `CRTC_ID`, but a test that is *given* its fixture cannot go stale on the machine that serves it), and `sll_ifindex = 2` is now `SIOCGIFINDEX`'s answer for `eth0`. The second one also makes a claim real that the original only appeared to make: a BOOTP server answers the hardware address it was asked about, and the DISCOVER's `chaddr` is now the one `getsockname` reported rather than a constant. Every request record is spelled as `drm.h` lays it out with compile-time offset asserts - `t-fbdev`'s rule - and `drm_prime_handle` is the one worth naming, because the same twelve bytes are the argument to *both* halves of the prime pair with `handle` and `fd` swapping which is read and which is written, so a struct spelled twice could disagree with itself. **All three are named-not-made on a host**, for two reasons rather than one: the card's answers (no gamma table, no vblank, a kernel-mode copy that must fault politely) are this kernel's and a host's own card answers its own same requests differently; and `socket(AF_PACKET)` needs `CAP_NET_RAW`, which this sandbox does not have - with the capability being the lesser reason, since a DISCOVER sent to the link-layer broadcast would put a real DHCP request on the host's own LAN, which is not something a conformance test gets to do to the machine it runs on. Verdicts, per claim identical on both machines: `t-drm-args` 24/24, `t-prime` 23/23 (the 64x64 dumb buffer's pages reached through the dma-buf, through an `SCM_RIGHTS` handoff to a forked child, and back through the handle), `t-dhcp` 15/15 (a 576-byte BOOTREPLY carrying our transaction id and our MAC). Gates: aarch64 `159 staged, pass=159 fail=0`; x86_64 default `159 staged, pass=159 fail=0`; x86_64 with `--groups external,slow` `169 staged, pass=169 fail=0`. `user/abitest`'s integration directory is down to 10. **Batch 39 - `t-drm-lease`, and the family it pins.** The lease family was the one part of the KMS surface the no-std suite could not have caught, and the move found four separate wrongnesses in it, in two kinds. (a) *Values nobody outside the pair held.* The driver matched 0xd3..0xd6 where `<drm.h>` puts the family at 0xc6..0xc9, and the no-std test built its requests from the same four constants - so the expectation and the checked value agreed about a number no caller sends, and the whole family answered `ENOTTY` from the dispatch catch-all while the test stayed green. The std test holds the four against `DRM_IOWR(0xC6..0xC9, ...)` as literals, which is the check that would have caught it. (b) *Shapes taken from the driver instead of the header.* `create_lease` returned `fd` at 16 where the header puts `lessee_id` at 16 and `fd` at 20 - a `libdrm` caller would have been handed a zero descriptor it believed it could flip; `MODE_GET_LEASE` named a descriptor number in the struct instead of being issued on the lease descriptor (`drmModeGetLease(fd)` is given the fd `drmModeCreateLease` returned); `MODE_REVOKE_LEASE` took a descriptor where the header takes the *lessee id*, which is the entire reason the id exists, the lessor being unable to name the fd the lessee holds. **And then the size went wrong a second time while being fixed**, which is the part worth recording: it was written as 28 bytes from a field list ending in a `__u32 pad` this machine's `<drm_mode.h>` does not declare - the struct has five members, so `sizeof` is 24 and the request word is `0xc01864c6`. 28 is not a size that struct can have at any kernel version, since a `__u64` first member rounds six members up to 32 (`0xc02064c6`): a number no alignment can produce is a number invented from a remembered field list, so the four words in the test are literals `cc` prints for the installed header, not `sizeof` applied twice. (c) *Two overruns the move then exposed*, in the family's own two-call shape: `count_objects` and `count_lessees` are one field doing two jobs - it arrives as the array's length and leaves as the total - and both handlers wrote the answer first and read the capacity back afterwards, so the comparison was true by construction and the copy ran at the total's length. Linux reads the capacity, writes the total and copies the smaller; both now do, and the test brings a one-element array to a two-object lease and to a two-lessee card and names the second slot as untouched (`0xdeadbeef` survives). One fixture moved because the second machine made it visible: `KDSETMODE` goes to `/dev/tty1` rather than to fd 0, the harness's console being the serial wire rather than a virtual console (`t-vt`'s measurement). A lessee registry (`LESSEES`, `NEXT_LESSEE`) was added so an id resolves the way Linux's per-master file private does, and the program revokes both leases it makes - a lessee the card is still holding is a global count the next test would inherit. 55 claims, per claim identical on both machines: only the master may mint one (`EACCES` before `SET_MASTER`), the two returned words in the header's order, `DRM_CLOEXEC` on the leased descriptor, an unknown object and an empty lease `EINVAL`, a lease may flip its own CRTC and is refused the object outside it, a lease cannot sub-lease, a revoke by id empties the lease while leaving its descriptor answerable, and a second revoke is `EINVAL`. Host run: one `note:` and zero claims, for `t-drm-args`'s reason - a host's own card answers `MODE_CREATE_LEASE` with its own topology. Gates: aarch64 `160 staged, pass=160 fail=0`; x86_64 `159 staged, pass=159 fail=0`. `user/abitest`'s integration directory is down to 9. **Batch 40 - `t-ahci`, which needed the *machine* before it needed a test, and `t-sh`.** `t-ahci` was the last program still pinned to one machine, and what pinned it was not the driver but the second machine's *absence* of the bus the driver sits on: `drivers::ahci` and `drivers::pci` were both `#[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]`, and un-gating them alone would not have made them run there - the machine boots `acpi=off` (AAVMF then publishes a device tree and no ACPI tables at all), so the MCFG table `discover_ecam` reads does not exist, and a controller's BAR had no address in a port with no window for one. Three pieces closed it. (a) *ECAM from the device tree.* `pci::init` falls back to `discover_ecam_dt`, which reads the `pci-host-ecam-generic` node - `reg` for the window, `bus-range` for the buses it describes - and clamps the bus count to what one MiB per bus can address. Measured on QEMU `virt` (dumped device tree): `pcie@10000000`, `reg = <0x40 0x10000000 0x00 0x10000000>`, so a 256 MiB window at `0x40_1000_0000` and a bus range of 0..0xff - and the boot says so (`pci: the tree names ECAM at 0x4010000000, buses 0..=255, 256 MiB`). (b) *A window for a BAR.* `arch/src/arch/aarch64/mmio.rs` gives the machine a dynamic device window: the boot's own is a fixed 64 MiB at `0xffff_ffff_4000_0000`, a firmware-handed BAR is not inside it, so `window(phys, len)` carves a fresh page-aligned range out of the kernel half above it and `paging::map_mmio` walks it in page by page with device attributes. (c) *The port pair becomes x86_64's arm.* `CONFIG_ADDRESS` and `CONFIG_DATA` had one fixed call site each; they are now one `mod ports` whose non-x86_64 half answers the error response `0xffff_ffff` to every read and drops every write, which is also what lets `init` keep its compare-the-two-mechanisms check on a machine that has two and say plainly that the other has one. The wiring followed: `virt_machine_args` attaches `ich9-ahci` and an `ide-hd` on the *same* image file the first machine uses (`disk::ensure_sata_image`, one shared `test-sata.img`, now that the name and the size live in `disk.rs`), laid by `disk::ensure` and by `sweep_medium` for the reason the pattern image already is - a machine that is supposed to have a SATA disk and quietly does not would fail `t-ahci` for a reason that is not the driver's. The second machine's boot now reads `pci: 2 functions enumerated` and `ahci: sda, 8192 sectors (4 MiB)` (the two functions being the ESP's `virtio-blk-pci` and this controller). 21 claims, per claim identical on both machines (the 21 `ok:` lines byte-identical): the disk is `sda` under major 8 - not `vda` - and `/proc/partitions` gives its size in the column a partitioner reads; the first and last sectors read and one past the end is an *empty read* rather than a lie, which is the failure a wrong `PxCLB`/PRDT setup really has; a sector written with a pattern reads back with those bytes while its neighbour stays zero; a 16 KiB request is one request and every page of it arrives; and a 1 MiB read - `MAX_IO`, the widest this ABI carries - finds the pattern at the offset it was written at, which is what pins a chunking loop that forgot to advance the LBA. Host run: not run, `t-fbdev`'s open rule instead (a hosted run would read and write the *host's* SATA disk, which is not what the pin is about), so its validation is the two sweeps. **`t-sh` moved for a different reason, and changed the sweep to do it.** It is not a check at all: it execs an interactive busybox ash onto the console and prints where an interactive child stops before its first prompt, for a person to read. The no-std original was x86-only by accident of what that harness could see; on the std side both arms *build* it, and the question the move asked was which arms *stage* it - and the answer is neither, because what it prints is an interactive child's state letter, which is not something the kernel produces (§15.34's rule for what a boot may assert). So `sweep::select` grew a `hand_read` filter, keyed on the `diagnostic` group, that leaves such a program out on **both** arms; the other two reason-groups stay x86_64-harness words (`external` names programs that need that machine's QEMU wiring, `slow` its clock), while a diagnostic is the same object everywhere. Its own four claims are what a *hand* run asserts (the tty opened, the fork happened, the child's `/proc` entry read, the child reaped), and it opens the machine's own console node - `ttyS0` on a 16550, `ttyAMA0` on the PL011 - rather than a hardcoded first-machine name. Gates: aarch64 `163 staged, pass=163 fail=0`; x86_64 `161 staged, pass=161 fail=0` (the two counts differ because the two default selections are not the same set of programs: the second machine serves the `external`/`slow` instruments and does not yet build the `no_std` remainder). `user/abitest`'s integration directory is down to 7. **Batch 41 - `t-udev-worker`, whose every claim fits through the crate's own doors.** The program exists because of one line in the desktop image's boot log (`udevd[9]: failed to receive message: Invalid argument`, repeated for every worker return), and what it pins is why that line appeared: eudev's worker channel sends a **zero-byte datagram** - `struct worker_message` has no members, so the credentials *are* the payload - and receives it with an iovec whose length is zero and a control buffer sized for one `struct ucred`. A kernel that answers `EINVAL` for a zero-length data room answers it for every worker return, and the cost is every device tag on the machine. So the first section is that call, and the rest of the family is around it: a receive with no room for the credentials must still take the message and report `MSG_CTRUNC`, a zero-length buffer against a non-empty message must take it and report `MSG_TRUNC` (the message's own length is its boundary), `read(2)` with a zero-length buffer must leave the queue where the receive family consumes, and the credentials' pid must be the *sender's* - the manager looks its worker up by that number, which is why the forked-worker case is in here rather than the same-process one alone. **28 claims, per claim identical on both machines** (the 28 `ok:` lines byte-identical), and the migration found no new kernel wrongness - which is itself the result worth recording: this family had already been pulled on twice (`t-sockmsg` for the message calls on a pair, `t-unix-scm` for the credentials and the descriptor handovers), and what the third pull tested was whether the same shape can be said *through the crate*. It can. Every door here is `rustix`'s - `net::socketpair`, `net::sockopt::set_socket_passcred`, `net::recvmsg` with a `RecvAncillaryBuffer`, `net::sendmsg` with a `SendAncillaryBuffer`, `event::poll`, `io::{read, write}`, `process::waitpid` - with `libc::fork`/`_exit` for the two halves of batch 18's reason. Two claims change their door and neither changes its meaning. (a) The bytes, the flags and the credentials are all reported by the crate (`RecvMsg::bytes`, `RecvMsg::flags`, whose `TRUNC`/`CTRUNC` bits are the kernel's `MSG_*` word, and `RecvAncillaryMessage::ScmCredentials`); the *exact* header arithmetic (`cmsg_len == CMSG_LEN(12)`, the level, the type, `msg_controllen == CMSG_SPACE(12)`) stays `t-unix-scm`'s claim through the raw `libc::recvmsg` door, because a chain whose level, type or length the kernel wrote wrong is not classified as credentials at all - the parse covers what this program needs. The test's own `got_bytes` answers a *failed* receive with its negative errno, so the claim that names `EINVAL` still reports what arrived rather than only that it was not 0. (b) The seqpacket pair's claim says `CLOEXEC|NONBLOCK` **folded into the type**, which the no-std version made on the `socketpair` return alone; the two flags are read back off the descriptor now (`F_GETFD`, `F_GETFL`), so the claim is what its name says. This family has no mpos-only divergence, so the program is **run on the host as well** (`MPOS_ON_HOST=1`) and passes there without a single `note:` - every answer it compares is Linux's, which is the cross-check the guest sweeps cannot be. The gate itself was red when this batch started, and for a reason that was not the batch's: **three `xtask` unit tests could not pass on this machine at all** - `rootfs`'s three chrootless-account cases (added in `d4af4e7`, after the last green gate) reach `create_account_without_chroot`'s `chown(home, uid, gid)` as *root*, and root here is the root of a user namespace whose map holds one id (`/proc/self/uid_map` = `0 1000 1`), so `chown` to any other id answers `EINVAL` on every filesystem: measured, on tmpfs and on the repository's own, with and without the tool's sandbox. `EINVAL` is therefore read as what it says - this machine cannot own that id - and *printed* instead of being fatal, while every other error still fails, which is what `hand_back` already does for a refused hand-over of the build tree; the account's home is still owned by the build user on such a machine, and the build says so. With that, both legs are green: aarch64 `164 staged, pass=164 fail=0`; x86_64 `161 staged, pass=161 fail=0`, the host unit suite 101 passed, the kernel's 29 cases passed, 11 programs left out by `tests.toml`. `user/abitest`'s integration directory is down to 6. **Batch 42 - `t-virtgpu`, and the loader that carried the wrong kernel.** The program is the `DRM_VIRTGPU_*` family, which is how Mesa's virgl driver and every Vulkan-on-virtio-gpu stack reach the device, and it pins four things a table of constants cannot: that `GETPARAM` copies four bytes and leaves the caller's high half alone (Linux's struct spells the field `u64` and the copy is `sizeof(int)`, so a kernel that wrote eight bytes is visible); that `EFAULT` comes *before* the refusal, because Linux's ioctl layer copies the struct in before the handler can decline; that a resource really exists - one `MAP`, `RESOURCE_INFO` and `GEM_CLOSE` all take, and a transfer into it is served across the mailbox; and that this card answers `RESOURCE_CREATE` with a third answer of its own, an *over-read guard* that refuses a transfer whose rectangle does not fit the backing the device was given, where Linux would read past it. **45 claims on the card the gate boots, per claim identical on both machines** (the 45 `ok:` lines byte-identical), plus the virgl column the program carries for a hand-run boot. Both machines' cards negotiated no 3D (`virtio-gpu: virgl=0 context_init=0 capsets=0`), so the non-3D column is the one asked twice - and it is the second machine's first run of this family, which answered the first machine's numbers exactly. The move changed the *door* and nothing else: the program's own `ioctl` through `libc::syscall(SYS_ioctl, ...)` (as `t-drm-args` and `t-drm-lease` do it) and a `rustix` `openat`/`OwnedFd`, with the claim list, its order and its wording left as the original had them. It is not run on the host and says so: every answer is this kernel's virtio-gpu driver's and one of them is this card's own rule, so a hosted run prints a `note:` and makes no claim at all (`t-drm-lease`'s rule for the same reason). **This is also where the loader-carries-the-kernel change (§15.9) cost its first real failure, and the failure is worth more than the batch.** The gate hung: the second machine booted, printed `mpos: /init is up`, and produced nothing at all for fourteen minutes. The cause was not the new test but the new build script's freshness rule - the loader decides it is out of date from the *mtime* of the kernel it is handed, and cargo links an already-compiled artifact into place keeping that artifact's own compile time, so the single target-directory path the two builds alternate at is one whose mtime can stand still while its bytes change. The loader kept the *ordinary* kernel, a self-test boot ran it, and the early userspace then waited for ever on the console line only a self-test kernel queues. Fixed where it belongs - at the build: each caller now hands the loader a path only it writes, mirrored rather than copied so an unchanged kernel does not relink it - because the run-time guard that would have named the mismatch is refused by this repository's own boot gate (`docs/notes/` §15.19: whether a kernel carries tests is the *build's* to say, not a word's and not a run-time check's). A host test pins the cheap half of it. The full record, including what the gate's refusal settles, is §15.9. `user/abitest`'s integration directory is down to 5. **Batch 43 - `t-seat`.** The ioctls a seat manager (`seatd`) drives before weston's DRM backend can take the console and the card: the `'V'`(VT_*) and `'K'`(KD_*) families that own the console, and DRM `SET_MASTER`/`DROP_MASTER`/`AUTH`/`GET_MAGIC`/`AUTH_MAGIC` that own the card. It is a *memory* test more than an accept/reject one - the point of the handover dance is that the kernel keeps what it was told, so `KDGETMODE` reads back the mode `KDSETMODE` set, `VT_GETMODE` reads back the mode and both signals `VT_SETMODE` set, and a refused `VT_SETMODE` leaves nothing half-written. It pins four things a table cannot: that the `_IO` VT/KD requests carry their value in the third ioctl argument rather than behind a pointer (a kernel that checked the null buffer of `SET_MASTER`/`KDSETMODE` as if it were a buffer would refuse what Linux accepts); that `VT_GETSTATE` writes exactly two of `struct vt_stat`'s three shorts and leaves `v_signal` untouched (the sentinel-seeded buffer is the only way "never written" is observable); that `frsig` is the one `struct vt_mode` field Linux copies to zero and forgets; and that `v_state`'s mask agrees with `VT_OPENQRY` - the lowest free console is the highest bit clear, so every console below it is set. **69 claims on the card the gate boots, per claim identical on both machines** (the 69 `ok:` lines byte-identical - the original's 68 plus the one fixture claim the move added), and this is the second machine's first run of the family. **What the move changed is one thing, and it is the fixture the harness forced:** the console. The no-std original ran the whole VT/KD dance on fd 0, the console its runner handed it - but the console the std harness hands a program on fd 0 is the *serial wire*, which is not one of the virtual consoles the KD family is keyed to (`t-vt` measures this, `docs/notes/` §13.16). So the descriptor is now `/dev/tty0` - Linux's own door for "the console I am on now", which both the `'V'` and the `'K'` family resolve to the active virtual console (`vc_number` maps `(4, 0)` to `term::vc::active()`) - and its open is the one claim this move adds. Nothing else about a claim moved; every request word and every struct is the original's, and the door is the same one `t-drm-lease` and `t-virtgpu` took (this program's own `ioctl` through `libc::syscall(SYS_ioctl, ...)`, a `rustix` `OwnedFd` in place of a number closed by hand). **The batch's first gate run was red on both machines, and the failure is worth a line because it is a shape an `OwnedFd` makes easy to write.** Every VT/KD ioctl answered `EBADF` while `/dev/tty0 opens` passed: the open helper returned `as_raw_fd()` and let its `OwnedFd` fall out of scope, so the descriptor was closed the moment the helper returned and the number it handed back named nothing. The DRM block was already correct because its `if let Ok(card) = ...` keeps the `OwnedFd` alive for the block - the fix is to return the `OwnedFd` itself and bind it in `main`. A host run makes no claim and says so: `/dev/tty0` and `/dev/dri/card0` on a host are different devices, so a hosted run measures the host rather than this kernel. `user/abitest`'s integration directory is down to 4 (`t-drm`, `t-drm-props`, `t-drm-sync`, `t-evdev`). **Batch 44 - `t-evdev`.** The capability picture libinput reads before it touches events: opening an evdev node and asking what it *is* - `EVIOCGVERSION`, the type bitmap of `EVIOCGBIT(0)`, the per-type code bitmap of `EVIOCGBIT(EV_KEY)`, the `EVIOCGID`, and the `EVIOCGRAB` seat claim - plus the read rule (while one description holds the grab a reader that is not it is told nothing, and two readers of one node each keep their own copy the device produced) and the seat's `EVIOCREVOKE`. Nothing presses a keyboard during a sweep, so the events come from a device the program makes for itself through `/dev/uinput` - the real interface, not a hook. **86 claims, per claim identical on both machines** (the 86 `ok:` lines byte-identical, and with the fixture below the whole interleaved log is too), and this is the second machine's first run of the family. It pins what a table of constants cannot: `EVIOCGPHYS`/`EVIOCGUNIQ` answer `ENOENT` (not `ENOTTY`), because libevdev tolerates a failure there *only* for `ENOENT` and treats anything else as fatal - libinput would give up on every node on the machine; `EVIOCGKEY` returns the bitmap size it wrote (96, or the buffer's own length when shorter) rather than a bare 0 with nothing written; `EVIOCREVOKE` demands a NULL argument, is one-way, and leaves every other operation - reads included - answering `ENODEV`; and the `/sys/dev/char/M:m` link must resolve to the *same* device-tree path `/sys/class/input/event0` does, because `libudev` resolves one hop and libinput refuses a node whose two derivations disagree. **The one fixture the second machine forced is which node is the keyboard.** A boot gives the machine two virtio input nodes - a keyboard and a tablet - and which of `event0`/`event1` each lands at differs by machine: the first machine enumerates the keyboard first, the second the tablet first (virtio-PCI on one, virtio-MMIO on the other, and the order is the bus's, not this kernel's). The original opened `event0` and called it the keyboard, which is true on the first machine only - and its `EVIOCGABS ... is EINVAL` claim is exactly the one that would break, since a tablet *has* axis 0. So the keyboard is found by what it is (the node reporting no absolute axes), which is the read the program's own header describes and the same "ask, do not index" the tablet and the synthetic device were already found by; the classifier holds no descriptor, so the ones the test keeps get the same numbers on both machines. Only the fixture moved - every request word, struct and claim is the original's - and the door is the other batches' (this program's own `ioctl` through `libc::syscall`, and rustix for `openat`/`read`/`write`/`fcntl_getfl`/`fcntl_setfl`/`readlink`/`event::poll`). A host run makes no claim and says so: a host's `/dev/input/event*` and `/dev/uinput` are different devices with a different topology. `user/abitest`'s integration directory is down to 3 (`t-drm`, `t-drm-props`, `t-drm-sync`). **Batch 45 - `t-drm`.** A compositor's KMS bring-up, end to end on the card: open `/dev/dri/card0`, `SET_CLIENT_CAP(ATOMIC)`, `GET_CAP(DUMB_BUFFER)`, the two-call `GETRESOURCES`/`GETPLANERESOURCES` enumeration, `CREATE_DUMB`/`MAP_DUMB` and a real `mmap` round-trip, `RESOURCE_INFO`/`TRANSFER_TO_HOST`, `ADDFB`/`SETCRTC`/`PAGE_FLIP` with the flip-completion `drmEvent` read back off the fd, then the GL client's bracket - `GETFB`, the fourcc `ADDFB2`/`GETFB2`, the connector's and the plane's `OBJ_GETPROPERTIES`, `GETPROPERTY` by name, the `VERSION`/`SET_VERSION` handshake, `GET_MAGIC`/`AUTH_MAGIC`, `GEM_CLOSE` - the cursor plane (`MODE_CURSOR`/`MODE_CURSOR2`), the render node making and mapping its own dumb buffer, and the `/sys/dev/char/226:{0,128}/device/drm` identity walk niri stopped on. **122 claims, per claim identical on both machines** (the 122 `ok:` lines byte-identical), and this is the second machine's first run of the family - the original 121 plus the one claim the move adds, `DROP_MASTER releases the card`. The door is the other batches' (this program's own `ioctl` through `libc::syscall`, rustix `OwnedFd`s for the card and the render node, and `std::fs` for the namespace walk); every record is spelled as `drm.h`/`drm_mode.h` lay it out, with the sizes as `size_of` and the offsets as compile-time asserts. **The move found three things the no-std original could not tell was wrong.** (1) Two records were handed the kernel **four bytes short** of what its own `arg_shape` copies: `drm_mode_obj_get_properties` is `sizeof` 32 - the `__u64` pointers round the two `__u32`s up - and it was passed 28, and `drm_mode_fb_cmd2` is 104 with its first `ADDFB2` passed 100. Both worked only because a stack buffer reads four bytes into its neighbour without faulting. (2) The connector's property list is copied **all or nothing** - Linux's and this card's rule is `want >= count` (`obj_get_properties`), and the original left `count_props` at the total the sizing call answered while handing **two-element** arrays, so the copy ran the total's length into the caller's two slots and the two claims read their answers out of an overflowed stack frame; the std side sizes the arrays like the plane's list does. (3) `SETCRTC`'s struct has no `width`/`height` - the original wrote 64 into offsets 28 and 32 believing there were - the `#[repr(C)]` record makes that field a `gamma_size` and a `mode_valid`, so the mistake cannot be spelled. The first gate was red on **both** machines and it was not three bad programs: `t-drm` left the card's master and its `DRM_CLIENT_CAP_ATOMIC` behind when it returned, which broke `t-drm-args` (`MODE_ATOMIC` answering `EACCES` instead of `EFAULT`) and `t-drm-lease` ("a non-master cannot create a lease" answering success) - the reason `Report::finish` is `exit_group` and `master_closed` only runs on `close(2)`. That is a kernel gap, not a test one, and it is recorded whole as §15.12 with its fix (a `drivers::drm::close_at_exit` hook in `sched::exit`'s list). `user/abitest`'s integration directory is down to 2 (`t-drm-props`, `t-drm-sync`). **Batch 46 - `t-drm-props`.** The connector and plane properties a compositor reads *before* it decides it can drive this card, and the two blobs they point at: the four this card's hardware can back (`EDID`, `non-desktop`, `max bpc`, `vrr_capable` on the connector, `IN_FORMATS` on the plane) with the shapes Linux *measures* rather than the ones the sources describe - `non-desktop` and `vrr_capable` are immutable **ranges** of 0 and 1, not bools - the `DRM_CLIENT_CAP_ATOMIC` gate on what a property **list** carries while `GETPROPERTY` keeps answering by id, the EDID's own promises (one 128-byte 1.4 block, checksum zero, and the connector's millimetres being its centimetre fields times ten), the write/read-back round trips (`pixel blend mode`, `DPMS`, `ACTIVE`, `MODE_ID`), the cursor plane's rectangle and its steering state, the four object-typed properties carrying their one value each, and the `DRM_CAP_ADDFB2_MODIFIERS` promise in both directions. **98 claims, per claim identical on both machines** (the 98 `ok:` lines byte-identical), and this is the second machine's first run of the family. The door is the other batches' and every record is spelled as `drm.h`/`drm_mode.h` lay it out, sizes as `size_of`, offsets as compile-time asserts off a `cc` probe against the installed uapi headers. **The move found two things the no-std original could not tell were wrong.** (1) `struct drm_mode_create_blob` is `sizeof` 16 and `CREATE_BLOB` was called with 24 in its request word. (2) `drm_mode_fb_cmd2`'s `modifier[4]` is at offset **72**, not 68: `offsets[4]` ends at 68 and a `__u64` cannot begin there, so 68..72 is the uapi's padding. **The kernel read and wrote the modifier at 68 too** (`add_fb2` and `get_fb2`), and the no-std test named its offsets by hand in the same wrong place, so the two agreed with each other and with nothing else - the shape of mistake this migration exists to separate. It survived every prior gate because the only modifier anyone had ever named was `LINEAR`, which is 0, and reading the padding answers 0 either way. Fixed on the kernel side, and pinned here by naming a modifier that is **not** zero in the half that a four-bytes-early read would see: `FORMAT_MOD_BOGUS` is `0x0100_0000_0000_0000` - vendor 0x01's namespace with the value 0, whose low 32 bits are zero - so the offset, and not the value, decides the answer. Both directions are measured: with the kernel back at 68 the claim fails `got 0, want -22`, and at 72 it passes. With the original's `0x...0001` the refusal comes out the same either way and the bug stays invisible, which is why the constant had to change and not just the offset. `user/abitest`'s integration directory is down to 1 (`t-drm-sync`). **Batch 47 (`t-drm-sync`) closed the suite, and the `no_std` crate went with it.** It was the last program, kept alive in `user/abitest/src/bin/integration/` after every other family had moved, and it is the DRM sync-object family: `SYNCOBJ_EVENTFD` (what a compositor probes first), an object with no fence behind it refused rather than read as signaled, a timeline an object *becomes*, and the one blocking wait - `WAIT_FOR_SUBMIT` - proven to be woken by *another* task, which is why the test forks. The move changed the door and no claim: `rustix::event::eventfd`, `rustix::event::poll` and `rustix::process::waitpid` name three of them, and two are raw and say so - `setitimer(ITIMER_REAL)` has no `libc` binding on a Linux target, so it is `libc::syscall(SYS_setitimer, ...)`, and `libc::fork` is batch 18's reason (`rustix` binds no `fork`). Green on both machines, `user/abitest` is deleted whole: `git rm -r`, the workspace `members`, `build_user_tests`'s second build-and-collect leg, and the four comments that still named `lib.rs` as a `#[path]`-included source (`src/syscall/linux/nr.rs`, `user/tests/src/lib.rs`, `user/tests/Cargo.toml`, `user/Cargo.toml`). The sweep now builds one user-test target where it built two, and `nr.rs` is the kernel's table again rather than a file the harness also pasted in - the two could agree and both be wrong, which is the direction the std side's `libc::SYS_*` already guards. |
| 15.12 | **each configuration file describes both machines, in three sections.** `machine.toml` carried the second machine's values as `aarch64_`-prefixed keys beside the shared ones (`config.rs::take_for`), and `rootfs.toml`/`initramfs.toml` split the two machines across *files* (`rootfs-aarch64.toml`, `initramfs-aarch64.toml`). Each of the three is now one file with `[default]` (the baseline both machines read) and `[x86_64]`/`[aarch64]` (each machine's own half, overriding it), and keys are dotted by what they belong to (`qemu.memory`, `rootfs.arch`, `system.hostname`, `user.name`). The prefix helpers are gone: `config.rs::parse_sectioned_toml` overlays the baseline with that machine's section, and a key written before any header - or under a header that is not one of the three - is **refused** rather than skipped, because a `[qemu]`-style leftover of the flat format would drop its keys in silence and boot a machine on defaults. `RootfsConfig::load`/`InitramfsConfig::load` take the `Arch` now and every caller names `rootfs.toml`/`initramfs.toml` (the four in `aarch64.rs`, the Alpine stamp input included). **The effective values did not move**, which is the whole claim, and two had to be written *explicitly* into the second machine's half because an absent key would inherit the first machine's: `rootfs.base_packages = []` (e2fsprogs/dosfstools belong to the guest that makes filesystems) and `user.name = ""` (the desktop account is the first machine's; the second logs in as root). **The one cost, named rather than hidden**: both machines' stamps now compare against the one `rootfs.toml`, so an edit to either half re-installs both trees - the trade that split the files in the first place | x86_64 `user tests: 169 programs built` / `159 staged, 10 left out` / `=== SUMMARY pass=159 fail=0`, `X86_EXIT=0`; aarch64 `--arch aarch64` `44 aarch64 programs built` / `42 staged, 2 left out` / `=== SUMMARY pass=42 fail=0`, `AARCH64_EXIT=0`; `boot-aarch64` `EXIT=0` through `INIT_EXITED`; `alpine-aarch64` `EXIT=0` on `mpos login:` (34 packages, 16.1 MiB); `cargo xtask disk` reads the first machine's half and reports the existing 2G image; `cargo test -p mpos-xtask` **83 passed**, two of them new - the parser's two refusals, and `the_shipped_files_answer_each_machine_its_own_values` (memory 2048/256, `display` absent vs `none`, `extra_args` `["-vga","none"]` vs `[]`, arch, serial port, root device, base and extra packages, `user.name`); host tests 514; `depgraph` within the ratchet; `fmt --check` clean | **medium** - the parser's semantics changed, so the only real proof is that every booted machine answers exactly what it answered before |
| 15.13 | **the resource-limit table was indexed by this kernel's own numbering, and the migration that reads its numbers from a maintained table is what found it.** `getrlimit`/`setrlimit`/`prlimit64` speak `asm-generic/resource.h`'s `RLIMIT_*` numbers, and `crates/abi`'s `default_rlimits` held the descriptor ceiling on row `5` (Linux's `RLIMIT_RSS`) and the process count on row `7` (Linux's `RLIMIT_NOFILE`) - so a caller asking for `RLIMIT_NOFILE` read the process-count budget, `RLIMIT_RSS` reported the descriptor ceiling, and `setrlimit(RLIMIT_NOFILE)` wrote a row nothing enforced. `/proc/<pid>/limits` carried the same slip: fourteen rows where Linux has sixteen, shifted by it, so `ulimit` read the wrong names too. It was invisible because the one program that touched it (`t-prlimit64`) had the same number written into it (`lin.rs`: `RLIMIT_NOFILE = 5`) - which is exactly the failure mode S6 exists to remove, a test whose constant comes from the kernel it is checking and so cannot disagree with it. Measured against this host's own `/proc/self/limits` (Linux's sixteen rows, in `RLIMIT` order) **before** the change; the fix moves the row, the `resource == 7` fd-ceiling sync in `src/sched/runtime.rs`, and the procfs label table | `cargo test -p mpos --lib --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` 514; `cargo test -p mpos-abi --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` incl. `default_rlimits_budgets_stack_nofile_and_nproc`, which now also asserts row `5` is unbudgeted - the assertion that keeps the two rows from swapping back; `cargo test -p mpos-xtask --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` 83; `cargo xtask test` `pass=159 fail=0`; `--arch aarch64` `pass=51 fail=0`; `boot-aarch64` `EXIT=0`; `alpine-aarch64` `EXIT=0` on `mpos login:`; `depgraph` within the ratchet; `fmt --check` clean | **one row in three files** - and the shape S6 is for: the migration did not find a missing call, it found a *number* this repository had written down from its own table and then written a test against |
| 15.14 | **`setpriority` clamps an out-of-range nice; this kernel refused it.** `setpriority(which, who, prio)`'s nice is clamped to `-20..=19` by Linux (`if (niceval < MIN_NICE) niceval = MIN_NICE;`, `kernel/sys.c`), and the man page lists `EINVAL` under ERRORS only for a `which` that is none of `PRIO_PROCESS`/`PRIO_PGRP`/`PRIO_USER`. Measured on a current host: `setpriority(PRIO_PROCESS, 0, 20)` answers `0` and leaves the task at `19`, and `-21` answers `EPERM` - a *privilege* refusal for the raise the clamp turns it into, which a range check running first would have answered `EINVAL` before any privilege was consulted. This handler answered `EINVAL` for both, so a caller that asked for a nice outside the range was told the request was malformed when Linux would have taken it and picked the nearest value. The asymmetry with the modern door is Linux's and is now pinned too: `sched_setattr` *does* refuse an out-of-range `sched_nice` (`EINVAL`), so the two doors onto one nice slot disagree, and `t-priority` says so. Two programs pinned the old refusal - the no-std `t-priority` (migrated in the same commit) and `t-posix` - which is why this is one commit and not two | `cargo test -p mpos --lib --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` 514; `cargo xtask test` `pass=159 fail=0`; `--arch aarch64` `pass=54 fail=0`; `boot-aarch64` `EXIT=0`; `alpine-aarch64` `EXIT=0` on `mpos login:`; `depgraph` within the ratchet; `fmt --check` clean | **one range check** - found by reading the man page's ERRORS list rather than its DESCRIPTION, and confirmed by measuring the host: the probe is `setpriority(PROCESS, 0, 20)` answering `0` where this kernel answered `-EINVAL` |
| 15.15 | **`F_SETFD` masks its word to `FD_CLOEXEC`; this kernel answered `EINVAL` for anything else.** POSIX says `F_SETFD` sets the descriptor's flags, and Linux's `set_close_on_exec` reads `arg & FD_CLOEXEC` - so a word with other bits in it is *taken*, and the one bit it names is what the descriptor keeps. The no-std `t-fcntl` pinned the refusal ("F_SETFD with bits beyond FD_CLOEXEC answers EINVAL"), which is a claim about this kernel rather than about the ABI - and the migration's rule is that a claim's answer comes from a maintained table, with the host measured when the two disagree. Measured **through the raw syscall**, so that no libc's masking can be mistaken for the kernel's: `fcntl(1, F_SETFD, 0xFFFFFFFF)` answers `0` and `fcntl(1, F_GETFD)` then reads `1`, while `F_SETFD(1, 0xFFFFFFFE)` answers `0` and clears it. This is the shape `AGENTS.md` §3 warns about, a caller that builds the word from a variable taking `EINVAL` and its fallback path where Linux succeeded. Owner: `src/syscall/linux/fs/file.rs`'s `F_SETFD` arm, which now masks. The migrated claim is rewritten to Linux's answers and made *stronger* than the original: its second half reads the stored word back, so the three possible implementations are told apart - refused (`EINVAL`), stored whole (`0xFFFF_FFFF`), and masked to `1`. | `cargo xtask test` `pass=159 fail=0`; `--arch aarch64` `pass=57 fail=0`, with `t-fcntl`'s ten `ok:` lines identical on both machines; both bare-metal `cargo check`s clean; host 514; `fmt --check` clean | **one mask** - the third kernel bug the migration has found by asking where a claim's answer came from, and the second found in a batch's own subject rather than in a maintained table |
| 15.16 | **Two divergences this batch's pins surfaced, both closed on 2026-10-16, each with its measurement so neither is rediscovered as a shape.** (a) **`F_GETFL`: the access mode is half the word, and this row's own claim about it was wrong.** The row said Linux ORs the descriptor's mode into the word `F_GETFL` returns (measured: an `O_WRONLY` descriptor answers `1`) while this kernel reported a stored status word "whose access bits are always zero", so a caller was told `O_RDONLY` for every descriptor. The second half of that was false, and had been for four commits: since `b659a62` the open has stored `flags & OPEN_STATUS_FLAGS`, a mask that carries `0o3`, so a *path* open has reported its mode all along - and `t-fcntl`'s claim, stated on the inherited stdout, could not have caught it either way (which is this class of claim's own lesson: the shape the row was looking at was not the half that was wrong). What was really wrong is the pair a word-wide store hides, measured through `syscall(2)` on a current host (`.workbuddy/probe_getfl.c`, Linux 7.2.9, so no libc's rewrite is mistaken for the kernel's rule): this kernel's `pipe2` write end answered `0` where Linux answers `1`, `inotify_init1`'s queue answered `2` where Linux answers `0`, and `F_SETFL(O_NONBLOCK)` **cleared** the mode - Linux rewrites `filp->f_flags` as `(arg & SETFL_MASK) \| (old & ~SETFL_MASK)`, and `SETFL_MASK` has no member for the access mode, so the host's `O_WRONLY` descriptor answers `0x8801` and one asked for `O_RDWR` still answers `0x8001` - which made the word a caller reads to decide whether it may read or write a lie about the direction the kernel still enforced. **Fixed 2026-10-16** (docs/uapi/ §5.1 item 21), and the fix is one owner for the fact rather than two spellings of it: the mode lives in `src/vfs/file.rs`'s `access` field alone - the field the read/write `EBADF` refusal already asked - `File::open_flags` composes the reported word from it and the status flags, `File::set_status` keeps only the mask - which lost the `0o3` that made it a second copy of the mode, and is named `SETFL_MASK` now that it is Linux's own word for exactly this - `pipe2` records `ACC_RDONLY`/`ACC_WRONLY` on its two ends and `handle_inotify_init1` records `ACC_RDONLY`, which is also what makes `write` on a pipe's read end and `read` on its write end `EBADF`, Linux's answer - and it moved two more errnos with it, both toward Linux and both measured: a pipe's read end now answers `EINVAL` to `ftruncate` and `EBADF` to `fallocate`, because the mode refuses before the file type is asked (that kernel's `!(f_mode & FMODE_WRITE)`). The other two answers - the ones the pipe's *write* end owes, where the mode permits the write and the node is what answers - followed the same day as §5.1 item 22: the kind test cannot see a pipe here (its inode reports `NodeKind::File`, the vocabulary's only word for an unnamed channel), so `ftruncate` fell through to the VFS default's `EOPNOTSUPP` where Linux answers `EINVAL`, and `fallocate` landed in the `ENODEV` arm where Linux answers `ESPIPE` for a FIFO. Both handlers now ask `as_pipe` beside the kind, and nothing else in the zoo moved: the probe behind it (`.workbuddy/probe_fsize.c`) asked both calls of a socket, a `socketpair` end, an `eventfd`, a `timerfd`, a `signalfd`, an `inotify` queue, an `epoll`, a `memfd`, `/dev/null`, `/dev/zero` and a directory, and the kind test was already right for every one.
