The same item took the *third* spelling of the question with it, because the probe measured it beside the other two: `truncate(path)` had no kind test at all, so a directory and a character device reached the node's default `EOPNOTSUPP` where Linux answers `EISDIR` and `EINVAL` - and the two spellings' answers are *not* the same pair, which is the sort of thing only a measurement says (`ftruncate` on a directory is `EINVAL`, the path spelling `EISDIR`, because the descriptor spelling's write-mode check answers first). `src/vfs/ext4/`'s inode had already spelled Linux's pair for its own tree; the handler now spells it for every filesystem. What is left is a *fourth* door, and it was measured wrong when it was written down here: **closed 2026-10-16** (§15.40, `docs/uapi/` §5.1 item 24). The sentence said a pipe reached by its own `/proc/<pid>/fd` name was a case where "this kernel's fd links name an anonymous descriptor by its display string, which is not a path at all" - and reading the answer to that call off the code said `ENOENT`, where the probe says `EINVAL`: the door was not failing to resolve the name, it was resolving it to the *link* and acting on that, which is one door of a family. Every following door had the same shape, the walk now denotes the file the descriptor holds, and what the name leaves behind is named in `docs/uapi/` §5.2 (five items: the foreign descriptor, `st_dev`, `..`, the link's own metadata, and `getcwd`) with this probe's numbers. And the one inode the handler *cannot* tell apart, which was named there until 2026-10-16: a FIFO made by `mknod`, which `abi.rs`'s `mknod_kind` mapped to `NodeKind::File` - a pipe to Linux (measured: `truncate` `EINVAL`, and an empty read end answers `0`) and a resizable file here. It is closed by refusing the creation until a channel node exists (§15.41, `docs/uapi/` §5.1 item 25), so no node this kernel can make reports `File` without being one. `t-fcntl`'s claims moved onto descriptors the program *makes* - a file opened each way, a `pipe2` pair with and without `O_NONBLOCK`, a `socketpair` end - so the section now holds under `MPOS_ON_HOST=1` as well as in a sweep (22 claims, green on this host first), and the host unit test `vfs::file::tests::the_reported_word_keeps_the_access_mode_setfl_cannot_change` pins the mask directly. The blast radius this row worried about did not arrive: `rustix::fs::Dir`'s `openat(fd, ".")` is handed an `O_RDONLY` dirfd's word, whose extra bits are as inert here as on Linux. What Linux's word carries and this one does not is now a *measured* list in §5.2 - `O_LARGEFILE` (which that kernel adds to every path open, and machine-dependently: `0o100000` on x86_64, `0o20000` on arm64, neither defined here), `O_DIRECTORY`, `O_NOFOLLOW` (`0x28000`, so the bit is **not** open-only - `src/syscall/linux/abi.rs`'s comment about it is corrected by that measurement), `O_PATH`, `O_SYNC`/`O_DSYNC`, `O_ASYNC`, `O_DIRECT` and `O_NOATIME`. **Gates** (2026-10-16, both landings' trees): x86_64 `187 programs built` / `176 staged, 11 left out by tests.toml` / `pass=176 fail=0` + `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`; aarch64 `175 staged, 12 left out` / `pass=175 fail=0` + the same 32 cases - the program counts do not move for either landing, since both add claims to programs that were already staged. Host unit tests 545; xtask 121; `fmt --check` clean; clippy 0 error on `x86_64-unknown-none`, `aarch64-unknown-none`, `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu --lib` and the `aarch64-unknown-uefi` boot crate; `depgraph` within the ratchet. The affected programs' per-claim `ok:` lines are **byte-identical on both machines** - `t-fcntl` 22 (item 21), `t-truncate` 31 and `t-fallocate` 21 (item 22, whose pipe claims describe both ends now that the read end is not a per-machine answer) - and every one of them ran against real Linux first, where item 21's whole `F_GETFL` section and item 22's four pipe claims are Linux's own answers. (b) **A `flock` dies on *any* close of its description, not the last.** Linux releases the description's `flock` when the last descriptor referring to it closes; measured on this host, `flock(a, LOCK_EX)`, `close(dup_of_a)`, then a fresh `open()` of the file still answers `EWOULDBLOCK`, where this kernel has already released the lock - the close path passes the description's identity and nothing about how many descriptors still hold it. A process that dups its lock descriptor and closes the dup therefore silently loses its lock, and a second process can take it, which is what `apk`'s database lock exists to prevent. `src/vfs/locks.rs`'s own `release_on_close` record already stated the Linux rule, so the code and its documentation disagreed; the record was corrected here to say which of the two the code was. **Fixed 2026-10-16.** The close path splits the release the way Linux splits it: `drop_on_close` takes the process's POSIX record locks on any close (POSIX's own rule, unchanged), and `drop_with_description` takes the description's `flock` and `F_OFD_*` lock when the *last* descriptor referring to the description goes - asked from the description's reference count after the descriptor is removed, the test this path's evdev read position and DRM master already used. Both halves are pure functions over the lock table, host-tested; `t-filelock` grew two claims that tell the readings apart (each class held through a description whose `dup` is then closed, and a *fresh* `open()` must still find it in the way), measured on a current host first and byte-identical on both machines (26 `ok:` lines). The counterfactual is exact: with the release moved back to any close, `t-filelock` is the sweep's one red and the two new claims are the two that fail. | (a) `t-fcntl` on both machines, plus `t-truncate` and `t-fallocate`, whose pipe claims moved with it; (b) `t-filelock` on both machines, whose twenty-six `ok:` lines are identical - the two that tell the two readings apart are the new ones | **small each, and each needs its own pin** - (a) landed and left docs/uapi/ §5.2 with the rest of Linux's `F_GETFL` word named there, and the two errnos a pipe's write end still owed it landed the same day as §5.1 item 22; (b) landed and left that list |
| 15.17 | **An absolute path stopped the `*at` family asking about its dirfd here; on Linux it never asks.** Linux's rule is that the dirfd is not used at all when the path is absolute, and it is the *dirfd* that stops being a question rather than the path: measured on a current host, `newfstatat(77, "/tmp", buf, 0)` with `77` not open answers `0` with errno `0` - while the same call with a *relative* name answers `EBADF`. This kernel resolved the dirfd first whatever the path looked like (one `base_of` did it, and nineteen call sites went through it), so the absolute form answered `EBADF`, and it was the descriptor's *kind* as much as its state that stopped being a question: a dirfd open on a regular file serves an absolute name too, where a relative name through that same descriptor is `ENOTDIR` - all of it measured by `.workbuddy/probe_at_dirfd.c` on Linux 7.2.9, which calls every member through `syscall(2)` so that no libc's `/proc/self/fd` rewrite is mistaken for the kernel's rule. The no-std `t-newfstatat`'s bad-dirfd claim had an **absolute** path in it - `/etc/motd` - and so pinned this divergence as though it were the ABI, which is the class of claim batches 10, 11 and 12's §15.15 each turned up one of. The migrated claim names a relative path, which is `EBADF` on both machines and is what the claim is actually about, and the row is the fix now rather than the divergence's name: **fixed on 2026-10-16**, and the fix is the *order* rather than a case per call. The name is read before the descriptor is, and `base_of` became `base_for`, which takes the name as its second argument and answers the caller's cwd when it starts with `/` - the resolver ignores its start for an absolute name anyway, so that is the whole of what an absolute name needs. That one reorder carries the other half of Linux's order with it, because it is *why* the order is what it is: the name is copied in `getname()` before `path_init()` reads its first character, so a name pointer the kernel cannot read at all - a bad one, or a null one - answers `EFAULT` where this kernel answered the descriptor's `EBADF` (measured for every member). `linkat` asks its old name, its descriptor and its walk *before it reads the new name at all*, and `renameat`/`renameat2` read both *pointers* before either descriptor; both shapes are Linux's (`filename_lookup` of the old name before `getname` of the new one in the first, `getname` twice before the two walks in the second) and both were measured, the four combinations each: a missing old name is `ENOENT` through `linkat` with any new-side answer at all - a bad new dirfd, which alone would be `EBADF`, and an unreadable new pointer, which alone would be `EFAULT` - while through `renameat` it is the new dirfd's `EBADF`, and an unreadable pointer is `EFAULT` on either side of either call. Two members (`readlinkat`, `symlinkat`) had their tail split out so that the name is available before the descriptor at all. **And the flag that reinterprets the empty name closed with it the same day**: `AT_EMPTY_PATH` names the descriptor, six doors take it, and two of them were wrong here - `utimensat` served the descriptor only for the null-name spelling `futimens` gives, and `faccessat2` refused the flag outright (`EINVAL`) where Linux has taken it since 5.8. Both are served now, and the measurement around them is what said so: all six answer `0` for a descriptor the caller owns (with `fchownat`/`fchmodat2` setting the uid and mode already there, so the answer does not depend on who is running) and `EBADF` for one nothing is open on, while beside a name that is really there the flag is inert and the name answers. Nothing of this row stays named: the family's name questions, the empty name, and the flag that reinterprets it are all pinned in `t-at-empty-name`. **And the empty name followed the absolute one on 2026-10-16**, because it is the same rule - a question the *name* answers - and so it lives in the same place: the refusal is in `base_for`, and every door inherits it from the one helper they all come through rather than from a check of its own. Measured across all nineteen doors for that change too: an empty name answers `ENOENT` through a dirfd nothing is open on, through one open on a regular *file* and through one open on a directory, where the relative name beside it is `EBADF`, `ENOTDIR` and the walk's `ENOENT`. `readlinkat` is the one door that must not come through `base_for` with an empty name, and Linux is why: it consults its descriptor first (`EBADF`) and only then refuses the empty name (`ENOENT`, through a regular-file dirfd as well, so its kind is not asked either), which the handler now says out loud. Not a §3.2 defect - an honest refusal rather than a wrong number throughout - but a caller holding a stale dirfd and an absolute path took its fallback where Linux succeeded. | `t-newfstatat` on **both** machines (the served pair, the relative `EBADF` kept beside it, and the `EFAULT` order through a raw `libc::syscall` - `rustix` cannot spell a bad path pointer), `t-openat` (the same pair for the family's busiest member) and `t-link` (the old-name-before-the-new-dirfd pair, whose two claims tell "asked second" from "never asked") and `t-at-empty-name` (the empty name door by door, and the `AT_EMPTY_PATH` flag that reinterprets it - 55 claims over the three descriptor shapes each, the six flagged doors, and `readlinkat`'s exception); docs/uapi/ §5.1 items 18-20 | **nineteen call sites, one helper** - found by running the migrated claim against real Linux before pinning it, which is the whole reason that step is in the recipe, and the measurement took two passes to be about the right thing: the probe has to call each member through `syscall(2)`, because glibc rewrites `fchmodat`/`faccessat` into a `/proc/self/fd/<n>/<name>` open whenever the dirfd is not `AT_FDCWD` (the wrapper's answer, not the kernel's), and the probe's own setup was wrong in the same way twice - an `openat` in it handed back the very number it was using as its "closed" descriptor, so every later "closed dirfd" line of that run was really a live regular file and read `ENOTDIR` instead of `EBADF`, and a section that renamed the fixture aside left the next section's "existing name" missing, turning `linkat`'s `EFAULT` into an `ENOENT` that read as a real difference between the two calls until an immovable second fixture was added - and the family's own test was red on every one of its 41 claims in its first run, because `libc::syscall` answers `-1` with `errno` set rather than the kernel's negated number, so the harness read the wrong word of a call that was right all along. A probe's setup is part of its measurement, and so is a test's |
| 15.18 | **The capability model's gates are wired to the syscalls that own them.** `crates/cred` has stored the four sets, the dumpable flag and `KEEPCAPS` since it landed, and `capget`/`capset`/`prctl`'s credential options round-trip it - but `has_effective` had exactly one consumer (`FS_IOC_SETFLAGS`' immutable gate), which is why the migration batches kept meeting a shape they had to name as "no privilege check": `klogctl`'s gate on the host sits *before* the type (batch 29's unknown-type `EPERM`), `vhangup`'s before the no-terminal `ENOTTY` (batch 32), and `unshare`'s was an `euid == 0` stand-in. Five gates are now real, each asked of the caller's **effective set** through `sched::current_credentials()` and each in Linux's measured position: `syslog` *before* the type (so an unknown type from a capability-less caller answers `EPERM`, as the host's does), `vhangup` first, `chroot` before the path, `reboot` after the magic words and before the command (`CAP_SYS_BOOT`, the CAD policy included), and `unshare`'s privileged test is now `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` in the effective set rather than an identity word - the coarse check was already honest about *placement*, and only asked the wrong question. A root caller holds every capability in its effective set, so **no guest answer moved**: the whole sweep re-ran green unchanged, which is the design property - the model exists for the day there are two users, and until then it is invisible. The day is simulated by `t-caps` (new, both machines, 19 `ok:` lines identical): one identity dance - `setresuid(-1, 1, -1)` clears the effective set, `capget` reads the emptied effective word against the still-full permitted word (the saved id is root's), and the five gates answer `EPERM` while lowered, including `klogctl(99)` whose `EPERM`-before-`EINVAL` is the ordering claim in one line - then the raise-back through the saved id restores the effective set from permitted and every gate opens again, `CLONE_NEWUTS` splitting once more. `unshare(CLONE_NEWNS)` kept `EOPNOTSUPP` the whole time *at the time of this entry* - the refuse-rather-than-approximate answer no capability buys. **That row moved when MNT became a served kind** (§6.3's step 1): `CLONE_NEWNS` is now granted to a caller holding the capability and refused `EPERM` without it, exactly like `CLONE_NEWUTS`, so the "refused, not gated" claim belongs to a kind still unserved (`CLONE_NEWTIME` in `t-posix`, `CLONE_NEWPID` in `t-caps` once the capability is back - and the first of those is gone, since TIME was built on 2026-10-16 and `t-posix` pins the served answer now). The ordering the entry measured is the part that stands: privilege is asked *first*, so a lowered caller hears `EPERM` for **any** namespace word - Linux's own order. The host run names its own limit: the host kernel answers `EINVAL` to the lowering itself (§15.11 batch 32's probe), so the dance cannot be staged there and the gated claims are mint-not-made on a host run | `cargo xtask test` `pass=160 fail=0`; `--arch aarch64` `pass=136 fail=0`, with `t-caps`' 19 `ok:` lines identical on both machines; host tests 517; `fmt --check` clean; clippy clean | **five gates, one model already stored** - the work was wiring, not inventing, and `t-caps` is the difference between a model that stores and one that gates |
| 15.19 | **the boot's self-tests become suites in a test build, and the boot path stops branching.** The kernel used to decide whether to test itself by parsing `mpos.selftest` off its own command line, while `src/board/checks/{mm,sched,vfs,ipc}.rs` was compiled into every image: five `if selftest { … }` branches in x86_64's `init` (seven in aarch64's), interleaved with the real bring-up because each family must run at the step it depends on. What the kernel has is a *build* flag, and **a `--cfg` cannot be added here** - measured: `RUSTFLAGS` *replaces* `.cargo/config.toml`'s `[target.*] rustflags` rather than extending them, so the linker script and all nine `mpos_*` capability flags go with it (4 errors in `mpos-arch`) - so the switch is the cargo feature `selftest`, the additive form of the same thing and the shape of the one it imitates (`CONFIG_KUNIT=y`). `src/board/kunit.rs` is the harness: `Suite`s of named `Case`s, each declaring the boot `Phase` it needs; a case answers `Result<(), &'static str>` rather than asserting, because the kernel is `panic = "abort"` and a case that ended the machine would hide every case after it, which is the one thing a test system must not do. The phases are load-bearing rather than decorative: `mm` asserts increments on a *fresh* heap and `vfs` needs the bootstrap root mounted, so running everything at one point before the handoff would mean re-measuring every assertion against a machine that has already been used - a test redesign, not a refactor (`kunit.rs`'s header names it as the alternative not taken). Production compiles neither the families nor the harness, and that is what made the unused-import warnings on `check_arch`'s imports *correct*: the compiler was saying those names left the shipping kernel with the check. `xtask` builds the test kernel and **copies it aside** (`utils::build_test_kernel`), because cargo holds one `mpos` per target and profile and a production build after a test build would otherwise leave the ESP staging whichever binary happened to be there; `mpos.selftest` is gone from the test command line, since a marker the kernel ignores is the shape that reads as a mechanism and is not one. The sweep asserts the harness's own line in both arms - present *and* zero failed - which is what makes "the suites ran" a claim rather than a hope | x86_64 `=== SUMMARY pass=160 fail=0` with `mpos-kunit 6 passed, 0 failed` and exit 33; aarch64 `pass=143 fail=0` with `mpos-kunit 7 passed, 0 failed`; the per-case lines in boot order - `mm.allocators`, `arch.clock_tables_address_spaces`, `sched.tasks`, `vfs.mounts`, `ipc.pipes_tty_signals`, `ipc.boot_stack_depth` on the first machine, plus `arch.console_receive` and `arch.console_transmit` on the second; both bare-metal builds with the same four pre-existing dead-code warnings and no new ones; host `mpos` 517, `mpos-xtask` 89; `cargo xtask depgraph` within the ratchet; clippy 0 errors on `x86_64-unknown-none`, `aarch64-unknown-none` and the host lib | **the mechanism, landed** - the families are one case each for now (each body still answers by panicking), and the `M*` checks' split into bring-up assertions and tests is slice 4 |
| 15.20 | **the suite vocabulary moves into the library, and `mm`'s family into `mm` - slice 2 of the boot self-test refactor, one family at a time.** Two things had to move before a suite could live beside the module it tests. **The vocabulary** (`src/kunit.rs`: `Phase`, `Suite`, `Case`, `case_check!`) went into the library, because both sides need it and the suite modules are the side that cannot reach up; the *harness* stayed in the wiring layer (`src/board/kunit.rs`), where the machine's own suites and the boot's phase markers are, and it simply names other modules' `const`s now - so the registry and the `install()` step this phase's plan had assumed turned out to be unnecessary (the plan's slice 2 was narrowed on landing, and this row is that record). **A case's answer** became `Result<(), String>` rather than `Result<(), &'static str>`: half the checks worth making are about *values* - "text at 0x… has the wrong permissions" is what a reader needs - and a static string would have to drop the address. The failure path allocates, which is the one place this costs anything and the one place it is worth it. `mm`'s family is the first to move, and it is the shape the other three will follow: one case per thing the module promises, each an answer instead of an `assert!`. It became **three** cases rather than one because the family's assertions were never one claim - `frames`, `large_block`, `heap` - and `heap` is deliberately *not* split further, because its agreement check compares the frames consumed *before* the allocations against the heap's mapped bytes *after* them, so split apart it would compare a baseline with itself and pass whatever the kernel did. The incidental panic is *not* converted (an `expect` on a frame the allocator just said it had): that is not a check, and a machine that hits one is broken in a way no later case would have reported | x86_64 `=== SUMMARY pass=160 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 8 passed, 0 failed` (6 -> 8: `mm`'s three cases replace its one), exit 33; aarch64 `pass=143 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 9 passed, 0 failed`; the log's lines in order - `mm.frames`, `mm.large_block`, `mm.heap`, `arch.clock_tables_address_spaces`, `sched.tasks`, `vfs.mounts`, `ipc.pipes_tty_signals`, `ipc.boot_stack_depth`; all four kernel builds (both machines, with and without the feature) with no new warnings; host `mpos` 517, `mpos-xtask` 89; `depgraph` within the ratchet; `fmt --check` clean | **`mm` landed; `sched`, `vfs` and the cross-subsystem half are the next three steps** - and `vfs`'s suite is where `sched::permanent_tasks`' fate is decided, since that accessor's two remaining callers are the `sched` and `vfs` families (the `sched` one can read the private state through its own child module; the `vfs` one cannot) |
| 15.21 | **`sched`'s family moves into `sched`, as seven named cases** - slice 2's second family, and the shape `mm` set: every deliberate check answers a value, so a failure names the scenario instead of stopping the machine at the first one in file order. The five scenarios the old `run()` called in sequence are five cases (`alternating`, `preempted`, `sleeping`, `idling`, `priority_wins`) and **two cases had to be re-derived rather than moved**, which is the part worth keeping: `reaping` was the old tail (`task_count() == permanent_tasks()`) and that claim is *absolute*, so it survives as a case unchanged - while `stacks_returned` compared the frames the five scenarios consumed against the heap's growth over the same span, so as a case of its own it would have compared a baseline with whatever the state happened to be by the end of the last one. It now measures a window it owns (spawn four tasks, wait, look) and asserts the same property there. The task-observed half of `sleeping` needed a door too: a task cannot return to the case that spawned it, so the tick count it measured comes back through a static (`SLEPT_TICKS`) and is asserted *in the case* - asserting it inside the task, as the old body did, is what turns a wrong number into a dead machine instead of a failure with the number in it. The suite uses `sched`'s **public** API rather than its private state, which is the honest reading here (everything it observes has a reader outside the suite), and that is why `permanent_tasks` is *not* deleted by this step: its last two callers are in the `vfs` family, and its fate - most likely `pub(crate)` - is settled there. `drain`/`wait_for_code` are `pub` from this suite because the two families that run after it borrow them | x86_64 `=== SUMMARY pass=160 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 14 passed, 0 failed` (8 -> 14: `sched`'s seven replace its one), exit 33; aarch64 `pass=143 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 15 passed, 0 failed`; the log's lines in order through `sched.stacks_returned` before `vfs.mounts`; all four kernel builds with no new warnings; host `mpos` 517; `depgraph` within the ratchet; `fmt --check` clean | **`mm` and `sched` landed; `vfs` is next and it is the one that decides `permanent_tasks`, then the syscall gate with the tty and the signal path** |
| 15.22 | **`vfs`'s family moves into `vfs`, the gate's cases into `syscall`, and `sched::permanent_tasks` leaves production** - slice 2's third family, and the step where the module ratchet earned its keep. `src/vfs/kunit.rs` answers values like its neighbours, with `ramfs_file` and `mounted_tree_multi_task` (a mount, an identity across it, the double-mount refusal, and two tasks reaching one file through it - one case rather than two, because the second has no fixture without the first, and a case that needs another case to have run first is not a case). **Wholesale movement failed, and the measurement is the interesting part**: putting `crate::syscall::linux_dispatch` inside a suite that lives in `vfs` made the depgraph baseline test report `vfs names crate::syscall:: 1 times, up from the recorded 0` and `the module graph has 9 mutual pairs, up from the recorded 8`. The answer is not to raise the constant - the gate's cases never belonged to `vfs`, they were merely written in its file - so they are `syscall.gate` and `syscall.dispatch_in_task` in `src/syscall/kunit.rs`, and the seven-argument `syscall` helper lives with them. **`sched::permanent_tasks` turned out to be a hook, and the compiler said so**: with the `vfs` reader task waiting on *the writer's own flag* (the tighter claim - it cannot be broken by another task existing) instead of on "the task count is back to the permanent number", every reader is inside `sched::kunit`, and the production builds reported `function permanent_tasks is never used` plus `unused import: runtime::permanent_tasks`. It, `PERMANENT_TASKS` and the re-export are behind `--features selftest` now: gone from a shipping kernel rather than merely `pub(crate)`. Two smaller consequences: a task's verdict became shared vocabulary (`crate::kunit::{record_verdict, take_verdict}`, one slot per suite so two suites' tasks cannot overwrite each other), and the post-`drain` "did the task end" assertions were dropped as tautologies - `drain` waits for exactly that, and `take_verdict` is what proves the task reached an answer | x86_64 `=== SUMMARY pass=160 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 17 passed, 0 failed` (14 -> 17), exit 33, command exit 0; aarch64 `pass=143 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 18 passed, 0 failed`, exit code 0; the log's lines in order - …, `sched.stacks_returned`, `vfs.ramfs_file`, `vfs.mounted_tree_multi_task`, `syscall.gate`, `syscall.dispatch_in_task`, `ipc.pipes_tty_signals`, `ipc.boot_stack_depth`; all four kernel builds with no new warnings, **the two `permanent_tasks` warnings gone**; host `mpos` 517, `mpos-xtask` 89 including the ratchet test; `depgraph` within the ratchet, back to **8** mutual pairs; `fmt --check` clean | **`mm`, `sched`, `vfs` and the gate landed; the tty-and-signals family is the last one, and it takes `src/board/checks/` with it** |

| 15.23 | **the last family leaves the wiring layer's checks directory, and the boot's own check moves beside the check it drives** - slice 2's last step, and the one that deletes `src/board/checks/`. `check_ipc` was one panicking chain covering three different things, and it is `ipc`'s suite in `src/syscall/kunit.rs` now: `pipes` (bytes across two tasks, EOF once the writer is gone, `EPIPE` with no reader), `tty` (line editing, echo, `Ctrl-D`, and a blocking `read(2)` on fd 0 through the gate) and `signals` (`SIGTERM`; `SIGKILL` into a task blocked *inside* the dispatch; a signal a task sends itself; the `kill(0)`/`ESRCH`/`EINVAL` probe). It is **a second suite beside the gate's rather than three more cases of it**, and the reason is the one the vocabulary is built on: a suite declares the boot step it needs, and these need the tty table and delivery (`Phase::Ipc`) where the gate's cases need the descriptor table and the scheduler (`Phase::Vfs`) - a boot step is not a position in a file. Nor could the family live in `vfs` or `sched`, though that is what it checks: every claim it makes goes *through the gate* (`pipe(2)`, a blocking `read(2)`, `kill(2)`), and §15.22's measurement is what says a suite inside `vfs` may not name `crate::syscall` (`vfs`'s recorded count is 0, and the graph's 8 mutual pairs do not include `vfs <-> syscall`); the cases were written in the checks directory, which had no subsystem of its own. The boot stack's canary was the one case that could not move into a subsystem at all - a boot stack belongs to the boot - so it is `report`'s own `SUITE` (`boot.stack_depth`), in the module that owns `check_boot_stack`, and the harness in `src/board/kunit.rs` is finally the thing its header claims to be: the list and the driver, with no suite of its own. Three smaller things the move settled. The pipe writer's count comes back through a verdict slot - the fourth and last, one per suite that spawns a task, so `vfs`'s two, the gate's one and this one cannot collide. A fed tty read that cannot fail anyway answers through `case_check!` rather than an `expect`, because a case that panicked would hide the cases after it. And the `unreachable!` inside the reader that `SIGKILL` ends stays a scaffold on purpose: a task has no case to return to, which is the same reason `sched`'s run record keeps its `assert!`. The `syscall` helper the family borrows gained the `# Safety` section its dispatcher contract needs, which closes the one clippy warning §15.22 left in that file | x86_64 `=== SUMMARY pass=160 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 19 passed, 0 failed` (17 -> 19: one panicking family becomes three cases, and the boot stack's case becomes a suite of its own), exit 33, command exit 0; aarch64 `pass=143 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 20 passed, 0 failed` (18 -> 20), exit code 0; the log's lines in order - ..., `syscall.dispatch_in_task`, `ipc.pipes`, `ipc.tty`, `M8_IPC_OK`, `ipc.signals`, `boot stack: intact after the self-tests`, `boot.stack_depth`, `mpos-kunit: 19 passed, 0 failed`, then the handoff's own `boot stack: intact under spawn_init`; all four kernel builds with the same four pre-existing x86_64 dead-code warnings and no new ones; host `mpos` 517; `depgraph` within the ratchet - 8 mutual pairs, `vfs -> syscall` still 0/0; `fmt --check` clean; clippy 0 errors on both bare-metal targets, with and without the feature | **slice 2 is done; the `M*` checks' split into bring-up assertions and tests is slice 3** - the classification table is an approval point before any code moves, and the structural gate that keeps a test opening from growing back is slice 5 |

| 15.24 | **the marker vocabulary leaves the boot log, the last two panicking families answer, and the rule that decides which checks stay is written down** - the refactor's last step, and the one the plan kept as a review point. **The rule.** A *bring-up assertion* is what the kernel cannot run without - a device the machine was configured with, a filesystem it must read, a devfs it must publish - so it stays in production, fails loudly, and costs nothing measurable. A *test* is worth proving but not needed to run, or too expensive to prove on every boot, so it is a case. Fifteen of aarch64's device checks are the first kind and did not move: each is an open plus a read (the one that is not, `check_serial_tx`, was already test-build-only and waits in bounded ticks). `check_arch` was both at once, and splitting it is what the split says: `arch.clock_breaks_registers` (the clock's ±10% window, `int3`, the register-table/ABI agreement) and `arch.page_tables_and_spaces` (the direct map after the switch, W^X across the whole image, the null page, the kernel stack's region, two user address spaces mapped, isolated and torn down with their tables accounted for) - and it costs **half a second of busy-waiting per call**, which is the clearest reason a test is a test. Its address-space half had to be rewritten rather than converted: CR3 is on a process's space inside the `critical_section` block, so that block's answer comes *out* of the closure and the kernel's root is restored unconditionally and checked before the verdict is returned - an early return would have carried the rest of the boot on the wrong page tables, the class §13.14/§13.15 record. `check_serial_tx`/`check_console_rx` answer too, which let their two adapters go, and the transmit case still reports its *detail* through the machine's polled writer: a queued-bytes failure is by definition a ring that is not draining, so a report filed through the console queue is one nobody reads. **The vocabulary.** Nothing had parsed `M*` markers since §15.6/§15.7 retired the command that did, and a token a reader cannot tell from a mechanism is worse than a sentence: the board's seventeen checks and the machine layer's eleven (`M1_TIMER_OK`, `M3_TABLES_OK`, `M6_*` in `mpos-arch`) now name what they are (`virtio-blk: …`, `9p: …`, `tables: …`, `space: …`), which is what the first machine's driver lines had always done. Eighteen `_OK` lines were deleted as pure repetition of the sentence above them; three that carried data of their own were reworded (the timer's interrupt count, the context switch's hand-over count, the devfs read-back). The two production *events* became sentences - `mpos: init is pid 1, not yet dispatched`, and `mpos: /init is up; a child is running under it`, which is what `has_live_child` waited for all along - and `M4_CONSOLE_OK`/`M8_NET_STACK_OK` were deleted because the line before each is the same fact. The suites' own `M0_OK`/`M2_OK`/`M4_OK`/`M6_OK`/`M7_OK`/`M8_IPC_OK` went the same way: a case's verdict is the harness's `mpos-kunit: <suite>.<case> OK`. **The rule is a gate now.** `xtask/src/gates.rs`'s `test_openings` fails `cargo xtask test` before it compiles anything when the boot's own code reads a test word off the command line, asks `feature = "selftest"` outside a `#[cfg]`, or prints an `M*` marker literal - three words no compiler can see - over a list of seven files and only those, because three comments inside that list say `mpos.selftest` on purpose to record what the kernel stopped reading | x86_64 `=== SUMMARY pass=160 fail=0` with `mpos-kunit 20 passed, 0 failed` (19 -> 20: the `arch` family splits) and exit 33, command exit 0; aarch64 `pass=143 fail=0` with `mpos-kunit 20 passed, 0 failed`, exit code 0; the log's lines in order - ..., `arch.clock_breaks_registers`, `arch.page_tables_and_spaces`, `sched.alternating`, ..., `boot.stack_depth`, `mpos-kunit: 20 passed, 0 failed`, `mpos: handing the machine to /init`, `mpos: /init is up; a child is running under it`; the aarch64 log's plain lines as they now read - `devices: 32 virtio-mmio windows, ...`, `timer: 25 interrupts in ...`, `space: 4 pages mapped ...`, `tls: set_thread_pointer(...)`, and the board's `devfs:`/`virtio-gpu:`/`9p:` findings; both gate lines print before the host tests (`Target gates: every test module is one the host compiles`, `Target gates: the boot has no test opening`); all four kernel builds with no new warnings; host `mpos` 517, `mpos-xtask` 93 (89 -> 93: the gate's four tests); `depgraph` within the ratchet, 8 mutual pairs; `fmt --check` clean; clippy 0 errors on both bare-metal targets with and without the feature; and grepping the tree's Rust for the vocabulary finds only prose | **the refactor is done.** The one row a reader can still argue about is the pair the classification flagged as debatable: W^X across the whole image and the null page are cheap, and violating either is a security hole, so a production kernel *could* assert them the way Linux's `CONFIG_DEBUG_WX` does - they are in a test build today, and moving them back is a smaller step than the one they came with. **§15.34 revised the rule's other half**: *what* a device check may assert is bounded too - only what the kernel produces from the devices the machine was configured with, never a file or a byte pattern the build packed (`/hello`, the raw image's known sector and the host share's probe file were all read by checks until then, and each made an image's contents a boot condition) |
| 15.25 | **batch 37 of the ABI migration moves `t-fbdev`, and the hand-spelled record meets musl's `c_ulong`.** The framebuffer door was the smallest device-shaped program left that both machines serve: since the gap-closing pass opened the fbdev ioctl gates, aarch64 publishes `/dev/fb0` unconditionally behind its MMIO card (the boot log prints the geometry on both), so the move needed no new group decision in `tests.toml`. What the move measured is a *layout* trap the suite had not met: `libc` binds neither the `FBIO*` words nor either `struct fb_*_screeninfo` for linux at all (measured: absent from `libc` 0.2.190's whole tree; `rustix` stops at termios), so the records are spelled here as `t-rtc` spells its `_IOR('p', …)` family - but `fb_fix_screeninfo`'s two `unsigned long`s land differently than a Rust author expects, because `libc::c_ulong` follows Rust's `c_long` rule (pointer-width unless windows) and **musl targets alias it to `u32` even at 64-bit** (measured: `size_of::<c_ulong>() == 4` on both musl arms, 8 on gnu). A field spelled with it would put `smem_start` at 16 and fail the program's own offset assert on the very payloads it exists to check - the first batch where the maintained table itself is the wrong source for a *type*, not a number. The head therefore pins every field below the two longs by compile-time `offset_of!` asserts against `linux/fb.h`'s numbers (xres@0, bpp@24, red@32, transp@68, smem_len@24, type@28, visual@36, line_length@48, mmio_len@56) - `t-getdents64`'s build-claim rule, which also caught this file's own first draft: an early version asserted the var head's *size* as 44, which is the C field sum, while the trailing bitfield triples push it to 80; the seed-survives claim reads through byte 79/51, the bytes each kernel arm actually writes. Two claims are new pins of Linux's answers rather than migrations: `yres_virtual == yres` (a single scanout has nothing to pan across) and `smem_len == line_length * yres` (the stride-times-rows relation fbdev clients size their copy from), both stated from the headers and holding on both machines. The host cannot measure any of it: this sandbox's `/dev/fb0` is `crw-rw---- root:video` and refuses an unprivileged open (measured EACCES), so a hosted run forgives only what the host's privileges refuse - `t-dsp`'s rule - while the gate keeps pinning all twenty claims. | x86_64 `user tests: 170 programs built` / `160 staged, 10 left out` / `=== SUMMARY pass=160 fail=0` with `mpos-kunit 20 passed, 0 failed` and exit 33, command exit 0; aarch64 `146 aarch64 programs built` / `144 staged, 3 left out` / `pass=144 fail=0` (was 145 staged with one stray probe binary that had no source; the clean run is 144 and its payload now carries `t-fbdev` where the abitest side carried it before) reaching `=== END`, exit code 0 - **the first run of this program on the second machine**, with `=== RUN t-fbdev` / `=== PASS t-fbdev` in both logs and the **same 20 claims**, their per-claim `ok:` lines identical on the two machines; host tests 517; `mpos-xtask` 93; `depgraph` within the ratchet, 8 mutual pairs; `fmt --check` clean; clippy clean on both musl targets; `user/abitest` is down to **23** programs (0 syscall plus 23 integration) |
| 15.26 | **batch 38 of the ABI migration moves `t-fsflags`, and the flag's two halves get a case each.** The attribute word is the door `lsattr`/`chattr` are made of, and both machines serve it since the gap-closing pass opened the ioctl families: no group decision was needed. What the move found is that the no-std program had only ever pinned *half* the rule. Its claims all reach the checks through `ioctl(2)` on a descriptor, which is the syscall door; but the same four questions (`may_modify`, `may_write`, `may_alter`, `may_remove`) are asked by every path that changes a file, and two of them have no payload spelling at all: an **append-only directory** refusing the removal of what lives in it (Linux's `may_delete` asked of the parent - `chattr +a` on a directory is not a thing any test does), and a **symlink** - a node with no attribute word, which answers the flag question as ENOTTY-shaped refusal yet is still policed by its parent's flag like any other name. Both now live as a third case of the `vfs` suite (`inode_flags_are_policed`), reading the filesystem through the module's own API rather than through the gate: §15.23's rule that a suite beside the module may read its private state, applied to a claim the sweep structurally cannot make. The migrated program keeps every claim the no-std file made - the whole-word refusal set, the IMMUTABLE seven-door list, APPEND's end-of-file rule, the pipe's ENOTTY, and the capability dance - and moves three numbers onto maintained tables: `SYS_setresuid`/`SYS_fchown` per machine (93/55 for the chown, the number the no-std literal could not carry), and the drop itself raw because `libc::setresuid` takes `uid_t` and cannot name `-1` (`t-ns-uts`'s door again). One fixture step is new for a measured reason: the payload has no non-root user and `/tmp` is root's, so the owned file is created while root and handed over with `fchown(1000, 1000)` before the effective-id drop - the no-std version dropped first and then created, which works only because this kernel's DAC check reads the *effective* id where Linux's `may_create_in` would ask the directory's write bit of a uid that owns nothing there. The host measured its side of the shape: tmpfs stores none of NODUMP/NOATIME/SYNC/DIRSYNC (errno 95) and refuses `chattr +i` outright for a caller without `CAP_LINUX_IMMUTABLE` (errno 1), so the hosted run takes named refusal branches for those sections - `t-posix`'s rule - while the gate pins all forty-one claims on both machines. | x86_64 `user tests: 170 programs built` / `160 staged, 10 left out` / `=== SUMMARY pass=160 fail=0` (the count does not move - the program was already in the sweep from the abitest side) with `mpos-kunit 21 passed, 0 failed` (20 -> 21: the flags case), exit 33, command exit 0; aarch64 `148 aarch64 programs built` / `145 staged, 3 left out` (was 144: the std side now carries `t-fsflags`, which the abitest staging used to hand it) / `pass=145 fail=0` reaching `=== END`, exit code 0, `mpos-kunit 21 passed, 0 failed`; `=== RUN t-fsflags` / `=== PASS t-fsflags` in both logs with the **same 41 claims**, their per-claim `ok:` lines byte-identical on the two machines; host tests 517; `mpos-xtask` 93; `depgraph` within the ratchet, 8 mutual pairs; `fmt --check` clean; clippy 0 errors on both bare-metal targets with and without the feature, warning count unchanged at 49; `user/abitest` is down to **22** programs (0 syscall plus 22 integration) |

The vocabulary is hand-spelled as `t-rtc` spells its ioctl family, and the measurement says why: `libc` 0.2.190 binds neither `struct nlmsghdr` nor any `NLMSG_*`/`NLM_F_*` word for linux (those exist in its android and uapi-mirror trees only), while `rustix` speaks netlink *addresses* (`SocketAddrNetlink`, `bind`) but owns no part of the record shape - which is the subject, so the parser stays the test's. One door moved onto rustix that the no-std version did not use: the migrated program **binds explicitly**, because glibc's auto-bind-on-first-send is a wrapper convenience the raw door does not have and Linux does not promise; the bind is the shape busybox `ip` builds itself. Two claims are new pins of Linux's answers rather than migrations: an administratively up interface also reports `IFF_RUNNING` (the bit `ip link` prints inside `<UP,RUNNING>`, and a caller trusting `IFF_UP` alone misreads on a carrier-less card), and the link dump names **both** interfaces Linux's enumeration has - `lo` at index 1 with `IFF_LOOPBACK|IFF_UP`, eth0 at 2. Not run on the host, and batch 34's reason is exact: a host Linux answers every request here including all three refusals (EEXIST/EADDRNOTAVAIL/ESRCH are Linux's own numbers), but it answers them about *its* stack - 10.0.2.15 is this kernel's static address, and setting the host's real NIC down would take the sandbox's network with it - so the hosted run says so and claims nothing, finishing green with its reason printed. | x86_64 `user tests: 170 programs built` / `160 staged, 10 left out` / `=== SUMMARY pass=160 fail=0` (the count does not move - the program was already in the sweep from the abitest side) with `mpos-kunit 21 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33, command exit 0; aarch64 `149 aarch64 programs built` / `146 staged, 3 left out` (was 145: the std side now carries `t-netlink`, which the abitest staging used to hand it) / `pass=146 fail=0` reaching `=== END`, exit code 0, kunit 21/0; `=== RUN t-netlink` / `=== PASS t-netlink` in both logs with the **same 25 claims**, their per-claim `ok:` lines byte-identical on the two machines; host tests 517; `mpos-xtask` 93; `depgraph` within the ratchet, 8 mutual pairs; `fmt --check` clean; clippy clean on both musl targets; `user/abitest` is down to **21** programs (0 syscall plus 21 integration) |
| 15.28 | **batch 40 of the ABI migration moves `t-ioctl`, and three host walls get named instead of walked.** The console, entropy-pool and clock families are the boot's own services' doors - seedrng's credit, hwclock's read, the line editor's window query - and both machines serve every one: the `'K'` gates went down in the gap-closing pass, rtc0 answers on either machine (the first machine's CMOS, the second's PL031 through one `crate::hal`), and the pool is `src/net`'s generator with its designed-full 4096. | 15.29 | **batch 41 of the ABI migration moves the `slow` trio - `t-forkcost`, `t-pipecost`, `t-netlat` - and the aarch64 sweep's selection finally matches the rule written for it.** The three are instruments, not assertions: their numbers are the point (§10.2's wake bound is asserted *inside* `t-forkcost`, the pipe rows only report), which is why they sit in `slow`. What the move measured is bigger than the programs: §13.5 promised "the aarch64 sweep runs every program it built" except its own gap group, but `sweep::select` asked that promise through `runs()`, whose reason-groups (`external`/`slow`/`diagnostic`) name the *first* machine's harness needs - so every migrated program in a reason-group was built for aarch64, staged out, and run by neither arm's default sweep. Measured end to end: the trio appeared in `built` (153) with digests, never in the stamp or the payload; the guest ran exactly what the plan staged, twice over. The fix is one branch in `select`: on the second machine a *reason* never leaves a program out, only its own machine-named gap list does; x86_64's selection is byte-for-byte unchanged (pinned both ways by two new `mpos-xtask` tests). **Superseded by §15.38**, which found the branch's cost — it exempted `external` with `slow`, so the second machine's default gate kept depending on the host — and replaced it with the same question on both arms. Two consequences landed with it. The aarch64 root partition overflowed the moment the payload grew to the full std suite (`mkfs.ext4` refusing block allocation at 64 MiB with a ~57 MB `/bin`), so `SWEEP_DISK_BYTES` went to 96 MiB - the number is now a measurement with its failure mode written beside it. And the x86_64 test initramfs stopped being a repacked copy of the Alpine tree: staging unpacked and re-tarred 70 MB, doubling what the guest's ramfs charges (limit a quarter of RAM, §10.4) while `t-fill-ramfs` needs the headroom; the programs now ride as ustar members appended to the base archive's tail - the base's zero-block terminator stripped, its `./init` renamed `./init.boot` (the reader refuses a duplicate regular file outright), the runner and each `/bin/t-*` written with the header/checksum rules `src/vfs/tar.rs` parses. Pinned against GNU `tar` by a new xtask test that lists the result and demands each member exactly once - the draft that shipped wrote all-zero headers, then a checksum that counted the old chksum bytes, and both readers saw nothing. The programs themselves keep every claim: `t-forkcost`'s 200+20-round loop, witness-child share proof and sub-tick reap bound; `t-pipecost`'s stamped 64 KiB chunks across the fork plus the 4096-B control phase; `t-netlat`'s twenty hand-built echo rounds matched by id and sequence, with the NETLAT summary line. | 15.30 | **batch 42 of the ABI migration moves `t-rawicmp`, and the first draft's own failure is the batch's measurement.** The public-address pinger was the last std-buildable program in `external` whose claims are about the wire rather than about a server: three hand-built echo rounds against 110.242.74.102, every delivered byte printed - version/ihl, the ttl octet at 8, protocol, the ICMP type behind the header, the echoed pattern - beside the `IP_RECVTTL` control record, so the two witnesses ping reads can be compared against each other. Both machines serve it through one slirp, which is what makes it a machine check once the std side carries it (and §15.29's selection fix is what let it run on the second at all). Three doors, and the middle one is the lesson: rustix owns the socket (`socket(AF_INET, SOCK_RAW, Some(ipproto::ICMP))`, `t-netlat`'s three words); `libc::syscall(SYS_sendto/SYS_recvmsg)` drives the pair, clause (b) measured twice over - rustix 1.1.5's `recvfrom` decodes the sender from its own fixed 128-byte storage, so the kernel's write-back into *the caller's* length word cannot be claimed through it, and its `RecvAncillaryMessage` names only SCM_RIGHTS/SCM_CREDENTIALS (`IpTtl` is a later minor), while this file's subject is that record's level/type/data-at-16 shape and the `msg_controllen` write-back. `libc::msghdr` itself turned out to be unusable for the claim too - musl targets bind `msg_controllen` as a `socklen_t` (u32) where musl and this kernel's reader use a pointer-sized word (measured through the crate's source: the new-musl record pads around an int `msg_iovlen`) - so the header is spelled at the offsets `src/net/socket.rs` reads (name@0 namelen@8 iov@16 iovlen@24 control@32 controllen@40 flags@48), the same build-claim rule `t-fbdev` pins by asserts. Two failures named the migration's real risks before the program went green: a `MSG_DONTWAIT` receive reads EAGAIN on *every* round because slirp answers hundreds of microseconds after the guest's send returns (the no-std version blocked, and blocking is the measured shape - the wait ends on the stack's own wakeup, §10.2); and `sendto`'s length word must carry the struct's size, because this kernel's `parse_sockaddr_in` refuses a short one with EINVAL where Linux's `move_addr_from_user` ignores it - passing 0 for "Linux would not care" pinned the refusal instead (both arms now pass 16, which is also exactly what rustix's typed wrapper passes). The host says-so-and-claims-nothing: a host Linux answers about its own route, ttl and CAP_NET_RAW, not through any slirp. | x86_64 `user tests: 170 programs built` / `160 staged, 10 left out` / `=== SUMMARY pass=160 fail=0` with `mpos-kunit 21 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33, command exit 0; `--groups external` `pass=165 fail=0` with the five programs green and the measurement line `seq 1: rtt=45ms n=48 ver_ihl=0x45 ttl@8=255 proto@9=1 icmp@20=0 cmsg_len=24 cmsg_ttl=255 namelen=16 peer=110.242.74.102`; aarch64 `153 aarch64 programs built` / `153 staged, 1 left out` (was 152: the std side now carries `t-rawicmp`, which the abitest staging used to hand it) / `pass=153 fail=0` reaching `=== END`, kunit 21/0 - **the first run of this pinger on the second machine**, `=== RUN t-rawicmp` / `=== PASS t-rawicmp` in both logs with the **same 20 claims**, their per-claim `ok:` lines byte-identical on the two machines and the delivered bytes identical too (ttl 255, n=48, cmsg 24) down to the millisecond-scale rtt; host run green with its fabric note; host tests 517; `mpos-xtask` 95; `depgraph` within the ratchet, 8 mutual pairs; `fmt --check` clean; clippy clean on both musl targets; `user/abitest` is down to **16** programs (0 syscall plus 16 integration) |
| 15.31 | **`struct epoll_event` is the *machine's* layout, and the aarch64 half of the second machine's desktop was the x86_64 one.** `vfs::epoll` held the record's shape as one pair of numbers - twelve bytes, `data` at byte 4 - and `epoll_wait` wrote every report at them, with a host unit test asserting exactly those two values. Linux packs that struct on x86_64 *alone*: `EPOLL_PACKED` in `uapi/linux/eventpoll.h` is `__attribute__((packed))` under `#if defined(__x86_64__)` and nothing anywhere else, so on aarch64 the caller's record is sixteen bytes with `data` at 8 - measured here twice over: `linux-raw-sys` 0.12.1, the ABI crate `rustix` rides on and therefore the repo's own test library, declares aarch64 `#[repr(C)]` and x86_64 `#[repr(C, packed)]`; and a faulting udev worker's frame showed the caller's `events` word at `sp+0x88` with its `data.ptr` at `sp+0x90`, which is `data` at +8. Writing the x86_64 offsets on aarch64 put `data` four bytes before the caller read it, so the caller's low half came from the middle of the kernel's `data` and its **high half from the next record's `events` word** - which is what udev's workers died of: three forked siblings per boot, each dereferencing a `data.ptr` of `<the low half of a registered stack pointer> \| (EPOLLIN << 32)`, every device on the second machine left untagged while the boot still reached `mpos login:`. **The pin was the other half of the bug, and its first draft did not bite.** `t-epoll` carried its own `#[repr(C, packed(4))]` record and asserted `size_of == 12 && offset_of(data) == 4` on *both* machines, so it agreed with the kernel it was checking; and a round trip through the same wrong offsets *cancels* for a data word that fits in 32 bits - `0x1234` left at byte 8, was read at byte 4, written at byte 4 and read back at byte 8 as `0x1234` (measured: the claim printed `ok:` under the buggy kernel, which is why the first counterfactual run was green). Both halves are the machine's now, on the kernel side (`#[cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")]` 16/8) and in both records (`t-epoll`, `t-epoll-socket`), and the word the data claims register is a full 64-bit pattern - the one value shape that cannot cancel | the counterfactual, measured: with the two constants put back to 12/4 the aarch64 arm reports `=== SUMMARY pass=159 fail=1` and `=== FAIL t-epoll exit=1`, with exactly `FAIL: the report carries the data word` and `FAIL: the caller's data word rides with it`; with them the machine's own, `pass=160 fail=0` and `mpos-kunit 21 passed, 0 failed`; the desktop boot's udev faults went from three to **zero** while PCI still enumerates (`pci: 2 functions enumerated`) and `ahci: sda, 8192 sectors` still comes up behind the dynamic BAR window; `cargo test -p mpos --lib --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` 521 - the x86_64 half of the split, and its half of the host assertion, are byte-for-byte what they were; `fmt --check` clean on every touched file, and `clippy` 0 errors on both bare-metal kernel targets (the host clippy has pre-existing failures in files this change does not touch) | **low** - one record's layout, and the x86_64 machine's bytes are what they were |

| 15.32 | **`utimensat` on a `/dev` node keeps the clocks, and the node kind that mattered was the one a guest named rather than a reader.** The gap was docs/uapi/ §5.2's, and the plan it carried named the cell and the place: `NodePerms` is where a device node's mutable attributes live, so `vfs/devfs.rs`'s `NodePerms` gained `atime`/`mtime` beside `mode`/`owner`, `DevNode` forwards the trait's three time methods to it, and every other node holding one does the same in its own module (the console tty, `/dev/tty0`, the controlling-tty alias, `/dev/ptmx`, the DRM card, `/dev/fb0`, `/dev/uinput`, the evdev nodes - eleven sites). Both words are stored, not the mtime alone, because `touch -a` sends an atime with `UTIME_OMIT` in the other slot and the syscall resolves each sentinel against what the node reports. What the plan could not know is *which* node a boot refuses on, and the first draft measured that it had guessed wrong: with every character device wired, the same boot still printed the line. The refusal was then instrumented for one boot (a temporary `print!` in `utimensat` naming the node's kind, inode and `rdev`) and it answered four times over, every one a **block** node: `BlockDev ino 23 rdev Some((254, 1))` - that is `/dev/vda1` - then `/dev/vda2`, `/dev/vda` and `/dev/sda`. `DevBlockNode` is a separate inode in the same file and held no `NodePerms` at all: the plan's sentence "every devfs node answers the trait's default" was true of it, and the enumeration that would have caught it was never made. It holds one now, born `0660 root:root` - the number `abi::mode_of` already falls back to for `BlockDev`, so nothing `stat` reported moved, while `chmod`/`chown` on a disk node start sticking, the same claim the character nodes' modes make. Two lessons are worth more than the fix: the dispatcher's note is once per *syscall number*, so a second refusing node kind stays silent behind the first (the probe is what made this one visible), and `timeout` around `cargo xtask run` leaves its QEMU alive - the next aarch64 sweep then dies at `vhost-vsock: unable to set guest cid: Address already in use` (§13.28's trap, self-inflicted, and the reason this row's first aarch64 sweep line is a refusal rather than a summary) | the counterfactual, measured on the second machine's desktop boot (`cargo xtask run --arch aarch64 --mode terminal`): before, `mpos: syscall 88 (fd -100) -> EOPNOTSUPP` on the wire immediately after `Populating /dev with existing devices through uevents`; the instrumented boot named the four block nodes above; after, that line is absent from the whole boot and its 19 OpenRC service lines are byte-identical to the pre-change boot's, reaching `mpos login:`. Host: `cargo test -p mpos --lib --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu -- vfs::devfs` 8 passed, the new `device_nodes_keep_the_clocks_they_are_given` claiming a character *and* a block node with the two clocks moved apart. Sweeps: x86_64 `171 programs built` / `160 staged, 11 left out` / `=== SUMMARY pass=160 fail=0` with `mpos-kunit 21 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33, command exit 0; aarch64 `162 programs built` / `161 staged, 1 left out` / `=== SUMMARY pass=161 fail=0` with `mpos-kunit 21 passed, 0 failed`, reaching `=== END` - and `=== RUN t-devtmpfs` / `=== PASS t-devtmpfs` in **both** guest logs, which is the pin the two arms share. `clippy` 0 errors on both bare-metal kernel targets, aarch64's lib warning count unchanged at 48; `fmt` clean on every touched file | **low** - one cell and its eleven forwarders, and one node kind whose birth mode is the number `stat` already reported |

| 15.33 | **the initramfs overlay gets the rootfs's `core/`, and the two `/init` scripts become one - a decision §15.7 had deliberately left the other way.** §15.7 unified the pipeline and stopped short of the scripts, with the reason written down: "the script differences (the `R:` selftest read, `MPOS_SWITCH_ROOT_OK`, and the `exec` target) are the *disk root's* difference rather than duplication". Reading the two files against each other says that was one difference and three duplications - the `R:` probe (which the first machine's boots never trigger, since its test payload stages an `/init` of its own), the verdict line, and `export PATH`/`mkdir /sysroot/oldroot` - and the second file's own header had been claiming "This is the same script the x86_64 machine runs" while the bodies drifted. So the layout is the rootfs's now: `assets/initramfs/core/` holds the one `/init` both machines run (and the tree's inventory beside it), merged **first**, with each machine's own tree over it - both empty today, and a directory that does not exist merges to nothing, so an empty half needs no case. The one real machine fact survives on its own terms: which pid-1 chain the disk carries is asked of the disk (`if [ -x /sysroot/init ]`), which is the question `execve` asks a moment later, and it is the reason the second machine's script had to name `/init` at all (its cross-arch `apk --no-scripts` leaves the applet farm to that disk script) while the first machine's Alpine install has none to find. **What moved on the first machine's wire**, measured rather than reasoned about: a boot now prints `MPOS_SWITCH_ROOT_OK: / is /dev/vda2 (ext4)` (the second machine always did), a failed root mount reads `MPOS_SWITCH_ROOT_FAILED:` instead of `cannot mount …; staying here`, the selftest console probe is present but inert (no `mpos.selftest` word reaches that boot), and `/mnt/share` is re-mounted on the switched root as it already was on the second machine. Nothing parses any of those lines, and `gates.rs`'s rule that the marker is the *script's* and not a kernel literal still holds | `cargo test -p mpos-xtask --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` 99 passed, four of them this module's: the re-pointed `@vocabulary:` reader (it read the second machine's private copy before, which is how the two bodies could drift unnoticed) and the new pin that `core/init` exists and **neither** machine tree carries an `init`. Both archives rebuilt from the core tree and the script is the same file in both: `tar tvf` gives `./init` 8690 bytes, mode `0755`, owner `0/0`, and `tar xOf ./init \| md5sum` is `0d4399f4…` for `build/initramfs.tar` *and* `build/aarch64-initramfs.tar`, equal to `assets/initramfs/core/init`'s own; `./root/README` rides in both; the repository is down to two `init` files (`initramfs/core/init`, and the second machine's *rootfs* one, which is its disk's pid-1 chain and not this). The first machine boots through it: `initramfs: 469 members unpacked`, `MPOS_SWITCH_ROOT_OK: / is /dev/vda2 (ext4)`, `mpos login:` - so the `-x` test picked `/sbin/init` where a literal used to be. The second machine's sweep is green through the same file (`161 staged, 1 left out` / `=== SUMMARY pass=161 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 21 passed, 0 failed`, `=== END`, and `MPOS_SWITCH_ROOT_OK` in its guest log: the kernel unpacks the archive, runs core's `/init`, and the disk's own `/init` is what the test found). **Not verified, and why:** the second machine's *Alpine* boot - its image's root carries no `/hello`, so `check_fs` halts the boot before the archive is even unpacked (`ext4 mounted but /hello was not found through it`; `debugfs stat /hello` on the mounted partition: File not found). `/hello` is written only by a full install (`rootfs.rs`'s `install_base`, gated on `Arch::kernel_reads_root_probe`), and `cargo xtask rootfs --arch aarch64` answers "Updating the image (boot files only)" and then "up to date" because `build/.rootfs-aarch64.stamp` is newer than the config - so no command in the tree restores it. Forcing one (`rm build/.rootfs-aarch64.stamp` first) is the way back and wipes the evidence, which is why this session left it alone | **low** - one file moved and a merge order, with both machines' boots already running the same body; a machine that needs its own script now fails a test rather than drifting silently |

| 15.34 | **both machines boot one standard Alpine chain, the applet farm is laid by the build, and the aarch64 device checks stop depending on what the build packed.** One intent, three parts. **(1) The chain.** The second machine's disk carried a `/init` of its own (`assets/rootfs/aarch64/init`) whose work was the applet farm a cross-architecture `apk` cannot install (it runs with `--no-scripts`) plus the mounts a real init expects - and those mounts are what the shared initramfs script already does on the new root, so the file was a farm plus a duplicate. The farm moves into the build (`rootfs::lay_applet_farm`, which links `/bin/busybox` at each of 309 paths from `assets/configs/busybox-applets.txt` and *skips* a name a real package already answers), the disk `/init` is deleted, and `assets/initramfs/core/init` execs `/sbin/init` on both machines: the name a standard Alpine disk answers to, with the `-x /sysroot/init` test gone because no disk carries `/init` any more. A sweep's disk stages its runner at `sbin/init` for the same reason (the payload lands last, so the runner stands where an installed image's own pid 1 would) and now also carries the full farm, so a test disk answers the same names a product one does. **(2) The checks.** `check_fs` read `/hello` out of the image, `check_blk` compared a packed `i as u8` pattern *and* asserted the image's exact 2048 sectors, and `check_p9_dev` read a probe file xtask wrote into the host share - so an image missing any of them could not boot at all, which is exactly what this session hit (§15.33's tail: an image with no `/hello` halted before userspace). Each now asserts only what the kernel produces from the devices the machine was configured with: `check_blk` needs no fixture *content* (every window in the tree must answer a sector read, and the window `find_filesystem` finds no filesystem in is where a pattern it generates round-trips), `check_fs` mounts the ext4 and reads the root's own listing, `check_p9_dev` resolves the share's root inode and lists it. `PROBE_FILE`/`PROBE_BYTES`, their three writers and `Arch::kernel_reads_root_probe` are gone. **(3) What the first draft cost is the part worth keeping**: the write half of `check_fs` (write a marker, read it back, unlink) is *unreachable* from the boot context - `create` stamps the new inode's owner from `sched::current_owner()`, and by the time the device checks run `t.current` is the idle sentinel, so the kernel panics with `there is no task 63` and prints its stack. A boot check has no identity to own a file; the ext4 *write* path is left to the machine's own boot and to the sweep's programs, and the check stops at the read. `stage_program` grew one rule for the same reason in reverse: a payload name that is currently a symlink is *removed* rather than written through (`fs::write` followed the farm's `sbin/init` link out of the tree and failed with `Permission denied` on the host's `/bin/busybox`). **(4) Two inherited lines went, after a session's `ls /` caught them.** The unified script carried the second machine's `export PATH` and `mkdir -p /sysroot/oldroot`, whose comment claimed both were load-bearing for busybox's `switch_root` ("PATH must be set"; "deletes the old root through `/oldroot`"). Both claims are `util-linux`'s `switch_root`, not this busybox's, and the binary says so: `strings busybox | grep oldroot` is empty and `PATH must be set` is not in it either, while its usage line is the whole of what it does - "chroot to NEW_ROOT, delete all in /, move NEW_ROOT to /, execute NEW_INIT. PID must be 1. NEW_ROOT must be a mountpoint" - all four of which the script satisfies without either line (`PATH` is exported at the top, and the old root is deleted by walking `/`). The `mkdir` was not inert, though: it left an empty `/oldroot` on every root the **first** machine booted, a directory no standard Alpine root has, and the two built images carried one each until they were removed (`debugfs -w -R "rmdir /oldroot"`). The lesson is the §3.6 shape one step further out - a rationale copied from the *other* implementation is a measurement about neither machine - and the fix is two lines deleted plus a comment that names the command that checked them. | the farm list is `busybox --list-full` on the machine's *own* binary (BusyBox v1.38.0): 309 paths, 84 in `bin`, 60 in `sbin`, 138 in `usr/bin`, 27 in `usr/sbin`, both machines' answers byte-identical today (measured under `qemu-aarch64` for the second machine's busybox and with its own musl loader for the first's) - which is why one file serves both, and the pin turns red the day that stops being true. The four names a booted image lacked are the ones a real package owns (`sbin/ifup`/`ifdown` ifupdown's, `sbin/mkdosfs`/`mkfs.vfat` dosfstools'), which the build's skip rule leaves alone. A forced full reinstall (`rm build/.rootfs-aarch64.stamp`, then `cargo xtask rootfs --arch aarch64`) leaves **309 of 309** recorded paths present, `/sbin/openrc` (67440 B) and `/sbin/apk` (135248 B) untouched, and no `/hello` in the image at all: the farm is the build's now, not a boot's. The second machine then boots the chain - `MPOS_SWITCH_ROOT_OK: / is /dev/vda2 (ext4)` → `mpos login:` - with the checks' own lines `virtio-blk over MMIO: the tree describes 2 window(s)` / `answered a sector read on 2 window(s)` / `wrote and read back a sector of a raw device (2048 sectors)` / `ext4 mounted over the MMIO virtio-blk device; its root listed 21 name(s)` / `9p: /mnt/share is the host share over MMIO; its root listed 4 name(s)`. Both arms green with the new pin: aarch64 `162 staged, 1 left out` / `=== SUMMARY pass=162 fail=0` (161 + `t-busybox-farm`), `mpos-kunit 21 passed, 0 failed`; x86_64 `161 staged, 11 left out` / `pass=161 fail=0`, kunit 21/0. The pin's claims, per machine: aarch64 `every recorded path is in the image (0 missing)` / `308 are the build's links into /bin/busybox, 1 are a package's own answer (["sbin/init"])` - the *runner*, which the payload owns by contract - and x86_64 `309 are the build's links`, with `busybox names exactly the recorded paths (0 in busybox only; 0 recorded only)` on both. `cargo test -p mpos-xtask` 100 passed (the list's shape is pinned host-side: sorted, unique, under a directory a PATH holds, `sbin/init` present); `clippy` 0 errors on both bare-metal targets. | **low** - the chain is the one the first machine always ran and the second one now reaches by deleting a file, the farm is data with a machine-side pin, and the checks assert less than they did (the read path, not a packed file's contents) while still halting on a device that does not answer |
| 15.35 | **the refusal a desktop still showed after §15.32 was a different node in the same udev pass, and the way to name it was to enumerate what Linux answers rather than to guess a second time.** `mpos: syscall 88 (fd -100) -> EOPNOTSUPP` came back on an aarch64 desktop, in the same place §15.32 had left it ("*Populating /dev with existing devices through uevents*") - and §15.32's own lesson said why a second node could hide there: the dispatcher's note is keyed by syscall *number*, so once the block nodes were wired the next refuser printed nothing behind the first. So the enumeration §15.32 recorded as "never made" was made, on the host, with one probe (`.workbuddy/utimensat_host.c`, its output in the commit body): a `/dev` directory keeps both clocks, a device node's symlink keeps both, a `/proc` *file* keeps both and a `/proc` *directory* answers `EPERM`, sysfs answers `EACCES`, and the *only* nodes that refuse are the anonymous-inode descriptors (`eventfd`, `epoll`, `timerfd`, `signalfd`, `inotify`) with `EPERM`. **Nothing Linux produces answers `EOPNOTSUPP` for this call at all** - the errno the trait's default had been answering, its doc justifying it ("for a filesystem with nowhere to keep a time"), which is the sentence that made the refusal look intentional. So the default became `EPERM`, and the four nodes still refusing in devfs - the root, an mdev directory, a link and a socket name, exactly the ones §3.3 had named as "outside" - keep both words in a `Clocks` of their own; the ramfs symlink, socket and `mknod` device nodes keep *one*, that filesystem's single-clock rule, where they had refused too. | the counterfactual is the measurement. Host: `cargo test -p mpos --lib --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` 523 passed - `vfs::devfs::path_named_nodes_keep_the_clocks_they_are_given` (root, directory, symlink, socket, the two words moved apart; it goes red the day a forwarder is dropped, since the default is now `EPERM`), `device_nodes_keep_the_clocks_they_are_given` unchanged, and `vfs::ramfs::nodes_carry_a_clock_that_writes_and_utimensat_move` extended to claim a symlink, a socket and a device node. On the machine the pin is `t-devtmpfs`, grown to claim `/dev` itself and `/dev/dri` - a real `DevDirNode` the display driver lays, and the directory the pass in question makes: both take a stamp on **both** machines (`/dev takes a stamp (Ok(()))`, `/dev/dri takes a stamp (Ok(()))`, atime `1600000002` with mtime held at `1600000007`). That last line is a fix of its own: the test's two values were `ATIME` and `ATIME + 1`, so the `touch -a` half compared the mtime against a number equal to the new atime - a node that moved the wrong clock passed. They are distinct now. Sweeps: aarch64 `=== SUMMARY pass=170 fail=0` / `mpos-kunit 29 passed, 0 failed` / all 170 staged programs passed (2 left out); x86_64 `pass=161 fail=0` / kunit 29/0 / 161 passed (11 not run). `clippy` 0 errors on both bare-metal targets, aarch64's lib warning count unchanged at 48. | **low** - one default's error code and four nodes' clocks in one filesystem, with the enumeration that replaces guessing left in the tree as the probe. |
| 15.36 | **the aarch64 GL path died on a class of EL0 trap this kernel did not answer at all - and the two instructions behind it are the first two a code flush executes, four seconds into a weston boot.** `SCTLR_EL1.UCT` and `UCI` are clear on this machine - `paging::SCTLR_ENABLE` ORs only `M\|C\|I` onto what the firmware left, exactly as Linux's `__cpu_setup` writes its own set and nothing else - so an EL0 read of `CTR_EL0` and an EL0 `DC`/`IC` maintenance operation **trap** with `EC 0x18` ("MSR/MRS (AArch64)"). That is Linux's own design rather than an accident: `do_el0_sys` walks a table of exactly these (`ctr_read_handler`, `user_cache_maint_handler`, the counter and `CPUID` reads), and `el0_sync` had no row for the class at all - every non-`svc`, non-page-fault trap was reported as `user fault at {far}`, with `FAR_EL1` still holding whichever page fault came last, so the address in that line was *invented*, and the task was ended with `SIGSEGV`, a signal for a fault that has no address. **Measured in three steps on the second machine's desktop image.** (1) The reported `weston` run died at `user fault at 0xfe9ed4f9f000 (ESR 0x6232c061, pc 0xfe9ed60b582c, sp 0x3fd24e00)`, and that ESR is `EC 0x18` with `ISS 0x32c061`: a *read* (`ISS` bit 0) of `op0=3, op1=3, CRn=0, CRm=0, op2=1` - `CTR_EL0` - into `Rt = x3`, i.e. `mrs x3, ctr_el0`, the instruction a flush opens with because it sizes its lines from the register. (2) With that answered the same boot died at the *next* instruction of the same flush: `user fault: trapped MSR of system register 0x5bd9 at pc 0xfe3c1d2ce860 (ESR 0x6212dc56)`, which decodes to `SYS #3, C7, C11, #1` - `DC CVAU`. (The first draft of the report called that one an `MSR`; the report now names the *form* from `op0`, because `0b01` is the system-instruction spelling and `0b11` the register one, and a report that swaps them is a shape that looks like the truth.) (3) `t-el0-sysreg` then pinned both halves on the machine. The kernel answers the pair the way Linux does: the read writes the register's own value into the frame's `Rt` slot and the instruction is skipped (`ctr_read_handler`, minus the sanitization only a heterogeneous system needs), and `DC CVAC`/`CVAU`/`CIVAC` are *promoted* to `DC CIVAC` while `IC IVAU` is issued as itself, both at EL1 where `UCI` does not stop them (`user_cache_maint_handler`). The two failure shapes are told apart and reported as what they are: an address a maintenance operation names that the running space's *tables* do not map is `SIGSEGV` (`arm64_notify_segfault`; the check is `paging::user_page_present`, one question stricter than `user_accessible` because rule 1's absent-but-promised page takes an **EL1** fault, and an unresolvable one stops the machine), and anything with no row is `SIGILL` (`do_el0_undef`) rather than a `SIGSEGV` carrying a borrowed address. What is left of Linux's table is named in docs/uapi/ §5.2 - the counter reads, the `CPUID` reads, `WFI`, and the point-of-persistence pair `DC CVAP`/`CVADP`, which needs `FEAT_DPB` (an EL1 spelling the CPU does not implement is an undefined instruction, so refusing is the safe answer as well as the honest one). Cost, stated rather than hidden: with `UCI` clear every cache line of a flush is a trap into this handler, which is Linux's own trade for the broadcast promotion - llvmpipe's shader flushes pay it one line at a time | the counterfactual, measured: with the two files put back and the pin left in place, `cargo xtask test --arch aarch64` reports `=== SUMMARY pass=170 fail=2`, `=== FAIL t-el0-sysreg exit=139`, and the machine's own line for the test's first `mrs` - `user fault at 0xfedf576e5048 (ESR 0x6232c061, pc 0x25534c, sp 0x3f866c30)`; with them, `=== SUMMARY pass=171 fail=0`, `=== END`, `mpos-kunit 29 passed, 0 failed`. A harness fact that run turned up, worth keeping: the second arm stages every ELF `t-*` file in the *build output* directory, so the first pin's binary - whose source was deleted when the test was renamed - was still staged and still failed (`2 of 172 programs did not pass: t-ctr-el0, t-el0-sysreg`, the extra one being a program with no source; the count is 171 once its artifact goes). The hand-run `cargo xtask run --arch aarch64 --mode terminal` (root on the serial line) now takes weston 16's DRM backend through `gl-renderer`/`EGL`/llvmpipe to a running compositor: `Chosen EGL config details: id: 46 rgba: 8 8 8 0 ... vis_id: XRGB8888 (0x34325258)`, `Output Virtual-1 (crtc 34) video modes: 1280x800@60.0, preferred, current`, `Output 'Virtual-1' enabled with head(s) Virtual-1`, both virtio-input nodes associated, `Compositor capabilities:` listed, `Loading module '/usr/lib/weston/desktop-shell.so'`, `launching '/usr/libexec/weston-keyboard'` and `weston-desktop-shell`, and `wayland-1` bound in `/run/user/0` - where the same boot before this change ended at `Illegal instruction` one second into the renderer's setup.
