| 15.38 | **the second machine's sweep reads `tests.toml` the way the first machine's does, and the file moved into the three sections every config file here uses.** §15.29's selection fix - "on the second machine a *reason* never leaves a program out" - was written for the day the instruments first crossed, and it exempted `external` and `slow` along with them: the aarch64 gate's default sweep staged **178 of 180 and left out 2**, against x86_64's **169 of 180 and 11**, so what a red second-machine gate could still mean included the host's network, the host's resolver and a host that happened to be busy - the exact things the first machine's gate had stopped meaning. Measured before the change: `t-dns`, `t-stdresolve`, `t-udp`, `t-tcp-close`, `t-dhcp` and `t-rawicmp` (`external`) plus `t-fill-ramfs`, `t-forkcost`, `t-netlat` and `t-pipecost` (`slow`) all ran there by default, so the second machine's gate opened sockets to the internet and timed the host. The fix is one question on both arms - `select` stages a program when it is in no group or in a group the run asked for, whichever machine is asking - and the one group that stays machine-specific is an exclusion rather than a reason: a machine's own `gaps`, which `--groups` refuses by name and `--all-groups` still leaves out, because a machine has no call for what it cannot serve. With the reasons shared, the file had to say *where* a group belongs, so `tests.toml` took the three sections `machine.toml` took in §15.12: `[default]` binds both machines, `[x86_64]`/`[aarch64]` are each machine's own half and are **added** to the baseline's group of the same name rather than replacing it (`[aarch64] external = ["t-x"]` is one more program that needs the internet *there*, not a second answer to what `external` means), a key before any header or under an unknown one is refused rather than dropping its group in silence, and the old machine-named group `aarch64` became `gaps` under `[aarch64]` - being written in the machine's own section is what keeps it invisible to the other sweep, so `t-arch-prctl` stays staged on x86_64 exactly as before. `main`'s bail on `--groups`/`--all-groups` for this arm is gone: both flags mean the same thing on either machine now, and a machine-named group is refused in `select` itself, for both arms alike. | 15.39 | **the card reported its own framebuffer's size as the size it was driving, and a compositor reads that back before it decides whether to commit a mode at all.** The symptom was a desktop boot with no picture after the host resized the window, and the kernel had been printing its own diagnosis once per frame: `mpos-dbg: EINVAL from fb_fits_at fb=100 fit=1280x800 programmed=1280x800 advertised=(952, 1135) mode_blob=None ... fb=(10, 952, 1135, 3808, 32, ...) handle_live=true`, **461 times in one run**. Three facts in that line are the whole reading: the framebuffer being refused is 952x1135 (the size the host asked for, not a stale one); it is refused for not fitting 1280x800; and the same boot's own log says what the device is *actually* pointed at - `virtio-gpu: scanning out the compositor's resource 10`. The screen *was* at 952x1135. The card had two answers to "what size is the screen" and the wrong one won: `dims()` reads the `Scanout` struct, `resized()` is its only writer, and `resized()` is called from the **resize** path alone - while a compositor's own resource is presented through the render task's present step → `Gpu::scan_out_client(resource, width, height)`, which had the size in hand and never recorded it. `programmed_mode_dims()` - and so `GETCRTC` and `fb_fits_at`'s ceiling - went on answering the private framebuffer's geometry. What made it fatal is §二's own reading, one field over: `SDRMConnector::connect` takes `getCurrentMode()` (the CRTC's `MODE_ID` blob when it is set, `GETCRTC` otherwise) and calls `setMode` **only for a mode that `memcmp`s different**, so a compositor that read "the mode is already the one the connector offers" sent no mode commit at all and had every frame it did send refused. Measured from the guest's own console with a raw-ioctl probe, both doors at once: `GETCRTC: mode=1280x800` against `MODE_ID blob 1: 68 bytes, 952x1135`, and the verdict `connect(): connector 952x1135 vs current 952x1135 -> memcmp EQUAL -> setMode NOT called`; the same probe's own atomic `ACTIVE`+`MODE_ID` commit came back `rc=0` and moved `GETCRTC` to 952x1135, which is the measurement that named the field. The fix is the invariant the two readings were missing: **the size the card reports as driven is the size of the resource it is presenting.** `VirtioGpuDev` keeps a `presented` field, set by `presenting_client` when the present step points the device at a client's resource and cleared by `presenting_own_framebuffer` when the screen goes back to the driver's own; `driven_dims()` returns it ahead of the private framebuffer's geometry, and `programmed_mode_dims()` answers `driven_dims()`. `dims()` itself still reports the private framebuffer, which is what `/dev/fb0`'s block and `FBIOGET_VSCREENINFO` are about. | both directions measured on the machine, same boot and same compositor: with the old reading **461** `fb_fits_at` refusals and no picture; with this one **0**, and the compositor's resources cycling in the scanout (`scanning out the compositor's resource 10/11/9/10/...` - frames). The probe agrees on the new state where it had disagreed before: `GETCRTC: mode=952x1135`. The pin is in the boot self-test, because the *state* cannot be a gate claim: the render task consults `client_scanout()` only when the console is not painting - the situation a compositor creates by holding the card - so a test boot's console owns the scanout and answers the driver's own geometry. `drm::kunit`'s `the_crtc_reports_the_resource_on_the_screen` therefore puts the field where the render task would and asks the door (half the driven size, retried because the render task repoints the scanout on its own beat), and `t-drm` records why its own resource-backed `SETCRTC` cannot carry the claim. A first draft put it in `t-drm` and the aarch64 sweep caught it (`FAIL: GETCRTC reports the size of the resource on the screen (got 1280, want 96)`) - the gate that says a claim about a compositor's state cannot live in a program that never has one. | **low** - one field and three call sites: a card that answers the size of the buffer it is showing. The gate is unchanged by it (a console-owned machine answers the same size it did before, which is why both arms stayed green while the GL machine changed from black to a picture). |
| 15.40 | **a descriptor's second name denotes the file behind it, so the walk hands the file to every door instead of handing the link.** `/proc/<pid>/fd/<N>` is a magic link: its `readlink` answers a report (the path the descriptor was opened by, or `pipe:[<ino>]` for a channel with no name), and every *path* operation on it is an operation on the file the descriptor holds. Measured on a current host through `syscall(2)` (`.workbuddy/probe_fdlink.c`, Linux 7.2.9 - through the raw call because glibc's `stat` reaches for `statx` and its `open` rewrites): `stat` of the name reports the file (kind, size, inode, and `st_dev` 52 - the file's filesystem) while `lstat` reports the link (`S_IFLNK`, `st_dev` 24 - procfs - size 64, mode derived from the descriptor's direction: 0700 for `O_RDWR`, 0300 write-only, 0500 read-only); `truncate(name, 4)` moves the size the descriptor reads back, a directory is `EISDIR`, a pipe's two ends and a character device are `EINVAL`, and a *deleted* file still truncates because the name is not the path; `fchmodat`/`chownat`/`utimensat`/`access` act on the file; a name inside a path (`/proc/self/fd/<dirfd>/<name>`, glibc's own `*at` fallback shape) resolves to the child of the directory the descriptor holds; `chdir` into a directory's name works; a closed descriptor's name is `ENOENT`; and `open` of the name is a **fresh description of the file**, not a `dup` - `open(name, O_RDWR)` reads from offset 0 while the descriptor stands where it was, and `open(name, O_WRONLY)` on an `O_RDWR|O_APPEND` descriptor reports `F_GETFL` 0x8001, the word the *call* asked for. **What this kernel did was two shapes and only one was right**: `open_opt` cloned the descriptor (position and flags shared, the requested mode ignored) and every other following door acted on the link - `stat` returned a success carrying the link's metadata, `truncate` on a regular file answered `EINVAL`, a directory's `EISDIR` came back `EINVAL`, a name inside a path was `ENOTDIR`. The resolver handed the link back on purpose, and its comment's reason ("opening one has to clone the descriptor rather than reopen a path") is what the measurement refutes. **The fix is one owner**: `MountTable::resolve_walk` replaces a descriptor's payload link with the inode the descriptor holds - the new `crate::vfs::file::descriptor_inode`, `BadFd` -> `NoEntry`, Linux's `ENOENT` - and keeps walking, so a link mid-path is a directory the walk descends into and one at the end is the object every door acts on; a *namespace* link denotes an object `setns` joins rather than an inode, so the walk still hands it back and the door asks; `open_opt` loses its clone branch; `followed_link` (linkat's `AT_SYMLINK_FOLLOW`) collapses to the plain resolution the walk now performs, with `linkat`'s `via_descriptor` test unchanged because it asks the *no-follow* resolution, whose inode is the link. | the pin is `t-fd-name` (37 claims), green on this host first, its `ok:` lines byte-identical on both machines: the `stat`/`lstat` pair, `statx` both ways, the four refusals, the mutators read back through the descriptor, the name inside a path, `chdir`, the fresh description with its own flags and position, the deleted file, the closed descriptor and `/dev/fd/N`. **Its first guest run found a real gap in the kernel, which is the batch's other half**: `truncate` by a *pipe's* name answered `EOPNOTSUPP` - the path spelling's kind test let a pipe through (its inode reports `NodeKind::File`, the vocabulary's only word for an unnamed channel) and reached the node's default, exactly the test `docs/uapi/` §5.1 item 22 added to `ftruncate` and not to this door, which the denotation is what made reachable. The handler now asks `as_pipe` beside the kind, and the path spelling answers Linux's `EINVAL` for both ends. Two more things the run measured rather than asserted: the `st_dev` through the name is the *name's* filesystem here (the guest printed `2 by path, 3 by the name`), and `getcwd` after `chdir` through the name is the string the caller used (Linux canonicalises to `/tmp`) - both named in `docs/uapi/` §5.2, and the cwd one is a property of the cwd model rather than of this name. Gates: x86_64 177 staged, 11 left out, `pass=177 fail=0`; aarch64 176 staged, 12 left out, `pass=176 fail=0`; both `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`; host unit tests 545; `fmt --check` clean; clippy 0 error on both bare-metal targets and the host lib; `depgraph` within the ratchet. | **low** - one walk, one helper, one lost branch in `open_opt`, and every door that follows a name inherits it. |
| 15.41 | **`mknod`'s type field: Linux's whole vocabulary, and a FIFO refused rather than faked.** `docs/uapi/` §5.2's head entry said a `mknodat(S_IFIFO)` here makes a regular file and that "the real decision" is the node. Measuring the door first said the decision was bigger than the FIFO: on a current host through `syscall(2)` (`.workbuddy/probe_mknod.c`, Linux 7.2.9, an unprivileged caller), the `S_IFMT` field has eight values and this kernel answered four of them the wrong way - `S_IFREG` and a **plain permission word** (including mode `0`) are `EINVAL` here and a **regular file** there (`0`, mode `0000` for mode `0`); `S_IFDIR` is `EINVAL` here and **`EPERM`** there (the privilege word, not the argument word); `S_IFSOCK` is `EINVAL` here and a **socket node** there (`0`, unprivileged - which is how a `bind(2)` name can be pre-made, and a kind this kernel already had, since devfs mints socket names and `bind` owns them); and `S_IFIFO` makes a **regular file** here. `S_IFLNK` was `EINVAL` on both, and `S_IFCHR`/`S_IFBLK` are `EPERM` without `CAP_MKNOD` on both (so the two device types needed no change). Beside the type field, the *name*'s answer was wrong in both doors: **a name that already exists is `EEXIST`** - over the same type, over a different one, over a *dangling link* (the question is about the name, so it is asked without following), through `mknodat` and the legacy `mknod` alike - where this kernel answered `0`, on the reading that "the caller wanted the node, not the call". That is a success where Linux refuses, and the idiom it was rationalised by (`[ -c /dev/null ] \|\| mknod /dev/null c 1 3`) carries the guard *because* the call is not idempotent. **The fixes**: `abi::mknod_kind` covers the field's eight values (its unit test asserts Linux's answer for each, including the two refusals' *words*), and both `mknod` doors answer `EEXIST`. **`S_IFIFO` is `EOPNOTSUPP`**: a channel is what §5.2's entry asks for and a channel is what this kernel does not have yet - and a node that is a regular file under a FIFO's name is a wrong *shape*, not a wrong number, because the caller cannot tell it from an empty channel (rule 4: refuse rather than approximate). The contract the refusal stands in front of was measured whole before it was written down (`.workbuddy/probe_fifo.c`, same host): the name is `S_IFIFO` at the name *and* at an opened description, size `0`, `lseek` `ESPIPE`; `open` **parks** until a peer opens the other end (`O_RDONLY` with no writer, `O_WRONLY` with no reader; `O_RDWR` does not park), `O_WRONLY\|O_NONBLOCK` with no reader is `ENXIO`, and a reader parked in `open` counts as a reader - measured, a writer that arrived at 200 ms opened, wrote, and the parked reader's `open` returned to read its four bytes; a nonblocking reader reads `0` with no writer, `EAGAIN` with a live writer that has written nothing and `0` again after the last writer closes; the last reader closing makes a write `EPIPE`; the buffer is discarded when both counts reach zero; and `ftruncate`/`truncate(name)` are `EINVAL`, `fallocate` `EBADF`/`ESPIPE` per the mode's direction, `execve` `EACCES`. What building it needs is written into §5.2 in decision order, with the two places the pipe module has no shape for it named: `open_replacement` takes no flags, and it is the *direction* an open asked for that picks the end. A second divergence was measured and **named rather than fixed**: the `dev` argument is not written on the node - `st_rdev` is the field `mknod` fills (on the host `/dev/null` reports `rdev 1:3`, the number its own creation passed, and an unprivileged caller cannot make a device node to watch it, `EPERM`) - because this kernel has no table from a device number to a driver, so a true word would make `stat` right and the device still absent, and would put a fabricated node in front of the rdev-keyed ioctl families (`'R'`'s entropy words and `FIONREAD` dispatch on that word). | the pin is `t-mknod` (22 claims as root, 21 where the device-node claim is named - a hand host run need not be root), green on this host first - and its **first guest run is what turned the rest of the round up**: the `EEXIST` claim for "a different type" failed with `EOPNOTSUPP` and the mode-`0` claim failed with `0644`, which are the two findings below. Its claims are the field's whole vocabulary with the mode each leaves (including the umask's half: `0666` under `027` is `0640`), the order from both sides (an empty name and an existing name both answer the *type* first), the three `EEXIST` shapes, `ENOENT` for a directory that is not there, the FIFO's refusal in one claim text accepted for Linux's own answer under `MPOS_ON_HOST`, and - as root - a character node made with the `st_rdev` it came back with printed rather than asserted. Its `ok:` lines are identical on both machines. **The order was the first finding**: this kernel asked the *kind* before the name, so `mknodat("", S_IFIFO)` - the mode `t-at-empty-name` uses for its own claim - answered `EOPNOTSUPP` where the empty name owes `ENOENT`, and `mknodat(existing, S_IFIFO)` answered `EOPNOTSUPP` where Linux answers `EEXIST`. The probe then measured the order whole (the type's refusals before the name, `EPERM`/`EINVAL` over an empty name and over an existing one; the directory's `EACCES` before the capability), `abi::mknod_type_is_creatable` became the first question, and the kind moved last. **The mode was the second, and it is a `stat` bug the pin reached through `mknod`**: `mode_of` took a bare `u32`, so a stored `0000` read as "nobody said" and a file `mknod` was told to make with mode `0` - or one `chmod 000`ed - reported `0644` while `may_as` (which asks `keeps_modes`) enforced the empty mode, so the kernel disagreed with itself. The stat doors now pass `Option<u32>` through `reported_mode`, and both halves are claimed (`t-mknod`'s mode-`0` and `chmod 000` claims). The counterfactual is exact: with `mknod_kind`, the two doors and `mode_of` put back, the claims for a regular file, a plain mode, mode `0`, a socket node, `S_IFDIR`, both `EEXIST` shapes, both order claims and the FIFO refusal are the ones that fail. | **low** - two functions and one match, with the replaced behaviour (a FIFO that was a file) named in `docs/uapi/` §5.2 together with the design that would serve it. |
| 15.42 | **a FIFO is a channel, not a file: `mknodat(S_IFIFO)` makes one, and its `open` runs Linux's rendezvous.** §15.41 refused the FIFO rather than fake it and named the two shapes the pipe module lacked - `open_replacement` takes no flags, and the direction an open asked for is what picks the end; this pass meets both and builds the node. The contract was measured whole on a current host through `syscall(2)` (`.workbuddy/probe_fifo.c`, Linux 7.2.9) before any of it was written: the name is `S_IFIFO` to `stat` (size `0`, `nlink` 1) and to an *opened* description's `fstat`, and `lseek` on it is `ESPIPE`; `open(O_RDONLY)` with no writer and `open(O_WRONLY)` with no reader both **park** until a peer opens the other end - and a reader parked in `open` counts as a reader, so the arriving writer opens at once, writes, and the parked reader's `open` returns to read those bytes; `open(O_RDWR)` is its own peer and does not park; `O_RDONLY|O_NONBLOCK` returns at once and `O_WRONLY|O_NONBLOCK` with no reader is `ENXIO`; a nonblocking reader reads `0` with no writer, `EAGAIN` with a live writer that wrote nothing, `0` again once the last writer closes, and `EPIPE` is a write's answer once the last reader closes; the buffer is **discarded** when both counts reach zero; and `ftruncate`/`truncate(name)` are `EINVAL`, `fallocate` `ESPIPE`, `execve` `EACCES`. | **The build is three things the pipe module did not have**: a `NodeKind::Fifo` (with `mode_of`/`dt_type_of` answering `S_IFIFO`/`DT_FIFO`); a `Channel` in `src/vfs/pipe.rs` that carries the pipe's state and ring but is **minted at the name** and joined by every open, taking a reader/writer count at *open* and giving it back at the last close of a **description** (not node drop - a `dup`/`fork` shares the description, which is why the pipe's counts are per description); and `Inode::open_replacement` **gaining the open's `flags`** so the direction picks the end (`O_RDONLY` reader, `O_WRONLY` writer, `O_RDWR` one `Role::Both`, `O_NONBLOCK` choosing park-vs-`ENXIO`). The end an open returns *is* a `PipeEnd`, so `as_pipe`, the size-changing calls and `splice` follow the pipe's rules with no second answer, and its `metadata()` reports the FIFO's kind/ino - which is how an opened description says `S_IFIFO`. The rendezvous is `sched::block_until_interruptible` over a `ready` predicate and a `waiting_open` set every count change wakes; reaching `flags` touched every node that mints on open (`/dev/ptmx`, the ptys, the console tty, the socket node). **One residual is named rather than faked**: a **fatal** signal (`SIGKILL`) while a task is parked ends the task *inside the wait*, so the count that open took is never rolled back (Linux rolls it back on `ERESTARTSYS`) - closing it wants the pending open owned by something the exit pass drops, which an `open` only becomes after it returns, and `t-fifo` releases every parked opener rather than killing one so the pin does not rest on it - **closed by §15.43**, which makes the park *end* for a fatal signal instead of letting the death happen inside it, so the count is given back before the task dies. | the pin is `t-fifo` (33 claims, byte-identical on both machines, green on this host first) for the whole contract, `t-mknod`'s `S_IFIFO` section for the node, and the pipe's four new host tests (`a_fifo_channel_refuses_a_lonely_nonblocking_writer`, `a_fifo_channel_pairs_a_reader_and_a_writer`, `a_fifo_open_readwrite_is_its_own_peer`, `closing_a_fifos_last_two_ends_discards_the_buffer`). **The counterfactual is exact**: with the guest rendezvous put back to "always ready", `t-fifo` fails on the park claims (`pass=178 fail=1`); with `Channel`, `RamFifo` and the `flags` on `open_replacement` absent, its node claims fail too. | **medium** - a new node kind, a channel that lives at the name, an open-time park, and a signature (`open_replacement`) that every node which mints on open had to follow. |
| 15.43 | **a FIFO's parked `open` gives its count back: the *wait* was what had to change, not the owner.** §15.42's one named residual, and the count is not a detail - it is what the arriving peer sees, so a task ended inside the park left the FIFO counting a reader that was gone (every later writer's `open` succeeding into nobody) or a writer that was gone (every later reader getting `EAGAIN` where Linux's answer is end-of-stream). §15.42 read the problem as ownership - "closing it wants the pending open owned by something the exit pass drops, which an `open` only becomes after it returns" - and the measurement says the mechanism is one step earlier. **Measured whole on a current host first** (`.workbuddy/probe_fifo_park.c`, Linux 7.2.9), and the fixture is the measurement: an opener arms its own `ITIMER_REAL` and *then* parks in a blocking `open`, and no peer is ever opened, so the clock is the only thing that can end the park. Four scenarios, printed verbatim: while the opener is parked, `open(O_WRONLY\|O_NONBLOCK)` **succeeds** (fd 3 - the premise: a parked reader counts as a reader); a reader killed by `SIGALRM` inside the park leaves it answering **`-1`/`ENXIO`** (errno 6) instead of a success off a reader that is gone; a parked **writer**'s death leaves a reader reading **`0`** (errno 0) instead of the `EAGAIN` a live writer owes; and after both deaths the rendezvous still pairs (the writer's blocking open 3, one byte written, the reader reads it). **The fix is Linux's own order and it is one wait, not a new owner.** `Channel::rendezvous` parks on the new `sched::block_until_interruptible_rolling_back` instead of `block_until_interruptible`; the two differ in exactly one thing - a **fatal** signal *ends* that park and is left pending, instead of ending the task inside it - so the give-back `Channel::open` already had for a catchable signal runs for `SIGKILL` too, and the death happens where Linux's does: the syscall gate finds the signal still pending on its way out and takes its default action. Linux's `fifo_open` is the same shape one `goto` away - `wait_event_interruptible` returns `-ERESTARTSYS` for a fatal signal as well, the `err_rd:` label gives the count back, and the task dies after the syscall unwinds rather than in the middle of it. Two consequences worth naming because they are what a reader would check: the parked opener's own record in `waiting_open` is now cleaned by the same path (the retain after the wait is reached), and *nothing about the death changes* - the same number ends the task with the same exit code, and a traced task now gets the signal-delivery stop Linux gives it rather than dying without one. The wait is deliberately its own function rather than a change to `block_until_interruptible`: every other park takes nothing that its own body gives back, and the two that take a *task id* (`wait_readable`/`wait_writable`) say in their own doc why a stale one is harmless - a spurious wakeup on a reused id, re-checked before anything happens. | the pin is `t-fifo`'s new killed-opener section - nine claims that are three per direction and three more at the end: the opener's `open` has not returned (150 ms of no report, the same evidence the rendezvous claims use, with the timer as the *only* way out of the park), the fatal signal ended it **inside the park** (`waitpid`'s `WIFSIGNALED`/`SIGALRM`), and what the FIFO answers afterwards (`ENXIO` for a dead reader, `0` for a dead writer); then that a blocking reader still parks and the rendezvous still completes, which is what says the counts are *zero* rather than merely one short. `t-fifo` is 42 claims now, green on this host first (`MPOS_ON_HOST=1`, all 42 `ok:` lines as the sweep's). **The counterfactual is exact**: with the park put back to `block_until_interruptible`, `t-fifo` fails (`pass=179 fail=1`), and the claims that go red are the phantom peer's answers - `a dead parked reader counts for nothing: ENXIO (got 4, errno 0)` first, and then, because that reader is still counted, the *second* scenario's own setup fails with it (`a doomed writer's open does not return (it is parked)` returns at once off the phantom reader, so its death is no longer inside a park and the FIFO's answer for it is the `EAGAIN` a live writer owes - `read -1, errno 11`), followed by five of the file's older claims on the poisoned FIFO (`open(O_WRONLY\|O_NONBLOCK) with no reader is ENXIO (got 3, errno 0)`, the end-of-stream read, the read after the last writer closes, `EPIPE`, and the discarded buffer). The *first* scenario's own death claim - `and the fatal signal ends it inside the park` - is `ok` in both builds, which is the point rather than a hole: this pass changes what is *given back* before the death, not the death. **One draft of that pin hung the sweep, and the failure is worth its own line**: the scenario that proves the counts are back to zero closed its writer *before* waiting for the parked reader's report, and a parked reader is released by the *count* going up - so the writer's arrival and its departure both happened before the reader was ever scheduled, and the reader stayed parked with nobody left to release it (`waitpid` then waits for a task that is waiting for a peer that is gone). The claims keep the writer open across the wait for exactly that reason, which is the shape the rendezvous claims above already had. | **low** - one wait function (a copy of the interruptible one with the fatal `exit` left out and the signal left pending), one call site, and the rollback that was already written for the catchable case; the cost is in the fixture, which needs a clock the *child* owns to make "died inside the park" a measurement rather than a hope. |
| 15.44 | **a child's death is one `SIGCHLD` record, and it is Linux's record - so the second book this kernel kept for it is deleted.** §5.2's `signalfd`/`SIGCHLD` entry named three divergences on the path a descriptor reader walks: (a) a child's exit lived in one table (`SIGCHLD_PENDING`, read as a synthetic `ChildExit` record) *and* a generated `SIGCHLD` was queued beside it, so one death arrived as **two** records; (b) the queued copy's `ssi_code` was `SI_USER` with no status, where Linux's record for the same death is `CLD_EXITED` with the exit code beside it; (c) a death the parent never asked to be signalled for was reported anyway, where Linux queues nothing at all. **Measured whole on a current host first** (`.workbuddy/probe_signalfd_chld.c`, Linux 7.2.9) - one scenario per disposition, **each in its own forked child, because the first draft ran them in one process and measured its own leftovers** (a handler installed by scenario 3 was still installed in scenario 8, whose "handler's record" then named the *previous* scenario's child and carried `si_code=3 si_status=11` - a fixture bug that reads exactly like a kernel finding, and the reason this row's numbers are the second draft's): blocked `SIG_DFL` + a child exiting 7 → **one** record `signo=17 code=1[CLD_EXITED] pid uid=1000 status=7 ptr=0 utime=0 stime=0`, then `EAGAIN`, `wait4` still collecting it and the read *not* collecting it (the record survives the reap); the same with a child that burned 60 ms of CPU → `utime=6 stime=0`; unblocked `SIG_DFL` → **nothing**, before or after the reap; blocked + a handler → one record, and the read **consumes** it (the handler never runs, even after the unblock - measured with the handler's own flag); `SIG_IGN`, blocked or not → nothing, and `waitpid(WNOHANG)` is **`ECHILD`**; a child taking `SIGKILL` → `code=2[CLD_KILLED] status=9` and `WCOREDUMP=0`; a child taking `SIGSEGV` with `RLIMIT_CORE` **0** (set and read back) → `code=3[CLD_DUMPED] status=11` and `WCOREDUMP=1`; and the same death through a handler's `siginfo_t` and through `sigtimedwait` → `si_code=1 si_status=7 si_pid` the child `si_uid` its uid, with the word at offset 24 being the status rather than a `sigval`. **The fix is deletions and one record.** `SIGCHLD_PENDING` with `read_sigchld`, `child_exited_unreaped` and the retains scattered through `take_retired_for`/`take_exit_code`/`record_retired` are gone; the exit path queues the real signal, with `signal::SigInfo::child(pid, ruid, code)` - whose `(si_code, si_status)` pair is `crate::abi::exit_chld`, the function `waitid`'s record already used, **moved into the leaf crate** so the scheduler and the syscall layer cannot spell a child's death two ways (`syscall::linux::abi` re-exports it, as it does the `clone(2)` bits). The queueing predicate is refined to the measured rule: never for a parent that **ignores** `SIGCHLD`, otherwise a handler *or* a blocked signal - the old predicate queued for a blocked `SIG_IGN` too, and the ignore is asked first because generation and delivery ask different questions. The status reaches every door that renders the record: `signalfd`'s `ssi_status` at offset 40, and the `siginfo_t` union at offset 24 for a handler's frame and `sigtimedwait` - where the class test ("a positive code of 1..=6 is the `_sigchld` member, everything else the `_kill`/`_rt` one", Linux's `copy_siginfo_to_user`) is now **one** function, `hal::SignalInfo::chld_union`, instead of an `if` in each machine and a third spelling in the syscall layer. | the pin is `t-signalfd` (46 claims, **no per-machine branch left** - the count it used to claim with a `note:` reads `1` on both; 44 with two notes on a *host* run, where the real uid cannot be moved, `t-waitid`'s shape for that field), green on a current host first, claiming the record field by field (`ssi_status` 7, `ssi_uid`, `ssi_pid`), the unblocked death's `EAGAIN` **before** the reap - the clock is the evidence there, because the reap is what emptied the old second table, so claiming after it would pass on either kernel - with the reap's own exit code straight after it, a `SIGKILL`ed child's `CLD_KILLED`+9, and the frame's rendering of the same record through a `SA_SIGINFO` handler (`si_code`/`si_status`/`si_pid` read out of the handler's `siginfo_t`, recorded in atomics because `printf` is not async-signal-safe). **The counterfactual is the previous commit's kernel**, and it was measured rather than argued: the whole pass stashed (`git stash push` on the twelve source files) and the new `t-signalfd` run against it, `pass=179 fail=1` with exactly the seven claims this pass added or rewrote going red - `its code is CLD_EXITED` (the queued copy is `SI_USER`), `and its ssi_status is the exit code (7)` (0), `and carries the child's real uid, not its effective one (1000)` (0), `one death is one record (the next read is EAGAIN)` (two records), `its death was never queued for this descriptor (EAGAIN)` (the second table hands one over), `as CLD_KILLED, with the signal in ssi_status` (0), and `and the frame's siginfo_t carries CLD_EXITED with the code` (0) - while the fixture claims that are not about the kernel (the uid move, the child's own death) pass on both, which is what tells a pin from a description. **That run also caught the pin's own two defects**, and they are why its numbers are the ones quoted: `ssi_uid` was compared against `getuid()`, which the payload runs as root (0) makes a claim that is true either way, and the unblocked section reaped the child *before* reading, which is precisely what emptied the old second table - so it passed on the kernel it was written to fail on. Both are fixed in the file (the first by `t-waitid`'s moved-id shape, the second by reading before the reap with the clock as evidence), and the list above is the re-run. **Three residuals are named rather than hidden** (`docs/uapi/` §5.2 carries them with these numbers): an **ignored** child is reaped by the kernel on Linux (`ECHILD`) and kept as a zombie here; `si_utime`/`si_stime` stay zero where Linux reports the child's CPU in ticks (`utime=6` for 60 ms, `0` for a child that only exits, so the gap is about a child that *worked*, and the totals exist here - the record simply does not carry them); and the **`CLD_DUMPED`/`WCOREDUMP`** half, where the measurement also **corrects a reason**: the core bit is not about a file being written (a `SIGSEGV` child reports it with `RLIMIT_CORE` 0), so `wait_status`'s comment ("mpos writes no core files") explained the wrong thing - with the boundary named that this host cannot separate, its `core_pattern` being a pipe, so a dump is attempted whatever the limit says. | **medium** - a table deleted, a predicate corrected, a field threaded through four renderers, and one rule (the union's class) given a single owner; the cost is in the fixture, which needed a scenario per disposition in its own process before it measured anything. |

| 15.45 | **the host-testable core's contracts are executable now, and the gate runs them.** §11.2 Step F settles the criterion that makes the host the place an arch-independent module is *pinned* — a module is host-compiled when its logic touches no hardware, no port I/O, no virtio ring and no real clock, and then its dependencies must be host-compiled too — but `cargo test --lib` never compiled a doc example, so every fenced block in `src/` and `crates/*/src` was prose a reader could be misled by and nothing would say so. This pass makes **39** of them run, each on a function whose contract is worth meeting in the shape its caller meets it: the *order* an errno depends on (`Credentials::capbset_drop` asks the privilege question before the range one, so the same out-of-range number is `EPERM` to a caller without `CAP_SETPCAP` and `EINVAL` to one with; `seccomp::supported` refuses any word with a data half, which is Linux's own answer for `ERRNO|1`), the signed comparison that makes `KILL_PROCESS` (`0x8000_0000`) outrank `ALLOW` (`0x7fff_0000`) where an unsigned minimum would let `ALLOW` win a kill, the `u64` bit arithmetic of a signal set (`bit(64)` is the word's last bit and signal 0 has none), the real-time queue's per-number FIFO over a bounded ring, the `default_action` table's two deliberate "do nothing"s (`SIGCHLD`, `SIGWINCH`), a tar member built the way `tar` builds one, `/proc/<pid>/maps`' name column at its fixed offset, and twelve leaf crates' own facts (`abi`'s rlimit/clone/`exit_chld`, `acpi`'s table walk, `binfmt`'s shebang, `bootinfo`'s allocatable kinds, `cred`'s drop, `date`'s civil calendar, `evdev`'s grab/revoke, `link`'s `sockaddr_ll`/Ethernet/ring, `pci`'s BAR, `seccomp`'s ordering, `term::wire::cut`, `vm`'s alignment). **`run_unit_tests` now walks the same `LEAF_CRATES` list a second time with `--doc`** — a second invocation rather than a flag, because cargo refuses to mix `--doc` with `--lib` — so the crate the unit tests exercise is the crate whose examples run, and the existing `tests::every_leaf_crate_is_tested` keeps that list honest for both. Turning the compiler loose on the examples also *found* what had been invisible while they were only prose: `src/lib.rs`'s own usage line was an indented block, which rustdoc reads as a Rust test, so it is fenced ` ```text ` now — the first change that cares. | `cargo test --doc` over `mpos` and the 22 leaf crates: **39 examples, 39 passed, exit 0** (`mpos` 16, the leaves 23); `cargo test --lib` unchanged; `cargo test -p mpos-xtask` 121 passed; `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo xtask test` `pass=180 fail=0` (191 built, 180 staged), `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33. **The counterfactual is the gate's own invocation**: with the `--lib` run alone, a doctest that contradicted its function compiled to nothing and the gate was still green — which is why the second invocation is the change and the examples alone would not have been one. | **low** — documentation plus one gate invocation; no production code moved. |

| 15.46 | **`FIONREAD` answers Linux's per-type byte count on a regular file and a pipe, and the subtraction past the end is not clamped.** `docs/uapi/` §5.2's entry said this kernel answered `ENOTTY` for everything that is not the entropy pair, and that closing it needs a per-descriptor byte count - "a pipe has one; a file has `size - offset`". The measurement says the answer is not one rule but one *per file type*, and the whole descriptor zoo was run on a current host before any of it was written (`.workbuddy/probe_fionread.c`, Linux 7.2.9): a **regular file** answers `i_size - f_pos` - `10` at offset 0 of a ten-byte file, `6` after a four-byte read, `0` at end of file, and **`-10`** at offset 20 of that file, because Linux's `put_user(i_size_read(inode) - file->f_pos, int __user *)` truncates the wide subtraction to a signed 32-bit word rather than saturating (and a `/proc` file, a regular file by `S_ISREG` with size 0, answers `-f_pos`); a **pipe** answers its queued bytes on *both* ends (`5` after a five-byte write); the **entropy pair** answers `EINVAL` (which this kernel already did); a **directory**, an **`eventfd`**, **`/dev/null`** and **`/dev/zero`** answer `ENOTTY`; and a **`socketpair`** end answers its queued bytes while a **pty** answers `0` on both ends. The pointer is looked at only where there is a value to write: with the same bad pointer a file and a pipe are `EFAULT` while a directory stays `ENOTTY` and the pool `EINVAL`. **The fix is the dispatch the node already answers**: `as_pipe()` yields the queued bytes; the description's own position and `NodeKind::File` yield `size - pos`, written as `size.wrapping_sub(pos) as u32 as i32` to match Linux's truncation (not a saturating subtraction, which the `-10` claim is the counterfactual against); and everything else - plus the socket and tty kinds the probe measured - keeps the honest `ENOTTY`. | the pin is `t-ioctl`'s new section, **nine claims**, green on this host first (`MPOS_ON_HOST=1`): the file's four offsets (the negative included), the pipe's two ends, the NULL-destination `EFAULT`, and the directory's `ENOTTY` - so the section measures the same thing on real Linux as in a sweep. **Two kinds stay named in `docs/uapi/` §5.2** with these numbers rather than faked: a socket (a `socketpair` end or a network socket) and a tty/pty. | **low** - one arm of `handle_ioctl`, one enriched test section, and a `docs/uapi/` entry moved from "not yet landed" to "landed with two kinds named". |

| 15.47 | **`getcwd` derives the cwd's name from the directory, so a symlinked `chdir` reads back the target and a `..` taken after it climbs from the target's parent.** `docs/uapi/` §5.2 named it: this kernel's cwd was the path *string* `chdir` stored (lexically normalised, no link followed), so a `chdir` through a symlink - or a descriptor's `/proc/self/fd/N` name - read back as the argument the caller used, where Linux's `getcwd` walks the dentry chain and answers the canonical path. Measured whole on a current host first (`.workbuddy/probe_getcwd.c`, Linux 7.2.9): a regular path is itself; a **symlink** at the end *or* in the middle of the argument answers the target; a descriptor's `/proc/self/fd/N` name and a **`fchdir` through a symlinked descriptor** answer the canonical path; a `..` taken after a link that **crosses to another parent** climbs from the *target's* parent (`/tmp/gcprobe/real`), not the link's (`/tmp/gcprobe2`); a path with `./` and a trailing `..` folds; the root answers `/`; a name that is a file, not a directory, is `ENOTDIR`; and a cwd **removed underneath the caller** makes `getcwd` answer `ENOENT`. **The fix is one place** - `adopt_cwd`, the single writer of the cwd - which now stores the name `MountTable::path_of` finds for the directory (the bounded walk from the caller's root that `/proc/mounts` already uses) instead of the argument, with the caller's spelling kept as the fallback for a node the tree cannot name; that also fixed the `..`-through-a-link half, because relative resolution reads the same string. The local `path_of` in `file.rs` - a DFS from the *machine* root that only `fchdir`'s fallback used, and not chroot-aware - is deleted: the descriptor's label is now the fallback and the one search lives in the mount table, so the two doors cannot disagree about which table they search. | the pin is `t-fd-name`'s rewritten and new claims, green on this host first (`MPOS_ON_HOST=1`): its descriptor-name `chdir` now *claims* (not prints) `getcwd == /tmp`, a `chdir` through a symlink reads the target's own name, a cross-parent link reads the target too, and the `..` after it climbs to `/tmp`. | **low** - one function (`adopt_cwd`), one fallback simplified, a dead DFS deleted, and a `docs/uapi/` entry moved from "not yet landed" to "landed with two consequences named". |

| 15.48 | **`FIONREAD` answers the socket and the terminal queues next, and the "a pty answers `0`" that §15.46 wrote down was a canonical-mode artifact.** §15.46 landed the regular file and the pipe and left two kinds *named*: a socket and a tty/pty, both refused `ENOTTY`. This pass serves them, and measuring them first changed the entry it was written against. **Measured whole on a current host first** (`.workbuddy/probe_fionread2.c`, `probe_netinq.c`, `probe_ttyinq.c`, `probe_unixinq.c`, `probe_ttyasync.c`, Linux 7.2.9): a **stream** channel answers the *whole* readable stream, not the first write - an AF_UNIX `socketpair` end with 3 + 4 written answers **7**, the writer's own end **0**, and a TCP connection answers its whole readable queue (**8** after two writes, **4** after a four-byte read, then **6** across the peer's half-close); a **datagram** channel answers only its **first** pending message - an AF_UNIX `socketpair`/UDP pair with 5 then 9 queued answers **5**, an empty queue **0**; a **fresh, unconnected** stream socket answers **0** and a **listener** refuses **`EINVAL`** (TCP and AF_UNIX alike, and *before* the argument is read); a **terminal** answers its input queue - a canonical `"hi"` with no newline **0**, `"hi\n"` **3**, `"ab\ncd\n"` **6**, raw `"hello"` **5** and **3** after a two-byte read - while a pty **master** answers what the slave has printed (**3**). **The correction**: §15.46 and `docs/uapi/` §5.2 after it said "a pty answers `0` on both ends"; that was the earlier probe's canonical-mode reading with raw bytes still in the buffer, and a clean per-case probe shows the input queue instead (the same lesson `docs/uapi/` §5.2 now carries). **The fix is one owner for the answer - `Inode::fionread`, a default-`None` trait method** (`src/vfs/mod.rs`) - because the queue belongs to the node and the ioctl layer holds only an `Arc<dyn Inode>`: `src/vfs/pipe.rs` (`PipeEnd`, `DuplexEnd`), `src/vfs/tty.rs` (`LineDiscipline::readable_bytes` = queued answer + completed/raw bytes, and `Tty`), `src/vfs/pty.rs` (`PtyMaster` the `to_master` queue - *not* the slave answer its `as_tty` face would otherwise lend it - and `PtySlave` the slave terminal), `src/net/unix.rs` (a message socket's first `recvq` datagram, a stream through its `end_of()`, a listener `EINVAL`, a fresh one **0**), `src/net/mod.rs` (`net::fionread`: `recv_queue` for TCP, `peek` for UDP, `TcpListen` `EINVAL`, `Inet6` 0, `Raw` `None`) and `src/net/socket.rs` (`SocketInode`). The `0x541B` arm keeps only what a node cannot answer - the entropy `EINVAL`, the regular file's `size - pos`, and the kind refusal (`ENOTTY` for a directory and for a **netlink**/raw socket, whose own table the stack cannot serve) - and a node's own refusal comes *before* the pointer, as Linux's `tcp_ioctl` checks the state before its `put_user`. | the pins are four programs, each keeping the claim beside its fixture: `t-ioctl` (a `socketpair` stream end 0 then 7, its writer 0, a datagram end 5-not-9), `t-pty` (canonical incomplete **0** - proved by the echo landing on the master first, so the 0 is the exclusion and not a race - completed **3**, two lines **6**, raw **5** then **3** after a read, master **3**), `t-tcp-listen` (a fresh connection 0, 8 queued, 4 after a read, 6 across a half-close, the listener `EINVAL`) and `t-udp-loopback` (5 then 9 queued answers 5, then 4, then 0), all green on this host first. The host delivers a pty master's write through an asynchronous flip buffer (measured: `slave=0` right after the write, `3` after 30 ms), so the tty claims carry a bounded wait rather than a hope. | **medium** - one trait method with six overrides, an arm reduced to two answers, and a corrected measurement; the reach is small (no dispatch edge, no new node type), the cost is that a per-kind answer now has six owners behind one trait door. |

| 15.49 | **A signalled death is `CLD_DUMPED` with `WCOREDUMP` when the signal's default action dumps core *and the child was dumpable*.** The third residual of §15.44 (`docs/uapi/` item 29; §5.2): this kernel recorded `CLD_KILLED` and left the core bit clear for *every* signalled death, where Linux tells two apart. **Measured whole on a current host first**, in two probes, because the rule has two halves. `.workbuddy/probe_coredump_signals.c` (Linux 7.2.9, `RLIMIT_CORE` 0) walks every signal whose default action ends a task and reads back both `wait4`'s status word and `waitid`'s `si_code`: exactly ten — `SIGQUIT`, `SIGILL`, `SIGTRAP`, `SIGABRT`, `SIGBUS`, `SIGFPE`, `SIGSEGV`, `SIGXCPU`, `SIGXFSZ`, `SIGSYS` — are `code=3[CLD_DUMPED]`/bit-set and every other fatal signal (`SIGKILL`, `SIGTERM`, `SIGPIPE`, …) is `code=2[CLD_KILLED]`/bit-clear (the set is `sig_kernel_coredump`'s), and the bit ignores `RLIMIT_CORE` — which is what showed the reason this tree's `wait_status` comment gave ("mpos writes no core files") was answering a different question. `.workbuddy/probe_wcore_dumpable.c` then separated the *signal* from a *dump attempted*: under `PR_SET_DUMPABLE 0` — which makes `do_coredump()` bail at its first `__get_dumpable()` check, where this host's **pipe** `core_pattern` (`\|/usr/lib/systemd/systemd-coredump …`) defeats `RLIMIT_CORE` — the same `SIGSEGV` death is `CLD_KILLED`/bit-clear, while dumpable `1` sets it, and the flag is the *child's* (a child that sets its own back to 1 reports `CLD_DUMPED` under a non-dumpable parent). The kernel's own `SUID_DUMP_ROOT` value, 2, is *not* settable through the door — Linux answers `EINVAL`, measured — so 1-versus-0 is the pair the door can show; the predicate reads `!= 0`, `__get_dumpable`'s own comparison. **The fix threads the classification, computed once at the death**: the signal half is `crates/abi`'s `signal_dumps_core` (host-tested exhaustively over `1..=31`), `exit_chld` takes a `dumped: bool` beside the code (neither half is recoverable from the code once the child is gone), the exit path computes `dumped = code < 0 && signal_dumps_core(-code) && child's dumpable != 0` while the `mm` is still there (`release_exited` takes it a few lines later), and carries it on both records a `wait` reads — `Retired`/`WaitEvent::Exited` (with `take_retired_current` handed over whole rather than as a bare code) and `signal::SigInfo::child` — so `wait4`'s `wait_status` and `waitid`'s record read one value and cannot classify one death two ways. | `t-wait4` gains a signal-death section (a dumpable `SIGSEGV` sets `WCOREDUMP`, `SIGTERM` clears it, and a `PR_SET_DUMPABLE 0` `SIGSEGV` clears it too — the second half of the rule) and `t-signalfd` two blocks (the same death is `CLD_DUMPED` through the descriptor, `CLD_KILLED` when the child is non-dumpable), both green on this host first; the leaf crate's `a_core_dumping_signal_is_cld_dumped_not_cld_killed` walks `1..=31` and the `wait_status`/`waitid_info` host tests carry both halves. Gate: `pass=180 fail=0 (180 built)`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33. The counterfactual is exact: with only the kernel half reverted (the eight source files), both programs kept, the sweep is `pass=178 fail=2 (180 built)` and the kernel's own `mpos-kunit` line unmoved — the two reds are exactly the new claims, `t-signalfd`'s `FAIL: as CLD_DUMPED, not CLD_KILLED, with the signal in ssi_status` and `t-wait4`'s `FAIL: and WCOREDUMP (0x80) is set for a core-dumping signal`. | **low** — one leaf predicate, one signature widened through the death path, and the classification carried on two existing records; no new table and no dispatch edge. |

| 15.50 | **A `SIGCHLD` record carries the child's CPU in `si_utime`/`si_stime`.** Item 29's second residual (`docs/uapi/` §5.2): Linux fills the `_sigchld` member's two `clock_t`s with the child's CPU in `USER_HZ` ticks (`do_notify_parent`'s `nsec_to_clock_t(tsk->utime + tsk->signal->utime)`), and this kernel wrote zero on the one record every door renders. **Measured whole on a current host first** (`.workbuddy/probe_sigchld_cputime.c`, Linux 7.2.9, `_SC_CLK_TCK` 100): a child that burns ~100 ms of CPU is `si_utime` **9**, `si_stime` **0** through a blocked-`SIGCHLD` `sigwait`, through a `signalfd` read and through a `SA_SIGINFO` handler's frame; a child that only exits is `0`/`0` (so the gap is a child that *worked* - the totals exist here, the record simply did not carry them). The probe's third finding is what the other door does, and it is not this door: **`waitid` never fills them** - `struct waitid_info` has no such field, the syscall writes exactly six words (signo/errno/code/pid/uid/status) and leaves the caller's other bytes untouched, so every `waitid` reads `0` for the same 100 ms child the record reports as 9. **The fix is one conversion and one field pair carried to every renderer.** The `USER_HZ` conversion - `ticks * 100 / TICK_HZ`, truncated, Linux's `nsec_to_clock_t` one unit up - moves into the leaf crate as `abi::clock_ticks_to_user_hz`/`abi::USER_HZ` (a `u128` intermediate so a long uptime cannot wrap; host-tested), and `proc.rs`'s private `times()` copy delegates to it so a `times(2)` total and a child's record cannot come to different units. `signal::SigInfo` gains `utime`/`stime`, already converted because the sender computes them once; the exit path fills them from the group's `Mm::cpu_all()` where the `mm` still holds the counters (the group's totals are Linux's `own + signal->utime`, a dead thread already folded in), and the three doors write them: `signalfd`'s `ssi_utime`/`ssi_stime` at offsets 56/64, and the `siginfo_t` `_sigchld` member's `si_utime`/`si_stime` at 32/40 for a handler's frame and `sigtimedwait` alike. The other door is left alone on purpose: `write_waitid_info` clears the whole record where Linux writes six words, and its `si_utime`/`si_stime` staying zero is Linux's own answer, now recorded in its comment. | the pin is `t-signalfd` - a child that only exits is `0`/`0`, a child that burns 100 ms is `ssi_utime > 0` through the descriptor and `si_utime > 0` through the handler's frame (the frame half shares the block that already installs the handler), green on this host first - plus a host test on the conversion in `mpos-abi`. The program was **62** claims on a sweep and **60** with two notes on a host run (measured). Gate: `pass=180 fail=0 (180 built)`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33. The counterfactual is exact: with only the kernel half reverted (the nine source files) and the pin kept, the sweep is `pass=179 fail=1 (180 built)` and the kernel's own line unmoved at `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed` - the two reds are exactly the new claims, `FAIL: the record carries the child's CPU (ssi_utime > 0)` and `FAIL: and the frame carries the child's CPU (si_utime > 0)` (the `0`/`0` claim passes on both kernels, being a shape guard rather than a counterfactual - the honest shape of "does this field carry the child's CPU" is one claim that a zero record satisfies either way plus one that a zero record fails). | **low** - one leaf helper, one field pair on `SigInfo`, and three writers that already had the record in hand; no new table and no dispatch edge. |

| 15.51 | **`wait4`/`waitid` fill their `struct rusage`: the child's CPU, for every event they *report*.** The last residual of item 29 (`docs/uapi/` §5.1 item 34; §5.2), and the one a caller could not work around: both doors read their status/record arguments and never touched the fourth/fifth - `rustix`'s `waitpid` hardcodes `NULL` there, so nothing in the sweep noticed, and a program that asked for the child's CPU was handed success and a record it had to fill itself. **Measured whole on a current host first** (`.workbuddy/probe_wait4_rusage.c`, Linux 7.2.9, `sizeof(struct rusage)` 144, fourteen scenarios each in its own forked child): a death fills it with the child's **own plus its own reaped children** - Linux's `RUSAGE_BOTH`, not the child's own totals, which a grandchild's 200 ms of CPU shows arriving through the child that reaped it; the number is *exactly* what `getrusage(RUSAGE_CHILDREN)` reports once the reap has folded it in; a **stop** (`WUNTRACED`) and a **resume** (`WCONTINUED`) fill it too, and so does `waitid` through `WEXITED`, `WSTOPPED` and `WCONTINUED`; a `WNOHANG` that finds nothing running, and an `ECHILD`, both leave a 0xAA-seeded record **untouched**; and a `NULL` rusage is "not interested", not an error. **The two doors also differ, and that is Linux's own order**: `wait4` writes `*status` inside the wait and copies the record afterwards, so an unwritable `rusage` pointer is `EFAULT` with the status word already written and the child already collected; `waitid` copies the record **before** it writes the six-word `siginfo_t`, so the same bad pointer leaves the caller's own bytes there. **The fix is one writer and one carried pair.** `proc.rs` gains one `write_rusage` (the two opening `timeval`s off a tick count, the rest zeroed) that `handle_getrusage` now also calls, so seconds-and-microseconds off a tick count has one owner instead of two; `wait4` reads `args[3]` and `waitid` `args[4]`, and the write happens only where an event was *reported* - after `*status` in `finish_wait`/`write_wait_status`, before the record in `handle_waitid`. The CPU rides the event: `Retired` and all three `WaitEvent` variants gain `utime`/`stime` (raw internal ticks, because a `rusage` is a microsecond `timeval` and a `USER_HZ` pair would be the wrong unit), a death takes them off the record - read at the exit where the `mm` is still the live group's - and a stop or a resume reads them from the live table inside the scan's own borrow, through a new `Mm::cpu_both()` beside `cpu_all()`. The fourteen byte/page counters stay zero, which is the residual `getrusage` has carried since it landed and now carries here too. | the pins are `t-wait4` (the death fill, the `RUSAGE_CHILDREN` delta the same reap moved - the two sides truncate nanoseconds to microseconds independently, so the equality carries that rounding and nothing else: measured, 200 reaps land in 0..=2 us, `.workbuddy/probe_wait4_delta.c` - the unmeasured counters reading zero, a `WNOHANG` that leaves the seed, a `WUNTRACED` stop and a `WCONTINUED` resume each filled, and the `EFAULT` order with the child already collected) and `t-waitid` (the exit fill and the `WNOHANG` no-report half), all green on this host first; the record itself moved to `mpos_user_tests` because a second and third program needed it, and the CPU burner with it. Gate: `pass=180 fail=0 (180 built)`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33 - and the second machine's arm of the same gate, which these pins are in the `[default]` group for, is `pass=179 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 0. The counterfactual is exact: with only the kernel half reverted (the six source files) and the pins kept, the sweep is `pass=178 fail=2 (180 built)` and the kernel's own `mpos-kunit` line unmoved - the two red programs are exactly `t-wait4` and `t-waitid`, and every red claim is a new one: `t-wait4`'s `FAIL: the record carries the child's CPU (ru_utime > 0)`, `FAIL: and it is the CPU the reap folded into RUSAGE_CHILDREN`, `FAIL: the unmeasured counters are zero, not the caller's own seed`, `FAIL: an unwritable rusage pointer is EFAULT`, `FAIL: and the stopped child's CPU is in the record` and `FAIL: and the resumed child's CPU is in the record too`, and `t-waitid`'s `FAIL: and the record carries the child's CPU`. The shape guards pass on both kernels, as they should: the `WNOHANG` no-report half, the `WUNTRACED`/`WCONTINUED` record itself, and `waitid`'s report. | **low** - one shared `rusage` writer, one tick pair carried on the two records a `wait` already reads, and two argument reads; no new table and no dispatch edge. |

| 15.52 | **A full real-time signal queue answers `EAGAIN` from `sigqueue`, and the bound is the target user's `RLIMIT_SIGPENDING`.** Stage C's last named divergence (`docs/uapi/` §5.1 item 35; §5.2): the per-task ring **dropped** a copy past `RT_QUEUE_LEN` and every door answered success, so a caller's queued signal was gone with nothing to tell it - silence where Linux has an errno. **Measured whole on a current host first** (`.workbuddy/probe_sigpending{1,5,7,8,9,10}.c`, Linux 7.2, one scenario per process so no scenario's quota leaked into the next): at a soft limit of N, exactly N `sigqueue`s of one real-time number are taken and the **N+1th is `EAGAIN`** (N=8 and N=4, errno 11); the quantity is the **user's total** - a *second* real-time number meets the same wall, and a same-uid child that queued nothing is refused while the parent holds the quota (and queues its own N again once the parent drains); the limit that governs is the **target's**, not the sender's (a sender lowered to 1 still delivered to a target whose limit was 4, while a send to *itself* was refused); a **standard** signal is never refused (`sigqueue(SIGUSR1)` succeeds at the limit and the bit is pending); and **`kill` never reports** - at the same moment it answers 0 and its extra copies are simply not queued. **The fix is one enum and one resource row.** `send_signal_info` answers `Sent { Taken, NoTask, QueueFull }` instead of a bool, `enqueue` answers whether the ring took the copy, and the budget is read *before* the mutable borrow because the count walks the table: `queued_for_user` sums `RtQueue::queued()` over the tasks of the **target's** real uid, against the **target's** `rlimits[RLIMIT_SIGPENDING]`, after the "discarded anyway" rules - an ignored signal needs no slot, which is `prepare_signal`'s order. `rt_sigqueueinfo` maps `QueueFull` to `EAGAIN`; `kill`/`tgkill` and the group/process fan-outs keep answering 0. `mpos_abi::default_rlimits` gains row 11 = `RT_QUEUE_LEN` both ways, because the records live in that ring and so `getrlimit` should tell a caller the truth about this machine; Linux's own default (`max_threads / 2`) is policy rather than ABI. | the pin is `t-rt-signal-queue` - the fill stopping at the lowered limit, the copy past it `EAGAIN`, a second RT number meeting the same total, `kill` answering 0 at the same moment, a forked sibling with an empty queue refused too, and the soft limit restored; its earlier three sections are what empty the queue first, and it lowers only the **soft** limit because the hard one cannot be raised back without privilege even on this host - plus `abi.rs`'s `rlimits_report_the_startup_defaults_and_refuse_unknown_rows`, which reads row 11 against `RT_QUEUE_LEN` itself. 19 claims, green on this host first. Gate: `pass=180 fail=0 (180 built)`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33 - and the second machine's arm of the same gate, which these `[default]`-group pins also run under, is `pass=179 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 0. The counterfactual is exact: with only the kernel half reverted (the six source files) and the pin kept, the sweep is `pass=179 fail=1 (180 built)` with the kernel's own `mpos-kunit` line unmoved - the one red program is `t-rt-signal-queue`, its four red claims are all new (the fill's stop, its `EAGAIN`, the second number, the sibling), and every shape guard (the FIFO drain, the two kills, the handler-per-copy, `kill`'s 0, the setrlimit and its restore) passes on both kernels. | **low** - one enum at the send door, one accessor on the ring, and one resource row; no new table and no dispatch edge. |

| 15.53 | **A device node asks `CAP_MKNOD` — one capability, one kind of node, in Linux's place in the order.** Item 25's second residual (`docs/uapi/` §5.1 item 36; §5.2): both `mknod` doors built a device node for **any** caller where Linux answers `EPERM` without the capability. The entry that named it also named why it was not one line — a `setuid` here did not re-derive the capability sets, so a dropped-privilege process would still have held `CAP_MKNOD` and the gate would have refused nobody — and that prerequisite has since landed on its own (item 4: `set_current_identity` runs `Credentials::on_identity_change`, the measured `cap_emulate_setxuid` rule `t-caps` drives). **Measured whole on a current host first** (`.workbuddy/probe_mknod_cap.c`, Linux 7.2.9, run as an unprivileged caller — every chr/blk answer here is the capability's directly — one scenario per answer): `S_IFCHR` and `S_IFBLK` are **`EPERM`**; `S_IFREG`, `S_IFIFO` and `S_IFSOCK` are **served** to a caller holding not one capability, so the gate is a *device* node's and not a create's; the character node whose `dev` is **`0`** is served too — the *whiteout* overlayfs spells that way — and the exception is the character type's alone, since `makedev(0, 1)`, `makedev(1, 0)` and a *block* node with the same `0` are all `EPERM`; and the question comes **after every refusal above** (a name that exists is `EEXIST`, a directory the caller may not write is `EACCES`, a path that is not there is `ENOENT`). **The fix is one predicate and one gate per door.** `abi::mknod_needs_capability(mode, dev)` decides it — the mode's type, and the low 32 bits that are the whole of the uapi's `dev` argument — and each door asks it where its own comment had already reserved the place: after `may_create_in` and the kind, as the last question the call has, against `current_credentials().has_effective(crate::cred::CAP_MKNOD)`. **One corner is measured and named rather than fixed**: Linux's `vfs_mknod` asks `capable(CAP_MKNOD)`, which is the **initial** user namespace, so a caller that has entered a user namespace — uid 0 in it, the whole set read back — still cannot make a device node on a filesystem the parent namespace owns (`.workbuddy/probe_mknod_userns.sh`: `touch` in the same directory answers `0` while `mknod c 1 3` answers `EPERM`, and the `dev 0` whiteout is served there as it is outside), where this kernel's checks ask the caller's own namespace; the gap is the capability model's (`crates/cred`'s `on_enter_user_ns`, §6.3) rather than this call's, and §5.2 names it. | the pin is `t-mknod`'s capability section — 14 new claims (36 where the program had 22), the same 36 on both machines: the window's two directories (one `0777`, one `0555`), the name it finds already there, the node a *capable* caller makes, the lowering, the character and block refusals, the whiteout with the block-`0` refusal beside it (the control that puts the refusals on the capability rather than on the directory), the three types that need none, the three ordering refusals, and the raise-back with the same node served again. It runs on both machines *and* on this host, where it makes the same claims as an ordinary user — a hand run is already without the capability, so this section has no host-run hole in it — and every `ok:` line it prints is identical on the two machines. The predicate's own pin is `abi.rs`'s `mknod_mode_field_picks_the_node_kind`, which now asserts Linux's answer for each type *and* for the two `dev`s that decide a character node. Gate: x86_64 `pass=180 fail=0 (180 built)`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33; the second machine's arm of the same gate is `pass=179 fail=0` with `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`. The counterfactual is exact: with only the kernel half reverted (three source files) and the pin kept, the sweep is `pass=179 fail=1 (180 built)` with the kernel's own `mpos-kunit` line unmoved — the one red program is `t-mknod`, and its three red claims are `a character node is EPERM without CAP_MKNOD (got 0, errno 0)`, `and a block node too (got 0, errno 0)` and `but the exception is the character type's: a block node with dev 0 is EPERM (got 0, errno 0)` — each answered `0`, the wrong-shape success this pass removes — while the whiteout, the three no-capability types and all three ordering claims stay green on both kernels. | **low** — one predicate in the leaf, one constant, and two gates placed where the doors' comments already said the question belonged; no new table, no dispatch edge. |

| 15.54 | **A procfs link reports its *own* metadata, and the descriptor directory's size is the number of descriptors it lists.** §5.2's fourth named residual of item 24: `lstat` of `/proc/<pid>/fd/<N>` is the door that stops at the link, and this kernel answered the *kind's* default there — `S_IFLNK \| 0777` with size `0`, because `reported_mode` reads a filesystem that keeps no modes as its kind's default (the entry's own text said `ProcLink` reported the trait's `mode()` of `0`; the counterfactual run is what shows the word a caller actually read back was `0o777`). **Measured whole on a current host first** (`.workbuddy/probe_proclink_mode.c`, Linux 7.2.9, `lstat` through `syscall(2)` so a libc cannot rewrite what is being measured): a descriptor's link carries the direction its descriptor was opened in as two *bits* — `0500` read-only, `0300` write-only, `0700` both (a socketpair's end included, and a directory's `O_RDONLY` descriptor `0500`) — and `0` for one opened with neither, which is what an `O_PATH` descriptor reports, while every magic link that names something other than a descriptor (`exe`, `cwd`, `root`, `ns/*`, `/proc/self`) is `0777`; the size is **64** for every descriptor link whatever the target's text is (`readlink` on the same name answers 9 bytes for a `/dev/null` link, 33 for `exe`), and `0` for the others; and the *directory* the links live in is `S_IFDIR \| 0500` rather than the `0555` every other proc directory carries, with a size that is the **number of descriptors it lists** — 3 descriptors answer 3 and 23 answer 23 at once, and a hole is a number rather than a span, so fds 0 and 200 with a third open answer 3. | **The fix is two fields and one lookup**, because the mode belongs to the link and the direction lives on the description: `sched::descriptor_access(pid, fd)` reads `File::access` off the live descriptor table (the scheduler half, host-stubbed like `descriptor_numbers` beside it), `fd_link_mode` maps the three `ACC_*` words onto the two bits (a pure function), and `ProcLink::metadata`/`mode` report the link's own halves. `ProcDir` gains `mode` and `size_is_count` — zero and false are "the kind's default", which every other directory in the tree keeps — and exactly one constructor passes them, `descriptor_dir(pid)`, so the one directory whose metadata is about its contents says so in one place. The `64` is procfs's own constant rather than a property of the file the descriptor holds, which is why it sits on the link and not on the description. **Two things stay named** (`docs/uapi/` §5.2): an `O_PATH` descriptor — a flag this kernel drops and serves as the open it was asked for — would report the direction its open actually got where Linux reports neither bit; and every *other* proc/sys node still answers the kind's default where Linux answers the node's own mode (directories `0555`, `/proc/<pid>/ns` `0511`, `map_files` `0500`, read-only files `0444`, writable ones `0644`, `mem` `0600`, and `/proc/cmdline`'s `st_size` its own line's length), which is a classification of the whole tree rather than of a link. | the pin is `t-fd-name`'s new section — eight claims, the same on both machines and green on this host first: the link's own mode (`0700` for the `O_RDWR` fixture) and its 64 bytes beside the two directions a pipe's ends give (`0500`/`0300`) and the magic link that names no descriptor (`0777`, size `0` — the control that keeps the refusals on the descriptor's direction rather than on the link), then the descriptor directory's kind, its `0500`, and the property that its size is the count, measured by *opening one more descriptor and seeing the size move by one* so the claim does not depend on how many the runner happens to hold. `t-fd-name` is 49 claims now (was 41; the `docs/uapi/` count had gone stale at 37) and its `ok:` lines are identical on both machines bar the `st_dev` it prints. A host unit test in `src/vfs/procfs.rs` (`an_fd_links_own_mode_is_the_direction_it_was_opened_in`) pins the mapping itself. Gate: x86_64 `pass=180 fail=0 (180 built)`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33; the second machine's arm of the same gate is `pass=179 fail=0` with `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`. The counterfactual is exact: with only the kernel half reverted (three source files) and the pin kept, the sweep is `pass=179 fail=1 (180 built)` with the kernel's own `mpos-kunit` line unmoved — the one red program is `t-fd-name`, and its six red claims are exactly the new ones (the link's mode and size, both pipe directions, the directory's mode, and its moving size), each mode/size claim answered with the kind's default (`got Some(1ff)` is `0o777`) — while the `0777` control and every older claim pass on both kernels. | **low** — one accessor in the scheduler, one pure mapping, two fields on `ProcDir` with a single caller, and no dispatch edge. |

| 15.55 | **Every other procfs/sysfs node reports its own word, and it is the word its own *shape* is worth.** §5.2's second and last named residual of item 24 (the entry §15.54 handed on): both trees are filesystems that keep no modes, so `reported_mode` fell back to the *kind's* default for every node in them — directories `0755`, files `0644`, links `0777` — whatever Linux's creator had said about the node. **Measured whole on a current host first** (`.workbuddy/probe_proc_node_modes.c`, Linux 7.2.9, `lstat` through `syscall(2)`, over every path this kernel's two trees build, plus a walk of both trees tallying mode against kind): a proc directory is **`0555`** — `/proc` itself, `/proc/sys` and every directory under it, `/proc/net`, `/proc/bus/pci/<bus>`, every `/proc/<pid>` and `/proc/<pid>/task` — with the `w` bit absent from all three triads; a generated file nothing can write is **`0444`**; a file that *can* be written is **`0644`** (every sysctl this kernel builds, `/proc/<pid>/oom_score_adj`/`uid_map`/`gid_map`/`timens_offsets`, and the live `/proc/bus/pci/<bus>/<dev>.<func>` window); and the two words *narrower* than their node's shape are `/proc/<pid>/environ` at **`0400`** beside its neighbour `cmdline`'s `0444`, and `/proc/<pid>/ns` at **`0511`** — reachable by name, not listable, which is the door `setns` walks. `/proc/cmdline`'s own `st_size` is **the length of the line it answers** (86 for this host's own, its whole content with the newline, where `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` beside it reports `0`). In `/sys` the tree's root is **`0555`** while every directory under it is the kind's `0755`, and the attributes split by the macro they were made with: `0444` for a block device's `dev`/`size`/`partition`, a PCI function's `vendor`/`device`/`class`/`irq`/`modalias`/`resource`/`power_state` and an input node's `name`; `0644` for a device's `uevent` (the door `udevadm trigger` writes) and a function's `config`. **The fix is the word following the node, and that turns out to be Linux's own rule on both trees**: `proc_create` with no mode means `S_IRUGO`, a sysfs attribute carries its macro's word (`DEVICE_ATTR_RO` against `DEVICE_ATTR_RW`), a directory its creator's — so the *types* answer (`ProcCtl`/`ProcConfig` `0644`, `ProcFile` `0444`, `ProcDir`/`MirrorDir` `0555`, `SysFile` `0444`, `SysConfig`/`SysEventAttr`/`SysPciEvent` `0644`, `SysDir` the kind's own unless it is the root), and the three nodes whose word is their own say so where they are built: `env_file` (both spellings of `environ`), `ProcDir::own(.., 0o511, false)` (`ns`) and `file_of_size` — `/proc/cmdline` is the one generated file whose length is known before it is read, so the size is a *fact* rather than a generator run during a listing. **Three things stay named** (`docs/uapi/` §5.2): the nodes Linux makes writable and this kernel did not (`pid_max`, `threads-max`, `pty/max`, `<pid>/comm`) report the read-only word, because the file refuses the write and the word now says so; the word is a *report* and not a gate, since procfs keeps no modes to enforce — an `0400` node is readable by any caller exactly as its `0444` neighbours are, the mode-less model's own limit and unchanged here; and the three names whose *kind* is a directory here where Linux answers a `0777` link (`/proc/self`, `/proc/thread-self`, `/proc/mounts`) follow the shape they actually have. | the pin is `t-proc-modes` — **18 claims**, green on this host first, with `ok:` lines **byte-identical on both machines** (bar the carriage return the second machine's console adds), because both fixtures are *asked for* rather than named: the first block device under `/sys/class/block` that has a `dev`, and the first PCI bus listing a function (measured: both machines answer `sda`, the AHCI disk the sweep lays for the second machine and attaches to the first). Two host unit tests pin the mapping itself (`a_proc_nodes_own_mode_follows_its_shape`, and sysfs's `a_sysfs_node_reports_its_own_mode_rather_than_the_trees_kind_default`). **The pass' first gate run is part of the measurement, and it turned up a fourth divergence**: `/proc` and `/sys` themselves went red at `0755` — and it was not the trees, because `resolve_no_follow` splits the last name off its parent and looks it up there, so the mount crossing the walking resolver performs on every child never happens for the last component and the no-follow door answers the directory the mount *covers* (the initramfs's `0755` ramfs directory) where Linux answers the mounted root. The program claims those two through the door that *does* cross (`stat`), and the divergence is now its own §5.2 entry with this run as the measurement. Gate: x86_64 `pass=181 fail=0 (181 built)`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33 (181 staged, 11 left out by `tests.toml`); the second machine's arm of the same gate is `pass=180 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 0 (180 staged, 12 left out). The counterfactual is exact: with only the kernel half reverted (two source files) and the pin kept, the sweep is `pass=180 fail=1 (181 built)` with the kernel's own `mpos-kunit` line unmoved at `32 passed, 0 failed` — the one red program is `t-proc-modes`, and its **twelve** red claims are exactly the words this pass changed (`/proc` `0755`, `/proc/sys/kernel` `0755`, `/proc/self/task` `0755`, `/proc/self/ns` `0755`, `/proc/version` `0644`, `/proc/self/status` `0644`, `/proc/self/environ` `0644`, `/proc/self/cmdline` `0644`, `cmdline`'s size `0`, `/sys` `0755`, and the block device's `dev` and `size` at `0644`), while the **six** that pass on both kernels are the shape guards — the words that were already right: the sysctl `0644`, `oom_score_adj` `0644`, the configuration-space window `0644`, `/sys/class` and `/sys/class/block` `0755`, and `uevent` `0644`. | **low** — one word per node type, three builders for the three nodes whose word is their own, one field pair on a directory and one on a file; no new table and no dispatch edge. |

| 15.56 | **A capability is held *in* a namespace, and the two questions Linux asks about that are two different ones.** Item 36's named corner (§5.2's mknod entry, `docs/uapi/` §5.1 item 38): `vfs_mknod` asks `capable(CAP_MKNOD)`, which is the **initial** user namespace, where this kernel asked the caller's own - so a caller that had entered a user namespace, was uid 0 in it and read the whole capability set back was served a device node Linux refuses (§15.53's measurement). The corner was named as the *capability model's* rather than that call's, and this pass closes it where the model can. **Measured whole on a current host first** (`.workbuddy/probe_capable_scope.{c,sh}`, Linux 7.2.9, the *same table* run in four namespaces - unprivileged in the initial one, uid 0 in a user namespace, uid 0 in a user namespace made *with* a network namespace, and root in a user namespace whose `0` is mapped to the outside root): a user-namespace root, `CapEff` `0x1ffffffffff` read back, is **served** `chroot` (`0`) and `unshare(CLONE_NEWNS)` (`0`) and **refused** `mknod(c 1 3)` (`EPERM`), `reboot` with an invalid magic (`EPERM`), `syslog`'s ring size (`EPERM`) and `FS_IOC_SETFLAGS` with `FS_IMMUTABLE_FL` on a file it owns (`EPERM`); the ordinary file beside the device node is served (`0`) and the `dev 0` whiteout too (`0`), so each refusal is the capability and not the namespace's unmapped ids. Three more answers come out of the same table, and they are what makes the *split* visible rather than assumed: `AF_PACKET` is `EPERM` in a **bare** user namespace and **served** in one made with `CLONE_NEWNET` (Linux asks the *network* namespace's owner), `setns` into a mount namespace owned by the initial one is `EPERM` from a credential scoped to a child (Linux asks the namespace *being joined*, before its "already in it" no-op), and a `0600` file owned by the outside user is `EACCES` to a caller whose uid 0 is mapped to the outside root (Linux asks the *file's* filesystem's namespace, `capable_wrt_inode_uidgid`). **The fix is one field and one predicate, and it is deliberately not a namespace pointer.** `Credentials::user_ns_initial` records whether the sets are held in the initial namespace at all - a flag rather than a level or an id, because every door here moves a caller *deeper* (`unshare`, `clone(CLONE_NEWUSER)`, and a `setns` that refuses a caller's own namespace with `EINVAL`), so it can only ever go true→false, and `on_enter_user_ns` clears it beside the grant it already makes; `capable(cap)` is the initial-namespace question, `has_effective(cap)` - whose doc now says which namespace it is asked *of* - stays the caller's own.
