The four sites whose Linux rule is `capable()` switch: both `mknod` doors (`CAP_MKNOD`), `FS_IOC_SETFLAGS`' immutable and append bits (`CAP_LINUX_IMMUTABLE`), `reboot` (`CAP_SYS_BOOT`) and `syslog` (`CAP_SYSLOG`). **Three scopes stay named in §5.2** because this word cannot express them, each with its measurement above: `setns`'s and `AF_PACKET`'s ask the *object's* namespace (a credential carries its own and not the object's), and `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` asks the *file's* filesystem's - where this kernel's permission model (`src/vfs/mod.rs`'s root branch) asks `uid 0` outright, so a caller that maps uid 0 to the outside root reads what Linux refuses it; that is the permission model's own item rather than a syscall gate's. Two smaller members of the family are named with them: `reboot`'s rule is one step finer than `capable()` (`ns_capable(task_active_pid_ns(current)->user_ns, CAP_SYS_BOOT)`, which agrees with the initial namespace for every caller this kernel has) and asks the capability *before* the magic, where this kernel answers `EINVAL` for a bad magic first (the same probe: an invalid magic gets `EPERM`); and `/proc/<pid>/uid_map`'s writer rule is the *parent* namespace's `CAP_SETUID` or the writer being the map's owner (§6.3 carries the self-naming line the owner rule is measured by). | the pins are four programs, one claim each plus a control, all built on one new helper (`mpos_user_tests::in_user_namespace`: a forked child that `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)`s and answers through a pipe, so the parent keeps its own namespace and its buffered report): `t-mknod` (a device node inside a user namespace is `EPERM`, an ordinary file in the same `0777` directory is served - the control that puts the refusal on the capability and not on the namespace's unmapped ids), `t-fsflags` (`IMMUTABLE` is `EPERM` there and `NODUMP` is served, on this process's own `O_RDWR` descriptor inherited across the fork, since the ioctl needs a writable one and the namespace's ids own nothing), `t-reboot-libc` (`CAD_OFF` is `EPERM` there, claimed *before* its root guard so a host run of a non-root user makes it too) and `t-syslog` (the ring's size, and an unknown type *before* its `EINVAL` - the ordering and the scope from the same side). Every one of the seven lines is identical on the two machines (`diff` clean bar the carriage return the second machine's console adds), and each is green on this host first where the program reaches it (`t-fsflags` stops earlier on a host, at its own named gap: the host's tmpfs refuses `IMMUTABLE` outright, so its host run never reaches the section - the probe is the host-side evidence there). The mapping itself is pinned by a new host unit test in `crates/cred` (`the_two_capability_questions_are_the_initial_namespace_and_the_callers_own`): the grant is held in the namespace entered and nothing in the parent, a capability dropped from the *bounding* set is false for both, and the flag moves one way only. Gate: x86_64 `pass=181 fail=0 (181 built)`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33 (181 staged, 11 left out by `tests.toml`); the second machine's arm of the same gate is `pass=180 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed` (180 staged, 12 left out). The counterfactual is exact: with only the kernel half reverted (the leaf crate and the four gates) and the pins kept, the sweep is `pass=177 fail=4 (181 built)` with the kernel's own `mpos-kunit` line unmoved at `32 passed, 0 failed` and exit 33 - the four red programs are exactly the four pins, and their five red claims are the refusals themselves, each served with the namespace's own capability (`FAIL: IMMUTABLE inside a user namespace is EPERM (got Some(0))`, `FAIL: a device node inside a user namespace is EPERM (got Some(0))`, `FAIL: CAD_OFF inside a user namespace is EPERM (got Some(0))`, `FAIL: the ring's size inside a user namespace is EPERM (got Some(16384))` - the size itself, so the ring was read - and `FAIL: and so is an unknown type there, before EINVAL (got Some(-22))`, the type check coming first) - while the two controls (`but an ordinary file in the same directory is served (got Some(0))` and `but a bit needing no capability is served there (got Some(0))`) pass on both kernels, which is what keeps the refusals on the capability rather than on the namespace. | **low** — one field and one predicate in the leaf crate, four call sites turned into the question their Linux rule already named, and one shared helper for the pins; no new table, no dispatch edge, and the namespace bookkeeping is a flag because the doors only ever go one way. |

| 15.57 | **The DAC override is two capabilities asked of the file's namespace, and never "is the caller root".** §5.2's third capability scope (§5.1 item 39), and the one with teeth: `may_as`'s override answered a `chmod 000` file, a `0444` file's write and a `0000` directory's search with **uid 0** plus a "some triad carries the bit" clause, where Linux asks `capable_wrt_inode_uidgid(idmap, inode, cap)` - two capabilities, of the *file's* filesystem's user namespace, and no triad clause at all. Both directions were wrong, and each is somebody's real answer: a caller that had entered a user namespace (uid 0 in it, `CapEff` full) read what Linux refuses it, and root could not read what Linux lets it. **Measured whole on a current host first** (`.workbuddy/probe_capable_scope.sh dac`, Linux 7.2.9, root with each capability dropped from the effective set in turn, against mode `0000`/`0444`/`0644` files and a `1777` directory whose *directory and file both* belong to another user): `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` grants a read **and a write** on a non-directory whatever its bits are - `0` on both rows, `EACCES` on both once it is dropped, and `0` on `open(0000, O_RDWR)` - and grants execute only where `S_IXUGO` (`access(X_OK)` is `EACCES` on a `0644` file even with the whole set and `0` on another user's `0700` one); `CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH` grants a read on its own (with `OVERRIDE` dropped the read still answers, with `READ_SEARCH` dropped the write does too, so each covers the other's kind) and everything on a directory but a write; the compound masks fall out of the execute clause (`access(R_OK|W_OK)` is `0` where `R_OK|W_OK|X_OK` is `EACCES`); with **both** dropped every row is `EACCES`; and the sticky-bit rule is the same family, its last clause `capable_wrt_inode_uidgid(dir, CAP_FOWNER)` (another user's file removed from another user's `1777` directory: `0` with `CAP_FOWNER`, `EPERM` without). **The fix is a caller-shaped identity and a rule that reads that table.** `may_as` takes a `Caller` - the two ids *the door* asked about (`access(2)` the real pair, `AT_EACCESS` the effective one) plus `Overrides`, the three capabilities this family consults - because the ids are the door's and the capabilities are the caller's either way; the triads are asked first and only what they refuse goes to `overrides_allow`, the measured per-kind rule; `may_remove`'s sticky half asks `CAP_FOWNER` rather than uid 0; `current_caller`/`caller_as` are the two doors into the new shape; and `root_override()`, the vestigial `true` whose own doc admitted the predicate lived elsewhere, is deleted with its prose folded into the new types. `Overrides::of` reads the three through `Credentials::capable` - §15.56's initial-namespace question - so the *scope* came free: a caller inside a user namespace holds none of them for a file whose filesystem belongs to the machine. **Two things stay named in §5.2**: the *owner-shaped* calls (`chmod`, `chown`, the `utimes` family) still ask "is the caller root or the owner" where Linux asks `inode_owner_or_capable` (`fsuid == owner \|\| capable_wrt_inode_uidgid(CAP_FOWNER)`), and `chmod`'s set-group-id rule asks `CAP_FSETID` beside the caller's groups where this kernel asks `uid 0` - the same shape with its own capabilities, each its own pass. | the pins are `t-caps`'s new DAC table (the three fixtures, the whole-set rows, each capability dropped, both dropped, the sticky pair, and the namespace pair - all guest-only, because the program's own first claim needs root to lower itself, which a host run is not) and `t-faccessat`'s two `access(2)` rows that separate `R_OK` from `R_OK\|W_OK\|X_OK` (root-guarded, named on a host run like the rest of that file's root claims), plus the host unit test that walks the same table - `the_dac_overrides_are_linuxs_two_capabilities_and_not_a_uid`, which *replaced* the old rule's own test (`root_passes_where_any_bit_of_the_kind_exists`) that had pinned the old rule as a contract. Gate: x86_64 `pass=181 fail=0 (181 built)`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed`, exit 33 (181 staged, 11 left out); the second machine's arm of the same gate is `pass=180 fail=0`, `mpos-kunit 32 passed, 0 failed` (180 staged, 12 left out). The counterfactual is exact: with only the kernel half reverted (the leaf crate's constants, `may_as`, `may_remove` and the three test call sites - five files) and the pins kept, the sweep is `pass=179 fail=2 (181 built)` with the kernel's own `mpos-kunit` line unmoved at `32 passed, 0 failed` - the two red programs are exactly `t-caps` and `t-faccessat`, and their nine red claims are the rows the old rule got wrong: `FAIL: whole set: a 0000 file is readable (got 13)`, `FAIL: whole set: a 0444 file is writable (got 13)`, `FAIL: OVERRIDE dropped: the read still answers, READ_SEARCH's (got 13)`, `FAIL: READ_SEARCH dropped: the read still answers, OVERRIDE's (got 13)`, `FAIL: READ_SEARCH dropped: and the write answers too (got 13)`, `FAIL: with CAP_FOWNER dropped the same removal is refused`, `FAIL: inside a user namespace the other user's 0600 file is refused (got 0)` - **served**, the divergence with teeth - and `t-faccessat`'s `FAIL: access(2) R_OK on 0000 is allowed for root (CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH)` and `FAIL: access(2) R_OK|W_OK on 0000 is allowed for root (CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE)` - while every control passes on both kernels (`access(X_OK)` on the `0644` file, the compound mask, "the whole set removes it", "the file the namespace's own uid owns is served", and the stranger's `EACCES` on root's `0000` file). | **medium** - the rule itself is a page of measured table, but the change is one predicate, one caller-shaped parameter and its construction sites, and a deletion; no new table, no dispatch edge, and the two capabilities were already in `crates/cred`'s vocabulary after §15.56. |

### 15.6 The initramfs flow — **LANDED**, all but the Alpine login pin

The stage's whole point was that the second machine stops being the exception: the kernel unpacks
`initramfs-aarch64/`, hands the machine to the script, and the *script* mounts `/dev/vda`, promotes
it into `/` and `switch_root`s into the disk's own `/init` - the flow `AGENTS.md` §3's kernel-space
discipline and
`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §4 require, and the one the first machine has always run. `check_disk_root`'s
mount half became `steps::mount_disk_root` (called from `init` exactly where x86_64's `init_blk`
calls it, with the wrapper deleted outright), `M7_ROOT_OK` stopped being a kernel marker, and the
`init` seam is one signature (`fn(&BootInfo) -> !`) on both machines.

**What the path cost: six defects, each measured, and only the first was the one §13.20 predicted.**

| # | Defect | The measurement |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `enter_probe` published its save-area address as `sp - 8` in Rust while the prologue stamped `[ret, sp]` at `sp - 16`, so `eret` aimed at the saved *stack* - non-executable - and the first EL0 program to ask to leave died on an instruction permission fault | `EC 0x21`, `IFSC 0b001111`, `ELR_EL1 == FAR_EL1` inside the stack the program was running on |
| 2 | the return stub landed on the caller's `x30` directly, skipping `enter_probe`'s own epilogue - so the caller came back with `sp` 80 bytes *below* where its frame expected it, and every `[sp, #imm]` store in `check_el0` afterwards wrote into its own locals | probe-in/probe-out `sp`, `fp` and `addr_of!(space)` all identical while `word_via_at` read a different value every run: the *memory* had moved, not the pointer |
| 3 | `mpos_enter_frame` reloads all of `x0..x30` from the frame, and the leave path runs no epilogue, so callee-saved registers did not survive an ordinary call | the first instruction after the landing read `[x20, #0x8a8]` with `x20` holding a length, and faulted at `0x906`; the save area now carries `x19..x29` + `q8..q15` |
| 4 | the *test* disk's `/init` was the overlay's `rootfs-aarch64/init` - a `#!/bin/sh` script whose interpreter the disk does not have, because the test disk is the build's own payload and carries no busybox | `switch_root: can't execute '/init': No such file or directory` with every `/t` marker absent; `aarch64_init::exec_launcher_init_elf` is the answer, and it is the shape `run_sweep`'s runner already had |
| 5 | the script read the console from `/dev/console` *after* `mount --move /dev /sysroot/dev` - the wrong device (this kernel's `/dev/console` answers `read` with end of input; the queued line is on **fd 0**) and the wrong moment (`/dev` was gone) | `cat: can't open '/dev/console': No such file or directory`, then `R:` with nothing after it. `read line` from stdin, gated on `mpos.selftest` - `cat` would block forever on the second read |
| 6 | the §15.6 inittab probe never ran, so four of its hand-encoded instructions had never been executed once: `udiv` without bits 15..10 (`0b000010`) is not a divide, `msub` without bit 15 is `madd`, `strb` built on `0x3E000000` is a SIMD store at a register offset, and `cmp` built on `0xB100001F` is `cmn`. Syscall 56 is `openat` on aarch64, not `open` (`nr_aarch64.rs` records `OPEN` as an unreachable 64000), so the path was in `x0` where `dirfd` belongs | an unknown-reason exception mid-print; then `MPOS_INITTAB_OK` absent with the open having answered a negative errno. `aarch64_init`'s tests now pin every one of these words against `llvm-mc -show-encoding`, which is the check the module's header had claimed since it was written |

**The pins, measured 2026-10-07.** `boot-aarch64`: the full chain through `MPOS_SWITCH_ROOT_OK`,
`R:echo-test`, `MPOS_FAULT_OK`/`MPOS_EXEC_OK`/`MPOS_FORK_OK`, `MPOS_INITTAB_OK bytes=1957`,
`MPOS_MUSL_OK` and the whole `MPOS_DEV_*`/`MPOS_FILE_OPS_OK` set, ending `INIT_EXITED`.
`test --arch aarch64`: `pass=27 fail=0`. `cargo xtask test`: `pass=158 fail=0 (158 built)`, exit 33.
`depgraph`: within the ratchet, no edge appeared and no count grew.

**The open pin, now closed.** `alpine-aarch64` boots a real openrc runlevel - clock, local
filesystems, hostname, syslog, crond, `local` all `[ ok ]` - and reaches the prompt:
`Welcome to Alpine Linux 3.25.0_alpha20260805 (edge) / Kernel 0.1.0 on aarch64 (/dev/tty1)` then
`mpos login:`. It cost one line's placement. `steps::arm_console` was inside aarch64's
`if mpos.selftest` arm while x86_64 calls it in `handoff_to_init` on every boot, so a boot without
the test word had **no console mux at all**: the getty busybox init spawns is on `/dev/tty1`, a pty
whose master only the mux reads, so its output had nowhere to go and the log simply stopped after
`Starting local ... [ ok ]`. The arming moved out of the gate; `check_serial_tx` - the check *of* the
armed path - stayed in it. Measured: the same boot now prints the prompt in **16 s**, against the
240 s deadline it used to exhaust.

**And the prompt is not the whole verdict.** A failed openrc service prints `[ !! ]` and the runlevel
carries on, so `login:` appears whether or not every service came up - which is how §13.30's
`mount: mounting proc on /proc failed: Resource busy` sat in a log this command called green. The
command now fails on openrc's own failure marker, and the boot that reaches `mpos login:` carries no
`[ !! ]` line at all.

**The last named difference is closed.** `init_net` + `virtio::spawn_service` sat behind
`mpos.selftest` in `board/aarch64.rs`, read as §13.14's rule ("a service that did not assemble an
interface must not be handed one"). That reading was wrong, and the measurement says so: those two
calls are *bring-up*, not checks, and the first machine has always run both unconditionally
(`init_net` straight out of its PCI enumeration, `spawn_service` at the end of `init_virtio`).
This machine's adapter is assembled before either call regardless of the gate - `check_net` is part
of `devices()`, which `arch::boot` runs on every boot in front of the unpack, and the window it
opens is what `net_device()` picks up - so a boot that reached the gate had already proven a
virtio-net window exists (`check_net` halts the machine otherwise). Gating the bring-up changed
nothing about what the tree carried and only decided whether the stack above it was polled:
measured, the Alpine boot ran with a published adapter and a dead `net` task, and `run --arch
aarch64` - a session boot, so it carries `ALPINE_CMDLINE` and not the test word - never started the
input pump, which is the same task. With the gate removed the Alpine boot prints
`M8_NET_STACK_OK` on the way to `mpos login:` (13 s), `boot-aarch64` keeps its full chain, and the
sweep is `pass=27 fail=0`.

After this, no line in the boot flow is written twice for a reason that is not a bus, a UART or a
page-table format. What each body holds is the shape §"The target shape" item 4 names: the entry's
callbacks, `devices()`, the machine's own device discovery and checks, and the ordering its own
milestones need.


### What stays the machine's, by design

PCI vs device tree; MSI vs GIC line routing; the lazy-FPU trap (no `#NM` on arm64); the two UARTs;
the page-table/IDT proofs (`check_arch`); the console arming order (`steps::arm_console` is *called*
at different points on purpose). None of these is duplication to remove; the phase's rule is the one
`board/mod.rs` already states - a body holds only what would have to be answered differently on a
different machine.

### Known traps for this phase

- **The aarch64 sweep's payload is not the x86_64 harness's machine.** Measured while writing
  §13.20's pin: the x86_64 test `/init` mounts `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, `/tmp`, `devpts` with
  busybox; the aarch64 runner mounts only `/tmp`. A test that needs `/proc` therefore behaves
  differently per machine, and one that does (this phase's shared checks will) must either mount what
  it needs or be honest about the difference. Closing *that* gap is itself part of stage 15.1's
  argument.
- **The marker chain is no longer asserted by a command of its own** (§15.7). The checks still
  print their `M*_OK`/`M*_FAILED` markers on every boot and a failure still halts the machine, so a
  red boot is readable off the wire - but nothing parses the chain any more: what a boot's verdict
  is, on either machine, is the sweep's `=== RUN/PASS/FAIL/SUMMARY`/`END`.
- **A check that mutates shared state damages the machine it checks** (§13.14, §13.15).
- **A device check runs on every aarch64 boot, a session included**, and halts the machine when it
  fails: `check_blk` needs the 1 MiB pattern image beside the disk, `check_fs` needs the root
  partition to carry `/hello`. Both are therefore inputs of *any* aarch64 image, not only a test's
  (`disk::blk_image`, `Arch::kernel_reads_root_probe`). *(Superseded by §15.34: the checks now
  assert only what the kernel produces from the configured devices — no packed fixture, no
  `/hello`; `PROBE_FILE`/`PROBE_BYTES`/`Arch::kernel_reads_root_probe` are gone. What survives of
  this bullet is the first clause: a device check runs on every boot and halts the machine.)*

---

### 15.7 The front end: one path for two machines, and an image that is reused

**Landed 2026-10-08.** `xtask/src/aarch64.rs` is deleted (812 lines), `boot-aarch64` and
`alpine-aarch64` are retired, and every command now handles the machine `--arch` names: `build`,
`initramfs`, `disk`, `rootfs`, `all`, `run`, `test`. The second machine's three entry points had
become copies of the first machine's steps - its own command line, terminator list, marker list,
ESP layout, disk lay-out, staleness rule and `getty` decision - and what was left in that file was
the scaffolding around those steps rather than a second machine's own work. The whole of the
deletion, and what replaced each piece:

| Deleted | Replaced by |
|---|---|
| the two commands | the same commands with `--arch aarch64`, which now really build, install and boot that machine |
| the aarch64 verdict boot (its `CMDLINE`, `DEADLINE`, `TERMINATORS`, `M*` marker list, `R:echo-test` pin) | nothing: the marker chain is what a boot's wire *says*, and the sweep is the verdict on both machines |
| `alpine-aarch64` (staging tree + `fakeroot` + `mkfs.ext4 -d`) | `rootfs --arch aarch64`: one install through the block layer, `apk add --arch aarch64 --no-scripts`, as root, into the mounted partition - which is also what makes ownership right without `fakeroot` |
| `build_alpine_esp`, `run_interactive`, `ModeChoice`, `alpine_cmdline` | `disk`/`rootfs`/`run` parameterised by `Arch`, one mode word for both machines (`mode.cmdline_consoles`) |
| `install_alpine_root`, `ensure_alpine_root`, `alpine_staging`, `alpine_stamp`, `alpine_root_is_stale` | `rootfs::install_base` + the rootfs/`disk` stamps, per machine (`Arch::rootfs_stamp`) |
| `openrc_failure`/`without_ansi` | nothing: they read the retired Alpine verdict's log |
| `Arch::console`, `qemu::vsock_available` | the config's `rootfs.serial_port` (one source for `console=` and the inittab) and `virt_machine_args`' own `/dev/vhost-vsock` check |
| `@SERIAL_GETTY@` and `render_inittab` | nothing, and the measurement is why: see below |
| `disk::boot_media`'s installed-root branch | `disk::sweep_medium`, which is now only a *sweep*'s medium - it lays a complete GPT out of files without root, and it writes its own `build/aarch64-sweep.img` so it can no longer overwrite the image `run` installed |

**The bug the request started from, and its fix.** `dir_newer_than` followed symlinks
(`fs::metadata`), and an installed Alpine tree carries *absolute* ones - `/etc/init.d/functions.sh
-> /usr/libexec/rc/sh/functions.sh`, which dangles on the host that installed it. Measured on the
second `cargo xtask alpine-aarch64` in a row: `Failed to get metadata for
build/aarch64-alpine-root/etc/init.d/functions.sh`, the command exiting non-zero *before* it
booted. Every run before that had hidden it by rebuilding: the fingerprint of the boot's command
line differed between the verdict and the sweep, so the staleness check short-circuited on the
fingerprint and never reached the tree. The walk reads `symlink_metadata` now
(`utils::tests::a_dangling_symlink_in_a_tree_does_not_fail_the_walk`), and the aarch64 image is
reused by the same rule as the first machine's: a stamp per image, a *boot-file refresh* when only
the kernel, loader or initramfs moved, and a package install only when `rootfs.toml` did.

**A divergence the measurement retired.** The template's `@SERIAL_GETTY@` line rendered a getty on
the second machine's serial node whenever the session had no window. Measured with the whole
rendering removed: the windowless boot still reaches `mpos login:` on the wire, and `root` (empty
password) logs in and runs `id` - because the vcmux carries the current virtual console's output
*to* the wire and routes the typed bytes back to it, which is also exactly how the first machine's
`--mode terminal` session has always worked. So the getty was a second login prompt next to the one
busybox init respawns, and the placeholder, the renderer and the per-machine rule are gone; the
inittab is one file both images get verbatim (`rootfs::merge_overlays` no longer renders anything).

**Measured (2026-10-08), after the change:**

| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| `cargo xtask all --arch aarch64` | exit 0: 2 GiB image created, GPT laid, ext4 formatted, **34 packages** installed into the mounted root partition, boot files written, `rootfs: completed` |
| `cargo xtask run --arch aarch64 --mode terminal` | the image is *reused* (no rootfs step at all when nothing moved), boots to `mpos login:`, `root` + `id` answers `uid=0(root)` on the wire |
| … after `touch target/aarch64-unknown-none/release/mpos` | `Updating the image (boot files only)` - no package install - then a boot whose cmdline is `root=/dev/vda2 rootfstype=ext4 rootflags=rw console=ttyAMA0` and whose script prints `MPOS_SWITCH_ROOT_OK: / is /dev/vda2 (ext4)` |
| `cargo xtask test --arch aarch64` | `=== SUMMARY pass=94 fail=10` - **the same ten names** as the pre-refactor baseline (`t-9p, t-brk, t-dsp, t-eventfd, t-inotify, t-mmap, t-mprotect, t-mremap, t-preadv2, t-statfs`), so the extraction cost no regressions; those ten are the in-flight ABI work's. Re-run once more with `build/aarch64-blk.img` deleted first, so the sweep had to lay its own block-check fixture: same summary, same names |
| `cargo xtask test` (first machine) | **stage 1 is red in this tree, and not here**: `cargo test -p mpos --lib` fails on `mm::vma::tests::mmap_placement_rules_over_a_lazy_arena` (`assertion left == right failed: no gap within one page of the cursor`), a test the *uncommitted* kernel work in this tree adds (`git diff src/mm/vma.rs` introduces it), so stage 2 never ran. The parts of the first machine's path this change touches were measured on their own: the full install below, and the boot-file refresh |
| `cargo xtask run --mode terminal` (first machine) | full install (`OK: 549.9 MiB in 164 packages`, `create_user` and the chroot half included), boots, `root` + `id` answers `uid=0(root)` |
| `cargo test -p mpos-xtask --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` | 88 passed |
| `cargo clippy -p mpos-xtask --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu --all-targets` | clean |

**Gaps this change names rather than closes.**

- **The kernel's aarch64 comments still name `cargo xtask boot-aarch64`** (`src/board/aarch64.rs`,
  five sites; `arch/src/arch/aarch64/mod.rs`, one). They belong to the files carrying in-flight
  work in this tree, so they are updated with that work rather than from an unrelated commit; the
  sentences are about *why* a failed check halts the machine, which is unchanged.
- **Nothing asserts the `M*` chain any more.** The checks run and print, and a failure halts the
  machine - so the evidence is on the wire for a person or a log reader - but a check that
  *silently* stopped running would no longer be caught by a gate. The sweep reads the test protocol
  only. Re-pinning the chain means deciding what it should be a verdict *of* on a machine whose
  userland is a distribution (`tests.toml`'s groups are the current answer to "what must run").
- **The aarch64 sweep still stages its payload on a disk** rather than into an initramfs the way the
  first machine's sweep does. That is not decoration: the aarch64 kernel's `check_blk`/`check_fs`
  run on every boot and halt the machine without a pattern image and a partitioned ext4 root, so a
  diskless test boot is a kernel change, not an `xtask` one.

---

### 15.8 The boot is one flow — **LANDED**, with its gaps named

**What was wrong.** `src/board/mod.rs` picked a body by `#[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]`
vs `#[cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")]`, and the two bodies were two boot sequences: the first
enumerated a PCI bus inside `init` and named `virtio-blk`/`ahci`/the share/the card, the second
opened virtio-MMIO windows in the *devices* hook and ran its own halting checks there. The same
device class was reached from two places, and the *order* of the shared steps was the machine's
(console arming before or after the spawn, unpack in the filesystem bring-up or after the
devices). Three trees the kernel mounted were the early userland's on one machine and the
kernel's on the other: `/dev` (built and mounted by `check_dev` on aarch64 only), the host share
(`steps::mount_share`) and the disk (`steps::mount_disk_root`, whose `/mnt` mount the initramfs
script then covered with its own).

**What landed.**

| file | change |
|---|---|
| `src/board/{x86_64,aarch64}.rs` | **deleted** — 719 + 1892 lines. The flow is one copy now, and what was machine evidence is a test build's (below) |
| `src/board/flow.rs` | **new**: the one sequence — console service, device step, then `init_mm`, scheduler, ramfs, archive, the display's task, console arming, the stack over the adapter, the service task, the exit hooks, the seed, and the handoff. Nothing in it names a machine |
| `src/board/mod.rs` | one `start`, no `#[cfg]` at all: `arch::boot(handoff, flow::console_up, flow::devices, flow::init)` |
| `src/drivers/virtio/probe.rs` | **new**: the device step. Each class asks the PCI front-end *and then* the windows the record's device tree describes (`mmio::windows`), and registers what answers through the class's own contract — so which bus carries a device stops being the kernel's question |
| `src/board/suites.rs` | **new**: the two machines' own cases (the x86_64 register-file/page-table pair, the aarch64 PL011 pair) plus the shared `devices` suite — a *test build*'s read-back of what the probe registered |
| `src/board/steps.rs` | `mount_share` and `mount_disk_root` **deleted** (the script mounts both), and the module doc now says what is left: the steps the one flow runs |
| `src/drivers/virtio/{blk,net,mmio,console,fs9p,gpu,input,rng,sound,vsock,hal}.rs` | every `#[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]` in the virtio drivers is gone: the PCI transport compiles wherever the bus does, and the one hardware-bound piece — MSI-X arming — is gated on `mpos_msi` instead |
| `src/drivers/virtio/net.rs`, `video/render.rs` | the adapter and the card each get **one** slot/call site: `net::install`/`net::device` (was a global inside the MMIO module, so a PCI-probed card had nowhere to go), and `render::spawn(Box<dyn GpuDevice>, _)` (was `impl GpuDevice`, which forced one spawn site per transport) |
| `.cargo/config.toml`, `Cargo.toml` | `mpos_virtio_pci`/`mpos_virtio_mmio` deleted — with one flow there is no body to select |
| `xtask/src/selftest.rs` | `TEST_INIT` mounts the host share itself (`/mnt/share`), which is what the kernel used to mount for it |
| `xtask/src/gates.rs` | `BOOT_CODE` follows the files, and the two scratch-tree cases write through the new paths |

**What the kernel mounts now: one tree.** The ramfs the archive is unpacked into — Linux's
rootfs. `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, the real root and the host share are mounted by
`assets/initramfs/core/init` (and by the sweep's own runner on the machine whose sweep stages its
payload on the disk), which is what a distribution's early userland is for and the only way their
owner can unmount them again (§15.6). The aarch64 `check_dev`, which built `/dev` in the kernel
and verified `/dev/urandom` and `/dev/hwrng` through it, is gone with it — the node's own pin is
the payload's (`t-devtmpfs`, and the `/dev` node tests).

**Where the machines' device evidence went.** The aarch64 checks that *were* the boot's verdict
(`check_rng`, `check_blk`, `check_fs`, `check_net`, `check_input`, `check_console`, `check_sound`,
`check_vsock`, `check_p9`, `check_gpu`, `check_dev` and the five `/dev` read-backs) are gone. The
bring-up half is the probe's, and the verification half is a *test build*'s: `devices.*` in
`src/board/suites.rs` asks each subsystem what it holds (the entropy source, the block layer's
disk, the adapter slot, the evdev nodes, the console port, the sound card, the 9p registry's tag,
and the display whose render task is running) on **both** machines. That is §15.19's rule applied
to devices: a case answers rather than halts, and a distribution boot neither pays for it nor can
be stopped by it (§13.14).

**Measured.** `cargo xtask test` → `=== SUMMARY pass=161 fail=0 (161 built)`, `mpos-kunit 29
passed, 0 failed`, exit 33. `cargo xtask test --arch aarch64` → `=== SUMMARY pass=162 fail=0`,
`mpos-kunit 29 passed, 0 failed`. Host unit tests 522; `mpos-xtask` 100; `cargo xtask depgraph`
within the ratchet, mutual pairs 8/8; `cargo fmt --check` clean; warning counts unchanged
(x86_64 bin 1, aarch64 lib 11).

**One trap the aarch64 gate found, and it is why both gates are the evidence.** The first run of
the merged flow hung the second machine right after the handoff, with no script output at all:
`assets/initramfs/core/init` reads one console line and echoes it back when the boot carries
`mpos.selftest`, and the kernel used to *queue* that line — in the aarch64 body I deleted, and
nowhere else. The x86_64 sweep cannot see this: its `/init` is the test runner, not the script.
The line is back, in the flow, a test build's only, with the same reasoning as before (a real
handoff must never inject one, because a distribution's getty would read it as a username). The
second thing the two-machine pair caught is cheaper but worth recording: a *stale* `qemu-system-aarch64`
from an interrupted run holds the guest CID, so the next boot dies at once with
`vhost-vsock: unable to set guest cid` — a machine that never started, read as a machine whose
boot broke (§13.28's trap, met twice in one session).

**Named gaps.** (1) The `devices` suite has no vsock case: QEMU only offers the device when the
host has `/dev/vhost-vsock`, so a case that must pass could only pass by not being asked; the
probe registers it when the host offers one, and `AF_VSOCK`'s own programs are the evidence where
it exists. (2) A class whose program is not staged on the second machine is now pinned there by
the probe's registration plus the shared read-back rather than by a halting boot check -
`t-ahci`/`t-evdev` are still `no_std` `abitest` programs built for the first machine only, so
"A SATA disk came up" and "an evdev node exists" are read-backs there, not data-path round trips.
(3) The kernel no longer mounts the ext4 root or verifies it at boot: the filesystem's programs
are the evidence, and a distribution mounts its own root. (4) `cargo xtask run`/`rootfs` (the
Alpine boot of either machine) was **not** re-run for this cut - the two sweeps and their kernel
cases are what was measured; the Alpine path is the same script doing the same mounts it already
did, with the kernel's three redundant ones removed.

### 15.9 The kernel travels inside the loader — **LANDED**

The boot volume carries one bootable file, not a stub beside a kernel. `boot/build.rs` compresses
the kernel this build is for (DEFLATE, `miniz_oxide` level 9) into the loader's own image, and
`boot/src/lib.rs` inflates it from the `include_bytes!` of that payload before it opens the volume.
The ESP's root now holds `EFI/BOOT/BOOT<ARCH>.EFI`, `initramfs.tar` and `mpos.cmdline`, and no
`mpos.elf`. This is Linux's `bzImage` shape, with the parts named: the stub is still a stub, the
kernel is still not a firmware application, and every step of the load path but the first is
unchanged.

**Why a build script and not a path.** The shorter spelling - `include_bytes!` of a fixed path - is
wrong for the one reason this crate has to be careful: the kernel is the input that *moves*. Cargo
rebuilds a crate when something it declared moved, and a path written in the source declares
nothing, so the loader would go on carrying the previous kernel with no word said. `build.rs`
declares the two dependencies that are real - `MPOS_KERNEL_ELF` (which `xtask` always sets, to the
kernel it has just built, so a self-test boot and an ordinary boot each carry their own) and the
file's own bytes (`cargo:rerun-if-changed`). The same crate appears in `[dependencies]` *and*
`[build-dependencies]` on purpose: a compressor from one project and a decompressor from another is
a pair that drifts, and what it drifts into is a boot that dies on a stream it cannot read.

**Why the other two stay files.** They are data the kernel is handed and unpacks itself, not
programs the loader has to place in memory and jump to. Carrying them would mean relinking the
loader into a larger image every time a file in the early userspace changed - and there is a
distribution's worth of them. Linux draws the line in the same place.

**Measured** (the two machines, 2026-10-10):

| | compressed | loaded | loader |
|---|---|---|---|
| x86_64 | 809 KiB | 2396 KiB (33%) | 64 KB -> 912 KB |
| aarch64 | 834 KiB | 2288 KiB (36%) | 66 KB -> 937 KB |

Both serial logs carry the new line - `mpos-boot: kernel image: 809 KiB compressed, 2396 KiB
loaded` on the first, `834 ... 2288` on the second - and `build/<arch>-sweep-esp` holds three
files. The bytes the volume carries *fall* (64 KB loader + 2.4 MB kernel becomes one 912 KB file),
so the compression pays for itself in the one place it was not needed.

**What it deliberately does not do.** `run()` still loads an image and jumps to it: `elf.rs` (379)
and `vm.rs` (256) are untouched and the kernel is still not a PE. This is therefore **not** step 1
of `TEST-AND-BOOT-REFACTOR.md` §5 - which now records it as §5.8 - but takes §5's *visible* half
(one artifact, no `mpos.elf`) without any of its costs. The three costs §5.7 named - one allocator
serving two firmware phases, the `x18` clash that makes `none` and `uefi` unable to be correct at
the same time, and the loss of the `-kernel` bare-metal path - all remain unpaid, because none of
them follows from the kernel being *carried*; they follow from the kernel being *started by the
firmware*.

**One guard got smaller, and the comments now say what it guards.** `HANDOFF_MAGIC`'s reason used
to be "an older `mpos.elf` left on a partition". The kernel is inside the loader now, so the record's
two ends are always built together and cannot disagree by accident; the check stays - it is one
comparison, and a boot volume assembled or half-updated by hand is still a thing to refuse - and
`src/boot/handoff.rs`, `docs/modules/boot.md` and `docs/modules/loader.md` were reworded rather than
left naming a file that no longer exists.

**The first version of this was wrong, and the way it was wrong is worth keeping.** The build
script watches the file `xtask` hands it and decides freshness the way a build script must - from
that file's modification time. The trouble is that cargo links an already-compiled artifact into
place *keeping the artifact's own compile time*, while the two kernels this front end builds (the
ordinary one and the self-test one) land at the **same** path in the target directory. That path is
therefore one whose mtime can stand still while its bytes change - and the case where they change is
exactly the alternation between the two. The first gate after this landed showed what that
produces: the second machine booted, printed every line up to `mpos: /init is up`, and then stopped
for **fourteen minutes** with nothing on the wire. What had booted was the *ordinary* kernel carried
into a self-test boot, and the early userspace was waiting for ever on a console line only a
self-test kernel queues. Three things came out of it.

* **The fix is at the build, not at the boot.** The loader is handed a path its *caller* owns -
  `build_kernel` the target directory's `mpos`, `build_test_kernel` its own copy under `build/` - so
  the two values differ, and switching between them reruns the build script before the file's own
  change is even considered. The copy is *mirrored* rather than copied (the bytes are compared
  first), so an unchanged kernel does not relink the loader and recompress it on every test run.
* **The runtime guard that "should" have made it loud is refused by this repository's own gate, and
  the refusal is right.** Reading a test word off the command line in the boot's own code is
  forbidden (§15.19 - whether a kernel carries tests is the *build's* to say, not a word's and not a
  run-time check's). The attempt was made, a host gate named it by file and line, and the guard was
  removed. It is also why `boot::cmdline_has` has no caller at all: it is the one spelling of a
  question the boot is not allowed to ask.
* **A host test now pins the cheap half**: the two kernels must reach the loader as two *different*
  paths (`xtask/src/utils.rs::the_loader_is_handed_a_path_each_kernel_build_owns`). The property
  itself - an mtime that does not move forward - cannot be unit-tested; naming the invariant can, and
  it turns a fourteen-minute hang into a one-second failure.

The build script also reports *which* file it embedded and by which route (`MPOS_KERNEL_ELF` or
derived from `TARGET`), because "a loader carrying the wrong kernel" is a failure whose symptom
names everything except its cause.

**Gates, after the fix.** aarch64 `165 staged, pass=165 fail=0` (the 165th is `t-virtgpu`); x86_64
`161 staged, pass=161 fail=0`; host unit tests 102; the kernel's 29. In the first version of this
section those numbers were `164`/`161` with host tests `101`; `xtask/src/fat.rs`'s 8.3 example was
`mpos.elf` and is now a name that stands for the class, because nothing the ESP carries fits in
8.3 any more.

### 15.12 The card is handed back when the process ends, not only when it closes — **LANDED**

Moving `t-drm` to the std side (§15.11, batch 45) put five `drm*` programs in **one boot** where
before they had been split across two images, and the first gate that ran them found three of them
red for a reason that was nobody's assertion: `t-drm`'s own three connector-property claims, and
then - the tell - `t-drm-args`' `MODE_ATOMIC ... is EFAULT` answering `EACCES`, and `t-drm-lease`'s
"a non-master cannot create a lease" answering success. That is not a card being wrong; it is a card
that still believes somebody else is its master.

**The mechanism.** `drivers::drm::master_closed` - the function that gives back a description's
master, its console binding, its client-capability set, its authorisations, its sync objects and its
framebuffers - is called from **one** place: the `close(2)` syscall's hook in `file.rs`. A process
that ends without closing its card never runs it, and *every* test ends that way: `Report::finish`
is `std::process::exit`, which is `exit_group`, which unwinds nothing - the `OwnedFd`s on `main`'s
stack are never dropped and `close` is never called. So `t-drm`, which sets `DRM_CLIENT_CAP_ATOMIC`
and takes the master, left both behind. The second half is why it poisoned a *different* program: an
`ofd` is the open file description's address (`file.ofd()` is `Arc::as_ptr`), so once the description
is freed the allocator hands the **same address** to the next one - and `t-drm-props` opens a fresh,
deliberately *uncapped* description and asserts it is not shown the atomic properties. It was.

**The fix is the missing registration, not a test-local cleanup.** `master_closed`'s own header
already promises the property ("a compositor that is killed - or a test that simply closes the card -
must not leave the machine holding a screen nobody can drive"); the implementation just delivered it
on the syscall path and not on the exit path, and `sched::exit` exists for exactly this shape
(`src/sched/exit.rs`: a subsystem registers a hook and the teardown walks the list without knowing
what is on it, so `sched` never names `drivers`). `drivers::drm::close_at_exit` is that hook; it uses
`vfs::file::for_each_last`, the same "is this the final reference" rule the close hook applies by
hand, so a card a `fork` still holds is left alone. It is registered in
`board::steps::register_exit_hooks` beside locks, the socket and the inotify watch - the three that
were already every machine's - in registration order, which is the order the resources have to go.
`t-drm` additionally hands the card back itself (`DROP_MASTER`) before it returns, which is the
explicit form of the same courtesy and the one claim the move added; with the hook in place the two
agree.

**The other two findings are the migration's own and are recorded in the §15.11 row** (batch 45):
two records the no-std original handed the kernel four bytes short of what the kernel's `arg_shape`
copies - `drm_mode_obj_get_properties` (`sizeof` 32, passed 28) and one `ADDFB2` (`sizeof` 104,
passed 100) - and a two-element property array that the kernel's all-or-nothing copy rule
(`want >= count`) had been writing through, which the std side's correctly sized array stopped.

**Gates.** aarch64 `168 staged, pass=168 fail=0`; x86_64 `161 staged, pass=161 fail=0`; host unit
tests 522; the kernel's 29. `t-drm` is 122 claims, per claim identical on both machines, and it is
the second machine's first run of the family.


### 15.13 `ADDFB2` read its modifier four bytes early — **LANDED**

`struct drm_mode_fb_cmd2` is `sizeof` 104, and its `modifier[4]` begins at **72**: `handles[4]` is
20..36, `pitches[4]` 36..52, `offsets[4]` 52..68, and 68..72 is the padding the `__u64` forces.
Measured, not counted — a `cc` probe against the installed uapi headers answers `sizeof` 104 and
`offsetof(drm_mode_fb_cmd2, modifier)` 72 (`.workbuddy/drmpropsize.c`, the same shape as
`.workbuddy/leasesize.c` and `.workbuddy/drmsize.c`).

`add_fb2` read the modifier at 68 and `get_fb2` wrote it back at 68. Both are fixed.

**Why nobody saw it.** The only modifier any caller ever named was `DRM_FORMAT_MOD_LINEAR`, which
is 0. Reading the padding instead of the field answers 0 as well, so `LINEAR` was accepted for the
right reason by the wrong bytes, and `GETFB2` reported a modifier a caller would find at 72 - where
it was zero too. The no-std `t-drm-props` could not have caught it for a worse reason: it spelled
its offsets by hand, in the same wrong place, so the test and the kernel agreed with each other and
with nothing else. That is the pair this migration separates — the std side states the uapi's
number as a compile-time assert, and the kernel was moved to answer it.

**How it is pinned.** `t-drm-props` names a modifier this plane does not list and expects `EINVAL`.
The constant matters as much as the offset: `DRM_FORMAT_MOD_BOGUS` is `0x0100_0000_0000_0000` -
vendor 0x01's namespace with the value 0, a real-shaped `drm_fourcc.h` vendor modifier whose **low
32 bits are zero**. A four-bytes-early read sees the padding plus the value's low half, which is 0,
so it takes a modifier it cannot scan out and calls it `LINEAR`. The original constant ended in
`...0001`, whose low half is nonzero, so the refusal came out the same at either offset and the bug
stayed invisible — the constant had to change, not just the offset.

**Both directions are measured**, on the first machine:

* kernel at 68, test at 72 — `FAIL: ADDFB2 refuses a modifier the plane does not list (got 0, want
  -22)`. The bogus modifier was accepted.
* kernel at 72, test at 72 — `pass=161 fail=0`.

`t-drm` (batch 45) had been green across the whole bug, and still is: its `ADDFB2` carries `LINEAR`
and its `GETFB2` read-back asserts zeros, and both answers are 0 at 68 and at 72. A green test is
not evidence about a field nothing has ever written a nonzero value into.

**Gates.** aarch64 `169 staged, pass=169 fail=0`; x86_64 `161 staged, pass=161 fail=0`; host unit
tests 522; the kernel's 29. `t-drm-props` is 98 claims, per claim identical on both machines, and
it is the second machine's first run of the family.


### R.15 (moved from ROADMAP's status board) — the card at exit, the ADDFB2 offset, and what the 3D work still has not measured

**A process hands the card back when it ends, not only when it closes (§15.12).** The DRM master, its console binding, the client-capability set, the authorisations, the sync objects and the framebuffers were released by the `close(2)` hook alone, and `do_exit` never runs it: `Report::finish` is `std::process::exit`, which is `exit_group`, which unwinds nothing, so a description that ended without closing its card left its DRM state behind. Because an `ofd` is the description's *address*, the next description the allocator placed there inherited a claim it never made. Found when five `drm*` programs first shared one boot — `t-drm` broke `t-drm-args`, `t-drm-lease`, then `t-drm-props` — and fixed by registering `drivers::drm::close_at_exit` with `sched::exit` beside locks, the socket and the inotify watch, so the exit path delivers the property `master_closed`'s header already promised.

**`ADDFB2` reads its format modifier at the offset the uapi gives it (§15.13).** The field is at 72, not 68: `offsets[4]` ends at 68 and 68..72 is the padding the `__u64` forces, measured against the installed headers. `add_fb2` read at 68 and `get_fb2` wrote at 68, and the no-std test it was written against spelled its offsets by hand in the same wrong place, so the two agreed with each other and with nothing else. Invisible while the only modifier anyone named was `LINEAR`, which is 0, because the padding reads as 0 too. Pinned by naming a vendor modifier whose low 32 bits are zero (`0x0100_0000_0000_0000`), so the offset — not the value — decides the answer: with the kernel back at 68 the claim fails `got 0, want -22`.

**What the 3D/virtgpu landing has *not* measured.** The family is served now, 2D and 3D both: all eleven requests are dispatched, `RESOURCE_CREATE` makes a real device resource across the new mailbox to the render task, the GL card negotiates `VIRGL` and the driver says so in its own words — `virtio-gpu: virgl=1 context_init=1 capsets=2 cached=2 mask=0x6`, every number the deleted `user/staging/virtgpu-probe/` measured on Linux — and `t-virtgpu` pins the lot, its 3D branch included, on the GL machine (`[qemu] gpu` names the card). Both forks §7.4 argued are settled and landed — the GPU driver is this kernel's rather than the crate's, and a submission reaches the device through the render task's mailbox, which the resource path already uses. What blocked `is_software()` was not the 3D queue but the card's identity, and that is built now: measured against a working card's own sysfs reads, the node's `device` link had to lead to the display-class PCI function — carrying `vendor`, `device`, `subsystem_vendor`, `subsystem_device`, `uevent` and the `subsystem` link libdrm resolves first — rather than to a `virtual` directory with no attributes at all. **The renderer itself is still unverified**: the GL-card run that would answer "eglinfo reports a non-software device" has not happened, because the console defect §7.4's update names killed every attempt at it. That is the first thing on this entry, and it is a measurement rather than a build.
